About The Stat Line

The Stat Line is an independent sports and culture desk. One stop for sports, gaming, collectibles and fashion — built as an autoblog: stories are spotted as they happen, verified, and published automatically. No waiting on a news cycle.

Every story on this site is verified real before it goes up. Stats get re-checked against the box score, historical claims get the full year-by-year treatment, and news gets corroborated — false alarms get killed quietly. You’ll never see them.

NFL · Nov 8, 2026 · 6:07 AM ET

Brady calls Packers-Patriots from Gillette booth

By Matt

Tom Brady returns to Gillette Stadium today to call Packers-Patriots from the FOX booth at 4:25 p.m. ET — his first Patriots game as a broadcaster, with both teams at 5-3 and Drake Maye facing Jordan Love in the late-afternoon national window.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Tom Brady returns to Gillette Stadium today to call the Packers-Patriots game from the FOX booth at 4:25 p.m. ET. Both teams arrive 5-3, with Drake Maye and Jordan Love facing off in the late-afternoon national window.

Game info

Matchup: Green Bay Packers at New England Patriots

Venue: Gillette Stadium, Foxborough

Kickoff: Today, 4:25 p.m. ET

TV: FOX — "America's Game of the Week" (also streaming on FOX One)

Booth: Kevin Burkhardt (play-by-play), Tom Brady (analyst), Erin Andrews and Tom Rinaldi (sidelines)

Brady's first Patriots game in the booth

This is Brady's first time calling a Patriots game as a television analyst since he joined FOX's top NFL booth — and his first game at Gillette Stadium in the booth. FOX has spent the week promoting the return with a 76-second teaser built around Brady's best moments from his 20 seasons in New England.

Brady admitted the day will hit different. "It'll be certainly emotional," he told Colin Cowherd this week.

The game itself

The football is worth the window on its own. Both teams sit at 5-3, and the matchup is a showcase for two of the game's top young quarterbacks: Jordan Love's Packers against Drake Maye's Patriots. New England is coming off a 24-19 win over Miami, and the Patriots enter as three-point favorites.

It is a full Foxborough weekend, too. Rob Gronkowski is inducted into the Patriots Hall of Fame, with the ceremony taking place at halftime.

Why this one matters

Brady won six Super Bowl rings in New England. Today he watches the franchise he defined from the booth above the field where it happened — calling a game between two 5-3 teams, with the next generation of quarterbacks on the field below him.

NBA · Nov 7, 2026 · 5:08 PM ET

Celtics set NBA Cup record with 24 threes

By Matt

Jayson Tatum dropped 34 points in 29 minutes and Boston buried an NBA Cup-record 24 threes in a 119-80 rout of the Hornets — a wire-to-wire blowout that pushed the Celtics to 7-2 and 2-0 in Cup play.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

The Boston Celtics routed the Charlotte Hornets 119-80 on Friday night at Spectrum Center, and the score barely tells the story. Boston led 40-20 after the first quarter, by 31 at halftime, and by as many as 46 points on the way to its second NBA Cup group-play win.

Jayson Tatum scored a game-high 34 points in just 29 minutes — he did not play a minute in the fourth quarter — and the Celtics made 24 three-pointers on 54.5% shooting from deep. That is a new NBA Cup record.

The win moved Boston to 7-2 on the season and 2-0 in East Group C, where the Celtics now lead the group by record and own a +53 scoring differential — 44 points clear of the next team.

The three-point blitz

The record was not a late-game accident. Boston hit 17 threes in the first half alone, tying a franchise record, and kept firing until the 24th fell. The Celtics finished 24-of-44 from deep (54.5%) with 33 assists as a team.

They were even flirting with bigger history. The NBA record for most threes in a game is 29 — a mark the Celtics themselves tied on Opening Night in 2024 against the Knicks. They settled for the Cup record instead, and with the game long decided, there was no reason to chase it.

The contrast on the other end was stark. Charlotte shot 11-of-50 from three (22%) and 27-of-92 from the field overall (29.3%) in its second-lowest scoring performance of the season. The loss was the Hornets' sixth straight, dropping them to 3-7.

Tatum's night

Tatum did most of his damage before halftime: 24 of his 34 points came in the first two quarters, including 18 in the first. He finished 9-of-16 from the field, 5-of-8 from three, and a perfect 11-of-11 from the free-throw line, with 8 rebounds, 9 assists, a steal, and a team-best plus-36.

The road crowd in Charlotte even gave him MVP chants — fitting for a player who averages nearly 30 points per game against the Hornets, the most he averages against any of the 29 other teams.

It is also worth remembering how recently this version of Tatum was in doubt. He returned from a torn Achilles tendon on March 6 of last season, and nights like this one — efficient, unhurried, done by the fourth quarter — are the clearest sign yet that the explosion and the shot-making are fully back.

He had help. Derrick White added 18 points, including 10 in the first quarter, with 4 threes, 5 assists, and 4 rebounds. Payton Pritchard came off the bench for 12 points and 4 more threes. Tatum was the only player on either side to reach 20 points.

The box score

Final score: Celtics 119, Hornets 80

Celtics threes: 24-44 (54.5%) — NBA Cup record

Hornets threes: 11-50 (22.0%)

Tatum: 34 PTS, 8 REB, 9 AST, 9-16 FG, 5-8 3PT, 11-11 FT, +36

White: 18 PTS, 4 REB, 5 AST, 4-6 3PT

Pritchard: 12 PTS, 4-7 3PT

Team assists: 33

Celtics record: 7-2 (2-0 in NBA Cup group play)

Hornets record: 3-7 (sixth straight loss)

Around the game

• Brandon Miller: Charlotte's night got worse when Miller left the game with a groin injury.

• First stop on the road: the game opened a four-game road trip for Boston.

• Cup math: point differential matters in NBA Cup group play, and Boston's +53 through two games is the best in East Group C by 44 points.

• Next Cup game: Boston's next group-play game is not until Tuesday, Nov. 24, against the Wizards.

What it means

This was a statement in the exact form the NBA Cup rewards: win big, and the tiebreaker takes care of itself. The Celtics did not just beat an injury-riddled, struggling Hornets team — they buried them from three, sat their star for the fourth quarter, and still walked out with the best point differential in their group.

The bigger signal is Tatum. A 34-point night in 29 minutes, with the efficiency and the bounce, against a team he has always torched, says the Celtics' best player is rounding into form at the same time the team is stacking wins. Boston's next test is a real one: at Milwaukee on Sunday night.

What's next

Boston visits the Milwaukee Bucks on Sunday, Nov. 8, at 8 p.m. at Fiserv Forum.

MLB · Nov 7, 2026 · 2:08 PM ET

Red Sox predicted to sign Freddy Peralta for $92M

By Matt

Sports Illustrated's Nicholas Selbe predicts Boston lands Freddy Peralta on a four-year, $92 million deal — a bet on the ace's Tampa turnaround after Sonny Gray elected free agency.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The prediction

The Red Sox need a starter, and one of the offseason's biggest names is being linked to Boston. Sports Illustrated's Nicholas Selbe predicted the Red Sox will sign Freddy Peralta to a four-year, $92 million contract this offseason.

The fit is obvious. Sonny Gray has elected free agency, leaving a hole at the top of Boston's rotation. Peralta is a two-time All-Star and one of the top arms on the open market — and after a wild 2026, his value is trending back up at exactly the right time.

The Jekyll-and-Hyde season

Peralta's 2026 was a tale of two teams. He opened the year with the New York Mets, where it went sideways: a 5-9 record and a 4.99 ERA in 22 appearances. A deadline trade to Tampa Bay changed everything.

With the Rays, Peralta went 5-2 with a 3.25 ERA in 10 appearances, allowing just three earned runs over his final six regular-season starts — a 0.79 ERA down the stretch. Overall for the season, he finished 10-11 with a 4.42 ERA and 156 strikeouts in 32 games.

Then came October. Peralta was named ALCS MVP after posting a 0.75 ERA over three appearances (12 innings) against Cleveland, helping the Rays reach the World Series against his old club, Milwaukee.

New York Mets: 5-9; 4.99; —

Tampa Bay Rays: 5-2; 3.25; —

Full season: 10-11; 4.42; 156

Why Boston believes in the Tampa version

The Rays version of Peralta is the one Boston would be paying for — and it's not hard to see why. A year earlier, in his final season with the Brewers, Peralta went 17-6 with a 2.70 ERA and 204 strikeouts, finishing fifth in NL Cy Young voting. That is the track record underneath the messy Mets half-season: a durable, strikeout-heavy starter with 30 starts in four consecutive years.

The question for Boston is which version shows up in 2027. The case for the Red Sox: the Tampa turnaround wasn't a small sample blip. It was a full second-half reset — a 3.25 ERA, a dominant closing stretch, and an ALCS MVP — from a 30-year-old entering his age-31 season who has done this before.

The competition

Boston won't be alone in the bidding. MLB Trade Rumors' Steve Adams predicted Peralta lands with the Chicago Cubs on a five-year, $110 million deal, and the Blue Jays, Giants, Padres, White Sox, and Orioles have all been named as fits. Spotrac has floated a seven-year, $201 million projection, while The Athletic's Jim Bowden predicted five years and $140 million.

Selbe's $92 million over four years would be the shorter, cheaper end of that range — a bet that Boston can land the ace without going to the nine-figure lengths other suitors might. Whether Peralta takes a shorter deal or chases the longer one is the open question of his winter.

What it would mean for Boston

If the prediction holds, the Red Sox would be buying a proven top-of-the-rotation arm to replace Gray — and keeping him away from the rest of the AL East in the process. The Rays aren't expected to re-sign him at this price, and every team in the division knows what the Tampa version of Peralta looks like. Boston landing him would be a statement that the rotation hole gets filled with a real ace, not a stopgap.

TSL Bets · Nov 7, 2026 · 7:56 AM ET

2-Leg Parlay (about +257): Spurs -5.5 + Canadiens Moneyline

By Matt

Two plays, one story: the league's hottest big man against a shorthanded backcourt, and the East's hottest team at a coin-flip price. Together they pay about +257.

The legs

Leg 1 — San Antonio Spurs -5.5 (-110)

San Antonio Spurs at Portland Trail Blazers, 10:00 PM ET. Portland is down Ja Morant, Shaedon Sharpe, and Vit Krejci on the second night of a back-to-back, and Victor Wembanyama has scored 82 points in his last two games — 52 against Dallas on Wednesday, 30 against Sacramento on Friday. The Blazers just spent Friday night chasing the Lakers around Los Angeles. The Spurs are the better, healthier team, and the line has already moved from -4.5 to -5.5.

Leg 2 — Montreal Canadiens moneyline (-115)

Montreal Canadiens at Toronto Maple Leafs, 7:00 PM ET. The Canadiens are 10-3-3, have won six of seven and four straight over Toronto, and start Jacob Fowler (7-1-1, .925 save percentage). The Leafs have lost three of four and are held below 30 shots a night lately. Tavares is expected back for Toronto, but one player returning from three weeks out doesn't close the gap at a coin-flip price.

Why these two together

Both legs are bets on the better, healthier team — and both are priced like coin flips. The Spurs need to beat a shorthanded team by six. The Canadiens need to beat a slumping team straight up. Neither asks for a miracle. Put them together and the price jumps to about +257 — a $10 ticket returns about $35.70 — which is the whole point: two solid reads, one ticket.

The honest caveat

Parlays multiply risk, not just payout. The Spurs are laying points on the road on a back-to-back, and Wembanyama rolled his ankle Friday — he finished the game, but if he is limited, leg one is in trouble. The Canadiens are the better team, but the Leafs are rested, at home, on a Saturday night, with their captain back. Either leg can lose on its own. That is why the price is +257 and not -257. Bet it as a fun ticket on two good reads, not as a mortgage payment.

The pick

2-leg parlay, about +257: San Antonio Spurs -5.5 (-110) + Montreal Canadiens moneyline (-115). The hottest big man in the league and the hottest team in the East, both at fair prices. That is the bet.

TSL Bets · Nov 7, 2026 · 7:56 AM ET

Canadiens Moneyline (-115): The East's hottest team against a slumping rival — even with Tavares back

By Matt

Montreal is 10-3-3, has won six of seven and four straight over Toronto, and starts a goalie at 7-1-1 with a .925 save percentage. The Leafs get Tavares back, but -115 is still the value side.

Game slip

Montreal Canadiens (10-3-3) at Toronto Maple Leafs (8-7-2)

Saturday, November 7, 2026 — 7:00 PM ET — Scotiabank Arena, Toronto, ON — ESPN+, SN, TVAS

Total: 6.5

Moneyline: Canadiens -115 / Maple Leafs +105

Odds source: ESPN via DraftKings, Nov. 7

### Injury report

Montreal: RW Ivan Demidov (ankle) — injured reserve. D Mike Matheson (lower body) — out. G Samuel Montembeault (lower body) — injured reserve, day-to-day, could return after a month out. D Arber Xhekaj (undisclosed) — questionable. Expected starter Jacob Fowler is 7-1-1 with a .925 save percentage and a 2.17 goals-against average.

Toronto: C John Tavares (lower body) — expected to return after missing nine games since October 15. He practiced Friday in a regular jersey, skated on a line with Nick Paul and William Nylander, and rejoined the top power-play unit. RW Kirill Marchenko (lower body) — injured reserve. C Max Domi (back) — injured reserve. F Miles Wood — injured reserve. D Nick Blankenburg — injured reserve. G Elvis Merzlikins (shoulder) — injured reserve.

### Storylines

• Montreal is the hottest team in the East right now: 10-3-3, winners of six of seven, coming off a 5-4 overtime win over Utah on Thursday in which Nick Suzuki returned from a six-game absence with three points and Cole Caufield scored the winner 30 seconds into OT.

• The Canadiens carry a four-game win streak over the Maple Leafs into Scotiabank Arena, and they just showed they can win the hard way — rallying from 2-0 and 4-3 down on Thursday, two nights after blowing a 4-0 lead to Winnipeg.

• Toronto is slumping: 8-7-2, losers of three of four, held below 30 shots in four straight games, and 4-4-1 at home. The middle six has buckled with Tavares and Marchenko out, and the Leafs are starting a stretch of five straight games against teams in a playoff position.

• The goalie matchup tilts Montreal's way. Fowler is 7-1-1 with a .925 save percentage; one starting-goalie service projects Sergei Bobrovsky for Toronto at .889 with a 3.27 goals-against average. That is a big gap in a game lined as a coin flip.

The bet

Montreal Canadiens moneyline (-115)

This is a bet on the better team at a coin-flip price. Montreal is better at even strength, better in goal, deeper down the lineup, and has owned this matchup lately — four straight wins over Toronto. The Leafs' best argument is Tavares returning and three days of rest, and both are real. But Tavares is one player coming back from a three-week absence, likely on the wing for the first time, and rest doesn't fix a team that has been outshot and outworked for a week. At -115, you're getting the 10-3-3 team at the price of a toss-up. Take it.

Why

The market is pricing the name on the front of the jersey, not the team on the ice. Toronto at home, on Hockey Night in Canada, against Montreal — that matchup always draws Leafs money, and it has kept this line near even despite everything in the form guide pointing the other way. Montreal has the better record by a distance, the better goalie by a distance, the head-to-head streak, and the momentum of a team that just stared down a brutal loss and answered with a comeback win. The Habs' blue line is banged up, but Fowler has covered for it all season.

The honest caveat

Tavares matters. He had seven points in seven games before the injury, he is a point-per-game player for his career, and his return immediately fixes Toronto's top power play and its faceoff circle. The Leafs have also had three days off since their 3-2 loss to Utah on November 3, while Montreal played Thursday and had one day of rest. And Montreal is missing Demidov, Matheson, and possibly Montembeault — if Fowler has an off night, there is no safety net. A rested Leafs team with its captain back, at home, on a Saturday night, is a live underdog. The price is fair, not a steal.

The pick

Canadiens moneyline (-115). The better team, the better goalie, the better form — at a coin-flip price.

If you or someone you know has a gambling problem, call 1-800-GAMBLER.

TSL Bets · Nov 7, 2026 · 7:55 AM ET

Spurs -5.5 (-110): A shorthanded Blazers backcourt meets the hottest big man alive

By Matt

Portland is down Morant, Sharpe, and Krejci on the second night of a back-to-back, and Victor Wembanyama just put up 52 and 30 in his last two games. Laying 5.5 with the Spurs is the side.

Game slip

San Antonio Spurs (7-2) at Portland Trail Blazers (6-2)

Saturday, November 7, 2026 — 10:00 PM ET — Moda Center, Portland, OR — DAZN-SAS, KUNP, BlazerVision

Spread: Spurs -5.5

Total: 227.5

Moneyline: Spurs -202 / Trail Blazers +170

Odds source: Consensus across major US books, Nov. 7

### Injury report

San Antonio: F Tobias Harris (calf) — out. F/C Jayden Quaintance (knee) — out. Neither has played this season, so the rotation is effectively intact: De'Aaron Fox, Stephon Castle, Dylan Harper, Devin Vassell, and Victor Wembanyama are all averaging 30-plus minutes. Wembanyama rolled his ankle near the end of the first half Friday against Sacramento but finished the game — worth watching, not a reason to fade.

Portland: G Ja Morant (Grade 2 left hamstring strain) — out. G/F Shaedon Sharpe (torn meniscus) — out. F Vit Krejci (left leg fracture) — out. G/F John Tonje — out. That leaves Damian Lillard, Scoot Henderson, and Jrue Holiday to cover the guard minutes, and Henderson is shooting 31.7% from the field this season.

### Storylines

• Wembanyama is on a tear that barely needs adjectives. He dropped 52 points, 24 rebounds, and 8 blocks on Dallas on November 5, then followed it with 30 points and 14 rebounds in Friday's 124-109 win over Sacramento. He is averaging 29.1 points per game this season and 34.6 over his last five.

• Both teams are on the second night of a back-to-back. The difference is who is missing: Portland just lost 124-118 to the Lakers on Friday night in Los Angeles, with Lillard and Deni Avdija each playing heavy minutes, and now comes home to face the West's hottest team without three rotation players.

• The Spurs are 7-2 and playing like the team that won the Western Conference last spring. Fox, Castle, Harper, and Vassell give San Antonio four more creators around Wembanyama; Portland's shorthanded backcourt has one.

• Portland is 6-2 for a reason — this team competes — and the Moda Center is a real home-court edge. The Blazers beat the Lakers in Los Angeles on October 27 and took them to the wire again Friday. They will not roll over.

The bet

San Antonio Spurs -5.5 (-110)

The number is fair, and the matchup is not. Portland's three best answers for Wembanyama's gravity — Morant's pace control, Sharpe's athleticism, Krejci's size on the wing — are all in street clothes. The Blazers' remaining guards shot a combined 31.7% (Henderson) on a night when they needed everything to go right just to hang with the Lakers. Now they get Wembanyama, who has scored 82 points in his last two games, on no rest, with a frontcourt rotation that has no answer for him. The Spurs' depth wins the minutes when Wembanyama sits, and Wembanyama wins the minutes when he plays.

Why

The math is simple and it is on our side. San Antonio is the better team at full strength, and Portland is nowhere near full strength. The Spurs' floor is high — four guards who can create, a center who bends the entire defense, and a defense that travels. Portland's 6-2 record is real, but it was built with Morant and Sharpe in the lineup. Without them, the Blazers are a different team, and the market knows it: this line opened at -4.5 and has been bet up to -5.5.

The honest caveat

Laying 5.5 on the road, on the second night of a back-to-back, is never free money. Wembanyama rolled his ankle Friday — he finished the game and scored 30, but ankles are ankles, and if he is limited or sits, this ticket is in trouble. Portland is 6-2, tough at home, and just played the Lakers to a six-point game with this same shorthanded group. And the total (227.5) tells you the market expects points, which means a backdoor cover is always live if the Spurs ease off late. This is a bet on the better, healthier team — not a lock.

The pick

Spurs -5.5 (-110). The hottest player in the league against a backcourt held together with tape. Lay the points.

Fashion · Nov 7, 2026 · 7:55 AM ET

Air Jordan 6 'White Infrared' drops today — MJ's first championship shoe returns with Nike Air on the heel

By Elijah

The OG 'White Infrared' Jordan 6 returns today, November 7, for $215 — the first retro since 2014, and the first since 1991 to bring back the Nike Air branding on the heel.

The Air Jordan 6 Retro "White Infrared" drops today, Saturday, November 7, 2026 — and for Jordan collectors, this is the one circled on the calendar all month. The OG colorway from 1991 returns in full-family sizing for $215 for men ($155 grade school, $95 preschool, $80 toddler), through Nike and select Jordan retailers.

This is the shoe Michael Jordan wore to his first championship. Tinker Hatfield designed the 6 with a streamlined shape inspired by German sports cars, and the "White Infrared" — crisp white leather, sharp red accents — is one of the model's original colorways. It has been 35 years since the debut, and 12 years since the last retro in 2014 (before that, 2010). For a lot of collectors, this is the first real shot at the pair in over a decade.

What makes this retro different

The detail that matters most: Nike Air is back on the heel. The 2010 and 2014 retros swapped the original Nike Air heel branding for a Jumpman logo. This release returns to the original construction, with the Nike Air graphic embroidered in white on the heel — the way the 1991 pair looked.

The rest of the shoe is faithful to the OG too. A crisp white leather upper with the 6's signature zig-zag structure and perforated panels across the sides, Infrared details on the midsole, the lace toggle, and the Jumpman logo on the tongue. Style code CT8529-105.

Where to get them

The "White Infrared" 6 is a wide release — Nike SNKRS and select Jordan Brand retailers, in-store and online, in full-family sizing. At $215 for men, it is also one of the more accessible grails on the November calendar, and pre-release pairs were already listed under retail in some sizes in the weeks leading up to the drop.

If you only buy one Jordan in November, the release calendars are near-unanimous: this is the one.

Gaming · Nov 7, 2026 · 7:55 AM ET

Ocarina of Time remake is 2026's highest-rated game — a 96 on Metacritic

By Matt

Nintendo's ground-up Switch 2 remake of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time launched November 5 to a 96 Metascore — the highest-rated game of 2026, and one of the 30 highest-rated games in Metacritic history.

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time is back, and the critics have spoken about as loudly as critics can speak. Nintendo's ground-up remake for Switch 2 launched Thursday, November 5, 2026, carrying a 96 on Metacritic — the highest-rated game of 2026, built from 87 critic reviews with zero mixed or negative notices among them. Players agree so far: the user score sits at 9.6.

The remake is a Switch 2 exclusive, $59.99 digital and $69.99 physical. It is, by the numbers, one of the best-reviewed games ever made — sitting inside Metacritic's all-time top 30, one rung below the original it remakes.

The original is still the one to beat

Here is the beautiful problem with remaking Ocarina of Time: the 1998 Nintendo 64 original holds a 99 on Metacritic, still the highest aggregate score the site has ever recorded for any game. The remake's 96 doesn't topple its own legend. It does, however, clear Nintendo's last attempt by a comfortable margin — Ocarina of Time 3D on the 3DS sits at 94 — and it puts the remake ahead of every other 2026 release, including Resident Evil Requiem, Phantom Blade Zero, Forza Horizon 6, and The Witcher 3 Remastered.

The review spread tells the story. The Guardian, Eurogamer, GamesRadar, and Destructoid all handed out perfect scores. IGN, GameSpot, and Forbes each gave it 9 out of 10. One outlet's verdict, quoted widely this week, called it "the gold standard for any remake of any franchise going forward."

What Nintendo actually built

This is not a remaster. Nintendo rebuilt the game from the ground up for Switch 2, with overhauled visuals across every dungeon and landmark, reworked camera and movement controls, new character dialogue, and a symphonic orchestral soundtrack. It targets and holds 60 frames per second in both docked and handheld modes, and it integrates with the Nintendo Switch App's Zelda Notes service for interactive maps and progress tracking.

There are new additions for returning players, too. A new Hall of Fallen Foes lets players view statues of the enemies they have defeated — a small touch, but the kind that tells you the developers were thinking about people who have already beaten this game three times and want a reason to do it a fourth. Nintendo's own Ask the Developer interview, published around launch, goes deep on just how much work the rebuild took.

Not everything made the cut. The lack of Master Quest — the harder alternative dungeon mode included in previous releases — is the most common complaint in otherwise glowing reviews.

Launch week had its wrinkles

No launch this big goes perfectly. Within hours of release, the remake had been dumped and run on a PC emulator, continuing a pattern that has shadowed Nintendo's biggest Switch 2 launches. And some Amazon physical pre-orders were delayed by up to a week after launch day, leaving buyers waiting while the digital version went live on schedule.

Neither wrinkle has dented the reception. At 96, the remake is also the most critically acclaimed game on Switch 2 to date — a remarkable achievement for a game that first came out before a lot of today's Switch 2 owners were even born.

NHL · Nov 7, 2026 · 7:55 AM ET

Cooley is the fastest active player to five hat tricks — and Caufield just set the NHL's overtime record

By Matt

Logan Cooley scored his second hat trick in four games and passed Alex Ovechkin as the fastest active player to five career hat tricks — but Cole Caufield's overtime winner gave Montreal a 5-4 win and made Caufield the fastest player ever to 15 career OT goals.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Logan Cooley scored his second hat trick in four games on Thursday night in Montreal, and it still wasn't enough. Cole Caufield scored 30 seconds into overtime to give the Canadiens a 5-4 win over the Utah Mammoth at Bell Centre — a night that produced two separate NHL speed records, one for each team.

Cooley became the fastest active player to record five career hat tricks, reaching the mark in 228 games and passing Alex Ovechkin, who needed 230. Caufield became the fastest player in NHL history to score 15 career overtime goals, doing it in 384 games — blowing past Connor McDavid's old pace of 559.

How the game played out

Cooley gave Utah a 1-0 lead at 1:42 of the first period, taking a stretch pass from Mikhail Sergachev at the blue line, driving in, and cutting across the goalmouth to tuck a backhand around Jakub Dobes' left pad. He made it 2-0 on the power play at 9:24, slipping behind the Montreal defense for a breakaway and scoring five-hole off a feed from Nick Schmaltz.

Montreal answered back. Nick Suzuki, playing his first game after missing six with an upper-body injury, cut it to 2-1 at 15:25, left unmarked at the left post to bury a cross-ice feed from Juraj Slafkovsky. Arber Xhekaj tied it 2-2 at 17:10 with a slap shot from the left point — his first goal of the season.

Cooley completed his fifth career hat trick with a power-play goal at 11:05 of the second period, putting Utah back ahead 3-2. Kirby Dach tied it 3-3 at 1:09 of the third in his return from illness, jamming in a loose puck at the left post. Schmaltz then gave Utah a 4-3 lead on the power play at 7:14, off a feed from Cooley — his fourth point of the night.

Adam Engstrom tied it 4-4 at 14:54 of the third, moving to the top of the slot and scoring through traffic for his first NHL goal. Thirty seconds into overtime, Caufield ended it: a 2-on-1, a fake slap shot, and a wrist shot over Sebastian Cossa's right shoulder. Cossa made 30 saves in the loss; Dobes stopped 16 for the win.

Cooley's heater is real

The hat trick was Cooley's second in four games, and the numbers behind the stretch are loud: nine points (seven goals, two assists) over his past four games. He has 11 goals in his last 10 games after a slow start to the season in which he went without a goal through his first half-dozen games.

The record he broke is a fun one to hold. Ovechkin — the greatest goal scorer in NHL history — needed 230 games to reach five hat tricks. Cooley did it in 228. It doesn't make Cooley the next Ovechkin, and nobody in Utah would say that. But being ahead of Ovechkin's pace at anything involving putting the puck in the net is a line that belongs in a young player's biography forever.

Cooley is doing it as the engine of a Mammoth team that keeps finding ways to stay in games. Utah fell to 10-6-1 but took a point on the road, and Sergachev's nine-game point streak (three goals, eight assists) is now the second-longest in Utah history, behind Clayton Keller's 10-gamer in 2025-26.

Caufield's overtime record is the quieter stunner

Fifteen career overtime goals in 384 games. McDavid needed 559 games to get there. Caufield has built his reputation as a shooter, not a volume guy, and overtime is where that skill pays off fastest — one clean look, one release, game over.

Thursday's winner was textbook Caufield: patience on the 2-on-1, a fake that froze the defense, and a wrist shot picked over the goalie's shoulder. It was also a response goal for a team that needed one. Two nights earlier, Montreal blew a 4-0 lead with 2:39 left in the third period and lost 5-4 in overtime to Winnipeg — the latest four-goal comeback in NHL history, and a loss that could have lingered.

It didn't. The Canadiens became just the fourth team in NHL history to win with a multigoal comeback in the game immediately after suffering a loss in which they held a four-goal lead, joining the Calgary Flames (October 18, 2007), the Toronto Maple Leafs (November 30, 2000), and the Edmonton Oilers (October 26, 1991). Suzuki put it simply after the game: "Big goals from different guys. Arber, 'Engy,' Kirby comes back, gets a big one there. It's a collective game."

Montreal improved to 10-3-3 and has won six of seven. Suzuki finished with a goal and two assists in his return, and coach Martin St. Louis was blunt about the bounce-back: "I loved the players' response to that game. I loved the players' response in this game."

What it means

Two records, one game, and both of them say something about where their teams are headed. Utah has a young center scoring goals faster than anyone in the league — and losing anyway, because the Mammoth are still learning how to close. Montreal has a team deep enough to survive its best players' absences, a captain who came back and produced immediately, and a closer in Caufield who now owns a piece of NHL history outright.

They meet again in the standings race soon enough. For one night, both fan bases got to watch history — just not the same kind.

NHL · Nov 7, 2026 · 7:54 AM ET

Wild are the fastest team in NHL history to 1,000 wins as Kaprizov drops four points on the Sharks

By Matt

Kirill Kaprizov scored his league-leading 16th goal and added three assists in a 4-1 win over San Jose, making Minnesota the fastest franchise in NHL history to 1,000 regular-season wins — 1,995 games, 23 fewer than the old mark.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Kirill Kaprizov scored his NHL-leading 16th goal and added three assists as the Minnesota Wild beat the San Jose Sharks 4-1 on Friday, November 6, 2026, at Grand Casino Arena in St. Paul — and the win put the Wild in the NHL record book. Minnesota became the fastest franchise in league history to reach 1,000 regular-season wins, getting there in 1,995 games.

The old mark belonged to the Philadelphia Flyers, who needed 2,018 games. The Wild beat it by 23.

How the game played out

Kaprizov opened the scoring on a 5-on-3 power play at 7:55 of the first period, beating Yaroslav Askarov five-hole with a one-timer from the right circle off a cross-ice feed from Quinn Hughes. It was Kaprizov's 16th goal of the season, the most in the NHL, and it set the tone for a night where Minnesota never trailed.

Macklin Celebrini tied it midway through the second period with his seventh goal of the season, but the Wild answered with three unanswered goals. Maxim Shabanov broke the 1-1 tie with a power-play goal at 14:15 of the second — his sixth of the season — and Hughes made it 3-1 at 6:54 of the third before Zach Bogosian capped the scoring at 13:58 with his first point of the season. Jesper Wallstedt made 23 saves as Minnesota improved to 5-0-1 at home.

Kaprizov had a hand in all four goals. Hughes finished with a goal and two assists of his own.

The 1,000-win club, on fast forward

The Wild joined the NHL as an expansion team for the 2000-01 season, which makes the pace of this milestone the story. One thousand wins in 1,995 games means Minnesota reached the mark faster than every Original Six franchise and every expansion team that came before it — including the Flyers, whose 2,018-game path stood as the standard.

It is a franchise record built on consistency more than dominance. The Wild have never won a Stanley Cup and have only reached the conference final once, but they have been one of the league's steadiest regular-season outfits for a quarter century. Mikko Koivu, captain for 11 seasons, still holds the franchise records for games played (1,028), assists (504), and points (709). Kaprizov, now in his seventh NHL season, is already the franchise's all-time goals leader with 246 — and after Friday night, he sits at 505 career points in 415 games.

The current team is the best version of that steady identity. At 12-3-3, Minnesota owns the best record in the Western Conference and the second-best mark in the NHL. The Wild are 6-0-1 in their past seven games and have won three straight.

Kaprizov is running away with the scoring race

The four-point night extended Kaprizov's point streak to seven games, during which he has five goals and eight assists for 13 points. He leads the NHL with 16 goals, and the Wild are 10-1-1 this season when he scores — and 10-0-3 when they score first, which they have now done 13 times in 18 games.

He is not carrying the team alone, either. Hughes has a seven-game point streak of his own (four goals, eight assists) and has recorded a point in 16 of 18 games this season. Four Wild players — Kaprizov, Hughes, Matthew Boldy, and Joel Eriksson Ek — are all riding seven-game point streaks at the same time.

The Sharks, meanwhile, are going the other way. San Jose fell to 7-10-1 with its fourth straight loss, scoring two or fewer goals in each game of the skid. Minnesota is 8-0-3 in its last 11 games against the Sharks, earning at least a point in every one of them.

What it means

A thousand wins is a franchise milestone, not a championship, and nobody in Minnesota will pretend otherwise. But the pace of it — fastest ever — says something real about how this organization has operated since day one: rarely bad, often good, and right now, genuinely dangerous. The Wild host the Tampa Bay Lightning on Saturday night, and the team chasing them in the West should be watching.

NBA · Nov 7, 2026 · 6:09 AM ET

Luka Doncic scores season-high 53 in Lakers win over Portland

By Matt

Luka Doncic dropped a season-high 53 — including a 62-foot halftime buzzer-beater — as the Lakers rallied past Portland 124-118, tying Allen Iverson for 10th on the NBA's all-time 50-point games list.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

The Los Angeles Lakers beat the Portland Trail Blazers 124-118 on Friday night at Crypto.com Arena, and Luka Doncic was the whole story.

Doncic finished with a season-high 53 points, 5 rebounds, and 5 assists on 17-of-33 shooting, including 14-of-18 from the free-throw line. Austin Reaves added 25 as the Lakers won their fourth straight and improved to 8-2 — and avenged a 136-129 loss at Portland on October 27.

The shot of the night

For nearly 24 minutes, the Lakers could not buy a three. Then Doncic ended the first half by making the toughest look of them all.

With six seconds left in the half, Walker Kessler grabbed a loose ball, handed it to Doncic, set a screen, and watched him launch a 62-foot heave from three-quarter court. It banked in at the buzzer — only the Lakers' fourth make from deep in 23 attempts to that point — and cut Portland's lead to 60-53 at halftime.

"He's incredible to watch," Kessler said after the game.

The heave flipped the night. Doncic scored 20 points in the third quarter alone — outscoring the entire Blazers team by five in the frame — as Los Angeles outscored Portland 34-15. A Ziaire Williams three with 4:23 left in the third gave the Lakers their first lead since Doncic's opening basket made it 2-0, and they carried an 87-75 lead into the fourth with Doncic already at 40.

Portland did not fold. A late 12-point Lakers lead dwindled to four, and Damian Lillard's two free throws made it 114-110 with 50.8 seconds left. Doncic sealed it with a floater with 27.7 seconds to go, moving past the 50-point mark, and he and Reaves combined for 10 points in the final seconds.

Why this one is historic

The 53 was Doncic's 11th career 50-point game, moving him past Kareem Abdul-Jabbar and into a tie with Allen Iverson for 10th on the NBA's all-time list. It was his second 50-point game of the season — he scored 52 in the October 25 road win over Utah — and his fourth as a Laker, tying him for fourth in franchise history.

The streak around it is even wilder. This was Doncic's fourth consecutive game with at least 40 points, making him just the fourth player in Lakers history to do that, joining Kobe Bryant, Elgin Baylor, and George Mikan. He is the first Laker to reach 40 in four straight since Bryant in 2012.

It was also his seventh straight game with at least 35 points, 5 rebounds, 5 assists, and 5 made threes. No player in NBA history had ever produced six such games in a row. Doncic has now done it seven times. He has scored at least 40 in six of his last seven games — and 180 points over his last four.

The season totals are starting to sound like Wilt Chamberlain. Doncic is averaging a league-leading 40.1 points per game through nine games, with 361 points in that span. Chamberlain is the only player to score more through his first nine games of a season — 472 in 1962-63 and 455 in 1961-62 — and no player has averaged at least 40 points in a season since Chamberlain in 1962-63.

That is the context for what is happening in Los Angeles right now: with LeBron James gone to Philadelphia in the offseason, this is Doncic's team, and he is captaining the NBA's No. 1 offense.

The box score

Points: 53

Field goals: 17-33

Three-pointers: 5-15

Free throws: 14-18

Rebounds: 5

Assists: 5

Minutes: 38

Plus/minus: +16

Final score: Lakers 124, Trail Blazers 118

Lakers record: 8-2 (four straight wins)

Around the game

• Austin Reaves: 25 points, and half of the Lakers' 10 points in the final seconds alongside Doncic.

• Walker Kessler: 16 points and 12 rebounds.

• Deni Avdija: 22 points, 9 rebounds, and 11 assists for Portland.

• Damian Lillard: 22 points for the Blazers, whose three-game winning streak ended. It was just their second loss of the season.

• NBA Cup: the Lakers improved to 1-1 in group play; the Trail Blazers fell to 0-1.

What it means

This was an NBA Cup game, a revenge game, and a statement game all at once — and Doncic treated it like all three. The Lakers trailed by 16, could not hit a three, and still won because their best player decided the second half belonged to him. At 8-2 with a four-game winning streak, and with the league's leading scorer averaging 40.1 a night, the Lakers are not just winning. They are the team everyone else in the West is now measuring itself against.

adidas x BAPE's Crazy Energy+ Leads Its Y2K Hoops Drop

By Matt

BAPE and adidas drop their Y2K hoops collection today — the Crazy Energy+ in metallic silver camo leads, fronted by Jalen Williams, with purple camo apparel and a second shoe in tow.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

BAPE and adidas are back together, and this time they've swapped the football pitch for the hardwood. Their new collection — a late-'90s and early-2000s basketball-inspired range fronted by Oklahoma City Thunder star Jalen Williams — drops today, November 7, and the shoe leading it is the adidas Crazy Energy+ in metallic silver camo.

The shoe

The Crazy Energy+ is one of adidas Basketball's newest performance models, and BAPE's version covers its sculpted upper in an all-over metallic print the brand calls COLOR CAMO SILVER — a tonal abstract ape camo with ape faces and Trefoils faintly visible in the pattern. A knitted "WGM" overlay sits where the laces would normally dominate, a nod to World Gone Mad, BAPE's long-running slogan, and the pair ships with two interchangeable lace options. Underneath the camo it's still a real hoops shoe, with Lightstrike Pro cushioning and a moulded base.

The details, as listed by release trackers:

Model: BAPE x adidas Crazy Energy+

Colorway: Silver Metallic / Silver Metallic / Silver Metallic

Style code: KH8660

Release date: November 7, 2026

Price: $200 (listed by most trackers; see note below)

One honest flag on price: most release trackers list the Crazy Energy+ at $200, but at least one outlet has it at $150. Check the CONFIRMED app on drop day before you commit.

The rest of the collection

The apparel leans on COLOR CAMO PURPLE — the purple camo print fans link to BAPE's early-2000s peak — led by a reversible mesh shark hoodie. The range also includes a statement chenille varsity jacket in black with leather sleeves, adidas Originals and BAPE ape-head patches, and big chenille "A" and "B" letters across the chest.

There's a second shoe in the drop, too: the adidas Adizero Goukana (KH8096), confirmed to also release November 7 via BAPE's retail channels, in bright pink, purple, and red tones with a large white STA motif.

When and where

The full footwear and apparel range releases today, November 7, via the adidas CONFIRMED app, select retailers, BAPE STORE locations, and BAPE.COM.

Why this one matters

The interesting call here is the silhouette itself. adidas and BAPE could have reached into the archive for this one — a Forum or a Superstar in camo would have been the safe play. Instead they put BAPE's treatment on a current performance basketball shoe, one that only launched earlier this year. The base Crazy Energy debuted in July 2026 at $110 in the Color Shift and Ice White colorways, built on full-length Lightstrike cushioning, and it's been worn on court this season by members of the adidas family — including Jalen Williams, who fronts this campaign. We first got a look at the silhouette when Steph Curry debuted it on court during his sneaker free agency season last year.

The timing fits the era the collection is chasing. The late '90s and early 2000s were when basketball style carried as much weight off the court as on it — baggy mesh, loud camo, varsity jackets — and BAPE was right in the middle of it. This drop is that whole wardrobe at once: silver camo on court, purple camo off it. It's also the latest in a busy year between the two brands, following their earlier 2026 project tied to last summer's global football tournament.

Gaming · Nov 6, 2026 · 5:09 PM ET

Steam Drops Four Free Games to Keep

By Matt

Four very different indie games — led by the 99%-rated Heartfall — are free to download and keep on Steam starting today.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Steam's newest round of free games is live starting today, November 6, 2026 — four games, free to download and keep forever. No catch, no subscription, no time limit on claiming them. Just add them to your library and they're yours.

This is part of a steady stream of free games Steam has delivered throughout 2026, and this week's set is a strong one: a story-driven visual novel RPG, a cosmic-horror cooking game, a found-footage horror short, and a post-apocalyptic tactical RPG. Four very different experiences, and every one of them carries an overwhelmingly positive rating from Steam users.

The four free games

Heartfall: A Tale in Three Lives: 99% positive; Story-driven visual novel RPG

Cucina Stellata: 97% positive; Psychological horror cooking game

Playable Archive: 97% positive; First-person found-footage horror

Outcast Tales: 95% positive; Post-apocalyptic tactical RPG

Heartfall: A Tale in Three Lives — 99% positive

The headliner of the set, and the highest-rated game of the four. Heartfall is a story-driven visual novel RPG from developer Kazehita that follows a character named Grian after he wakes up on a mysterious island. Players explore different storylines, form alliances, uncover secrets, and make decisions that change how the adventure unfolds.

The scale is what stands out: it's a fan project inspired by the YouTube series 3rd Life, featuring over 400,000 words of dialogue, 450 illustrations, and more than 50 original music tracks. A 99% positive rating puts it among the highest-rated free games on Steam — the kind of number usually reserved for established classics, not a free fan project.

Cucina Stellata — 97% positive

The weirdest game in the set, and maybe the most interesting. Developed by Filthy Eden and published by CRITICAL REFLEX, Cucina Stellata is a psychological horror experience that combines traditional Italian cooking with cosmic horror. Players prepare authentic 19th-century Italian recipes in what the game describes as "a kitchen haunted by hunger."

It starts out looking like a straightforward cooking game, then descends into disturbing imagery and strange supernatural elements. It's currently sitting at 97% positive across more than 1,100 Steam reviews, and it's short — about an hour — which makes it an easy weekend pickup.

Playable Archive — 97% positive

For horror fans looking for something different, Playable Archive is a first-person adventure from DarkStone Digital built around mysterious footage of a New England couple who discover a strange object while renovating their home. Players investigate disturbing events — demonic rituals, communicating with the dead — across multiple timelines.

The whole game runs 30–60 minutes and has to be completed in a single sitting, which fits the found-footage format perfectly. It also holds a 97% positive rating on Steam.

Outcast Tales — 95% positive

The final game in the set is something completely different: a post-apocalyptic tactical RPG with turn-based combat and roguelite mechanics. Developed by Hardlane Studio, it has players leading a dysfunctional crew of survivors through a dangerous wasteland filled with mutants, robots, and hostile raiders.

The hook is a personality system that lets characters develop friendships, rivalries, and even romantic relationships based on player decisions. Add in customizable character classes, strategic combat, and Twitch integration, and there's plenty here to keep you busy. It carries a 95% positive rating on Steam.

Why this batch is worth grabbing

Free-game drops on Steam are nothing new, but a set where the lowest-rated game still sits at 95% positive is rare. These aren't throwaway titles — they're four polished indie games across four genres, and the price of admission is zero. Whether you're hunting for something to play this weekend or just adding to the ever-growing backlog, all four are worth claiming while they're free.

MLB · Nov 6, 2026 · 2:08 PM ET

Red Sox open to trading Duran and Yoshida

By Matt

With Roman Anthony, Wilyer Abreu, and Ceddanne Rafaela locked in as the starting outfield, the Red Sox are open to moving Jarren Duran and Masataka Yoshida this offseason.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The Boston Red Sox are open to trading Jarren Duran and Masataka Yoshida this offseason. That's the word from Alex Speier of The Boston Globe, reported by Bleacher Report, as Boston looks to improve a roster that struggled down the stretch and was swept in the AL Wild Card round.

The reason is simple: the outfield is crowded, and the future is already set. Roman Anthony, Wilyer Abreu, and Ceddanne Rafaela are the starting outfield, all under team control through at least 2029. That leaves Duran and Yoshida, as Speier put it, in "a familiar state of limbo."

"With that trio in place for years, Duran and Yoshida reside in a familiar state of limbo," Speier wrote. "Either can contribute but the Sox are open to moving one or both."

The logjam

Boston has five outfielders for three spots, and the three who are staying are the three youngest and cheapest. Anthony, Abreu, and Rafaela are locked in for years. Duran and Yoshida are the odd men out — both useful, neither with a clear everyday role anymore.

Duran played 151 games in 2026 in large part because Anthony was unavailable for so much of the season. If Anthony had been healthy, Duran's role would have been a talking point for months. Yoshida, meanwhile, has been squeezed into a left field / designated hitter platoon that doesn't really have room for him either.

Duran: the fascinating, frustrating trade chip

Duran is the harder call. His ceiling is high and his floor is what makes teams hesitate.

He was an All-Star in 2024 and won the All-Star Game MVP award, hitting .285/.342/.492 with 21 home runs and 34 steals that season. Then came the slide. He hit just .207/.266/.361 in 151 games in 2026 — but still went 20/20, with 20 home runs and 21 steals.

That's the paradox of Duran's trade value right now: the power-speed combination is still there, but his chase and whiff rates soared in 2026 and his batting average cratered. His value is at an all-time low even after a 20/20 campaign, and the Sox have flirted with dealing him for two years now.

The money complicates it further. Duran turned 30 on September 5 and has two years of arbitration remaining before free agency. He's projected to earn about $11 million in 2027 through arbitration — an eight-figure salary for a player without an everyday role. The Sox may decide they'd rather sell low than carry that number.

Still, teams looking for outfield help would almost certainly have interest. A 30-year-old with 20/20 ability, two years of control, and a track record of an All-Star season is a real trade chip, even coming off a down year. Back in 2025, the San Diego Padres reportedly offered a package including ace Dylan Cease and elite catching prospect Ethan Salas for Duran, per Sean McAdam of MassLive — a reminder of what his value looked like before the slide.

Yoshida: the contract is the problem

Yoshida's situation is more straightforward, and harder to solve. He's entering the final season of the five-year, $90 million contract he signed to come over from Japan after seven seasons with the Orix Buffaloes. He has $18.6 million remaining on the deal.

He hit .274/.347/.399 in 87 games in 2026 — respectable numbers, but he's played just 142 games over the last two seasons, and at $18.6 million for a part-time DH, the contract is the obstacle. As MassLive's Chris Cotillo put it, Yoshida "looks like a goner" — Boston could try to trade him again, something it hasn't had luck with before, or simply cut bait and eat the money.

The Sox need to add a power bat this winter, and they'll need somewhere to play him. Moving Yoshida — or Duran — is how that spot opens up.

What it means for the offseason

This isn't a fire sale. It's a roster squeeze with a clear logic: three young outfielders are set, two veterans are in the way, and Boston needs to upgrade elsewhere — power, pitching, or both.

The open question is what the return looks like. Duran's value is depressed after 2026, and Yoshida's contract limits his market. The Sox will explore both markets, and how aggressive they get may depend on how much they need the money and the roster spot for the bigger moves they want to make.

One thing is clear: it would be a surprise if Boston entered the 2027 season with the same outfield it had in 2026.

NHL · Nov 6, 2026 · 10:10 AM ET

Zacha's pair powers Bruins' third straight win

By Matt

Pavel Zacha scored twice and five different Bruins found the net as Boston rolled past Pittsburgh 6-2 at PPG Paints Arena for its third straight win.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Pavel Zacha scored twice as the Boston Bruins beat the Pittsburgh Penguins 6-2 on Thursday, November 5, 2026, at PPG Paints Arena. Five different Bruins scored in the win, Boston's third in a row.

The Bruins answered Pittsburgh's early opener with three goals in the final eight minutes of the first period and never trailed again, improving to 12-4-1 while the Penguins fell to 5-9-2 with their fourth straight loss.

How the game played out

Pittsburgh struck first on its first shot of the game. Former Bruin Justin Brazeau dropped to one knee in the right face-off circle and snapped one past Michael DiPietro at 9:04 of the first period, set up by Hendrix Lapierre from below the goal line.

Boston responded with three goals in 6:50. Zacha tied it 1-1 at 12:07, finishing a cross-crease feed from David Pastrnak after JJ Peterka knocked down a bouncing puck and worked it to Pastrnak. Mark Kastelic put the Bruins in front 2-1 at 15:04, deflecting a point shot from Frederic Brunet in the slot. Elias Lindholm made it 3-1 at 18:57, stealing the puck from Erik Karlsson in Penguins territory and scoring unassisted — the eventual game-winner, his sixth goal of the season and his third game-winner.

Nick Robertson cut it to 3-2 at 7:57 of the second, redirecting a Kaedan Korczak shot for his team-leading seventh goal. Zacha answered again at 14:24, burying a wrist shot on a 2-on-1 off a feed from Peterka to restore the two-goal lead. Morgan Geekie pushed it to 5-2 at 16:57, taking a pass from Hampus Lindholm during a Pittsburgh line change and going in alone on a breakaway. Fraser Minten closed it out at 8:39 of the third, snapping one home on a 2-on-1 off a feed from Geekie for the 6-2 final.

Scoring summary

1st: 9:04; Justin Brazeau scores (Hendrix Lapierre) — 1-0 PIT

1st: 12:07; Pavel Zacha scores (David Pastrnak, JJ Peterka) — 1-1

1st: 15:04; Mark Kastelic scores (Frederic Brunet) — 2-1 BOS

1st: 18:57; Elias Lindholm scores, unassisted — 3-1 BOS

2nd: 7:57; Nick Robertson scores (Kaedan Korczak) — 3-2 BOS

2nd: 14:24; Pavel Zacha scores his second (JJ Peterka, David Pastrnak) — 4-2 BOS

2nd: 16:57; Morgan Geekie scores (Hampus Lindholm) — 5-2 BOS

3rd: 8:39; Fraser Minten scores (Morgan Geekie) — 6-2 BOS

Zacha and the top line

Both of Zacha's goals came off the same formula: the Peterka–Zacha–Pastrnak line moving the puck fast through the neutral zone and finishing on the rush. Pastrnak and Peterka each finished with two assists, and Zacha — the night's first star — finished +2 in 18:37 of ice time with five goals on the season.

The depth showed up behind them, too. Geekie scored for the third straight game and added an assist, Hampus Lindholm was +4 in 20:13 with an assist, and DiPietro made 16 saves for his fourth straight win. It was the fifth time this season Boston has put six goals on the board.

The one sour note: rookie James Hagens left early in the third period after taking a crosscheck to the back from Penguins defenseman Samuel Girard.

Why it matters

Boston has now won three straight and five of seven, sitting at 12-4-1 and on top of the Atlantic Division. The encouraging part of this one is that the Bruins didn't need Pastrnak to carry the scoring — he set up two goals without finding the net himself, and the goals came from five different sticks.

Pittsburgh, meanwhile, is sliding the other way. The Penguins have lost four straight (0-3-1), and Arturs Silovs allowed six goals on 24 shots. Boston returns to TD Garden to host the Florida Panthers on Sunday, November 8, at 1 p.m. ET.

TSL Bets · Nov 6, 2026 · 7:55 AM ET

Wild -1.5 (+102): Kaprizov past 500 and rolling against the Sharks

By Matt

Minnesota is 11-3-3 and first in the Central, Kaprizov is rolling after reaching 500 career points on November 1, and the Sharks come to St. Paul at 7-9-1. Laying 1.5 at plus money is the value side.

Game slip

San Jose Sharks (7-9-1) at Minnesota Wild (11-3-3)

Friday, November 6, 2026 — 8:00 PM ET — Grand Casino Arena, Saint Paul, MN — NHL Network

Spread: Wild -1.5 (+102)

Total: 6.5

Moneyline: Wild -258 / Sharks +210

Goalies: Jesper Wallstedt (MIN) vs. Yaroslav Askarov (SJ)

Odds source: DraftKings numbers via BetLegend, updated Nov. 6, 12:32 AM PT

### Injury report

Minnesota: G Filip Gustavsson (hip) — injured reserve, out until November 19. D Brock Faber (skull fracture) — injured reserve, expected back November 10. D Jared Spurgeon (lower body) — injured reserve, expected back November 10. F Marcus Foligno (lower body) — injured reserve, expected back November 12. C Joel Eriksson Ek missed Thursday's practice but it was a maintenance day — he will play Friday.

San Jose: C Alexander Wennberg (upper body) — injured reserve, expected back November 7. LW Igor Chernyshov (upper body) — injured reserve, expected back November 14. LW Quentin Musty (upper body) — injured reserve, expected back December 1.

### Storylines

• Kirill Kaprizov reached 500 career points on November 1 — an empty-netter against Washington, his 15th goal of the season. He is the second player in Wild history to reach the mark, after Mikko Koivu, and did it in 413 games, tied for fourth-fastest among active players. His 14 goals in October led the NHL and earned him Second Star of the Month.

• Minnesota is 11-3-3, first in the Central Division, and just beat Philadelphia 4-2 on Tuesday behind Jesper Wallstedt's 25 saves. The top line — Kaprizov, Eriksson Ek, and Matt Boldy — is intact.

• San Jose is 7-9-1 and starting Yaroslav Askarov in net. The Sharks have been game, but they are a bottom-half team walking into one of the league's best home environments against a team chasing a milestone for its star.

• The Wild's blue line is banged up — Faber and Spurgeon both out — which is the one crack in the armor. Minnesota has managed it so far: 11 wins in 17 games with a plus goal differential that sits among the league's best.

The bet

Minnesota Wild -1.5 (+102, DraftKings)

The moneyline at -258 is unplayable. The puck line at plus money is the way in. Minnesota wins games by two or more at home as a matter of routine — this is a team that beat Washington 5-1 on the road last weekend and has won 11 of 17 — and San Jose's defense is not built to keep this close for sixty minutes. Kaprizov coming off the 500-point milestone in front of the home crowd is the kind of thing that turns a two-goal lead into a three-goal lead.

Why

The gap between these teams is real. Minnesota is first in the Central; San Jose is 7-9-1 and playing its second game in three nights on the road. The Wild's top line is healthy and producing, Wallstedt has been steady filling in for Gustavsson, and the Sharks' injury list hits their middle six. Laying 1.5 at plus money on the better team, at home, with their star rolling off a milestone — that is the value side of this board.

The honest caveat

The Wild's defense is genuinely thin right now. Faber (skull fracture) and Spurgeon are both out, and San Jose has enough skill to punish mistakes if Minnesota gets loose. A one-goal game is the nightmare scenario for a -1.5 ticket, and the Sharks have kept games close this season. If the Wild's depleted blue line has a bad night, this can end 3-2 and the ticket dies on the margin. But the price is plus money for a reason — and the reason is that Minnesota is supposed to win this game by two.

The pick

Minnesota Wild -1.5 (+102). The best team in the Central, at home, at plus money, with their star one point from history. That is the bet.

2-Leg Parlay (about +308): Doncic Over 35.5 + Wild -1.5

By Matt

Two plays, one story: the league's hottest scorer at plus money, and the Central's best team laying 1.5 at plus money. Together they pay about +308.

The legs

Leg 1 — Luka Doncic Over 35.5 points (+102)

Portland Trail Blazers at Los Angeles Lakers, 10:00 PM ET. Doncic is averaging a league-leading 38.5 points per game and has scored 37 or more in six straight games — 52, 41, 37, 45, 42, and 40. He dropped 41 on this same Portland team nine days ago. The Blazers are without Ja Morant, Shaedon Sharpe, and Vit Krejci, and the Lakers' offense runs entirely through Doncic in a revenge spot. Standard line 34.5 (-115 at Fanatics); the 35.5 alt pays plus money.

Leg 2 — Minnesota Wild -1.5 (+102)

San Jose Sharks at Minnesota Wild, 8:00 PM ET. The Wild are 11-3-3 and first in the Central. Kirill Kaprizov sits one point from 500 for his career — 244 goals, 255 assists in 412 games — and the home crowd will be pushing for it. San Jose is 7-9-1 with a banged-up middle six. The moneyline (-258) is unplayable; the puck line at plus money is the value side.

Why these two together

Both legs are plus money on their own, and neither asks for anything exotic. Doncic needs to do what he has done in six straight games. Minnesota needs to beat a 7-9-1 team by two goals at home. The parlay pays about +308 — a $10 ticket returns about $40.80 — because two plus-money legs multiply instead of adding.

The honest caveat

Parlays punish the one thing you cannot control: variance. Doncic can have a 34-point night and the Lakers can still win comfortably — that kills the ticket even though the read was right. Minnesota can win 3-2 and the puck line dies on the margin. Both legs are individually sound, but they have to land on the same night, and that is always the tax on a parlay. Bet it small, the way parlays are meant to be bet.

The pick

2-leg parlay, about +308: Luka Doncic Over 35.5 points (+102) + Minnesota Wild -1.5 (+102). The hottest scorer in the league and the best team in the Central, both at plus money. That is the bet.

Collectibles · Nov 6, 2026 · 7:54 AM ET

Pokémon TCG enters the National Toy Hall of Fame

By Matt

Thirty years after it debuted in Japan, the Pokémon Trading Card Game is a Hall of Famer — inducted Thursday alongside bingo, Care Bears, and the playground slide.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

The Pokémon Trading Card Game is officially a member of the National Toy Hall of Fame. The Strong National Museum of Play in Rochester, New York, announced the 2026 class on Thursday, November 5, on the Today show — and Pokémon TCG made the cut in its first year as a finalist.

It joins three other inductees: bingo, Care Bears, and the playground slide. The four winners came out of a field of 12 finalists that also included Bop It!, Mahjong, Monster High dolls, Mouse Trap, Polly Pocket, Sorry!, Tickle Me Elmo, and Yahtzee. All four are now enshrined and on display at The Strong.

Thirty years, 85 billion cards

The timing is the story. The Pokémon TCG debuted in Japan in 1996 — thirty years ago this year — after the Game Boy games turned monster-collecting into a phenomenon. The Hall of Fame, established in 1998, recognizes toys that "have inspired creative play and enjoyed popularity over a sustained period." Few inductees can match this one on the sustained part: the game has never really left the cultural conversation since.

The numbers back it up. As of March 2026, more than 85 billion Pokémon cards have been printed and distributed worldwide. The Strong's own write-up calls the game "renowned for its relative accessibility compared to other collectible card games" — a big reason it spread from schoolyards to a global hobby that now supports grading companies, billion-dollar resale markets, and release-day lines at every major retailer.

"Pokémon TCG's widespread appeal and global presence are undeniable," curator Mirek Stolee said in the announcement.

A very good week to be a Pokémon collector

The induction lands on the same week as the biggest release day of the anniversary year. Mega Evolution—Delta Reign hits shelves today, November 6, alongside the final wave of 30th Celebration collections. It is the kind of week that shows exactly why the Hall of Fame came calling: thirty years in, the game is still setting release-day records instead of coasting on nostalgia.

The other inductees tell their own version of the same story. Bingo descends from an Italian lottery played around 1530 and is now played virtually worldwide. Care Bears, like Pokémon, got a boost from an active fan community that mobilized around the nomination. And the slide — the simplest toy on the list — was honored for its role in child development and a century of playground use.

What to watch

The Strong says the induction will likely lead to new exhibits and events around the Pokémon TCG in the new year. For collectors, the near-term watch is simpler: this is the third piece of institutional recognition the hobby has picked up in a year when the cards themselves keep breaking records. The game is not just popular. It is, officially, historic.

Shop the drop

Pokémon TCG on Amazon

Fashion · Nov 6, 2026 · 7:54 AM ET

New Balance 990v7 debuts today at $200

By Elijah

The seventh generation of New Balance's flagship 990 line arrives today — the Made in USA 990v7 in 'Kool Gray' for $200, with a campaign fronted by ROSALÍA, Cooper Flagg, and Josh Allen.

What happened

The New Balance Made in USA 990v7 debuts today, November 6. The seventh generation of the brand's flagship 990 franchise launches in the "Kool Gray" colorway for $200, available at 10 a.m. ET through New Balance and select retailers.

This is the drop the sneaker calendar has been building toward all month — DraftKings Network calls it the top sneaker drop of November 2026, and it is easy to see why. The 990 is the shoe that built New Balance's reputation with runners, dads, and fashion crowds all at once, and a new generation does not come around often.

The shoe

The 990v7 keeps the series' familiar suede-and-mesh construction — pig suede, tight-knit mesh, breathable mesh toe boxes — and streamlines the upper with updated proportions. Underneath, the cushioning setup pairs FuelCell with ENCAP, the two technologies New Balance has been building its performance line around.

The launch colorway, Kool Gray (style code U990GR7), is draped in the grey the brand is known for: Kool Gray, Calcium, and Light Arctic Gray, with a dark grey tumbled leather heel tab and a red "V7" lace holder on the leather tongue. It is, deliberately, the most New Balance-looking shoe New Balance could launch with — grey on grey on grey.

The campaign behind it is star-heavy: ROSALÍA, Cooper Flagg, Josh Allen, and IU front the launch. That is a deliberately broad net — pop, basketball, football, and K-pop — for a shoe that has always sold across tribes.

Also dropping today

November 6 is stacked beyond the 990v7:

• Nike SB Dunk Low "Champagne" — $130 at select skate shops, with a wider SNKRS drop to follow. A green patent-leather Dunk dressed like a bottle of champagne — dark green, metallic gold, a little cork under the surface — marking the run-up to the SB Dunk's 25th anniversary in 2027. Style code IQ0431-300.

• Kith x adidas Stan Smith Ballet — three coffee-toned colorways (Brown, Aurora Coffee, Chalky Brown) with the "Kith and Kin" crest on the heel, dropping at 11 a.m. across New York, London, Paris, and Tokyo time zones via Kith stores, site, and app.

• Reebok DMX Trailzillo — two general-release colorways (blue/orange and black/white/neon green) at $120 via Reebok, an archive-inspired trail hybrid with the line's moving-air cushioning.

Why this one matters

The 990 line is New Balance's crown jewel, and the v6 had a long, beloved run. A new generation is a statement that the brand thinks it can improve on a classic without breaking what made it one — and the grey-on-grey launch colorway is a bet that the core audience wants evolution, not reinvention.

At $200, it is also priced like the flagship it is. If the Kool Gray sells through today, expect the 990v7 to anchor New Balance's holiday season the way the v6 did for years.

Gaming · Nov 6, 2026 · 7:54 AM ET

God of War Laufey delayed to May 20, 2027

By Matt

Santa Monica Studio pushed God of War Laufey from February 16 to May 20, 2027 — three more months of polish for Faye's first outing as the series lead, still a PS5 exclusive.

What happened

Santa Monica Studio is delaying God of War Laufey by three months. The game was set to launch February 16, 2027. It will now arrive May 20, 2027, still as a PlayStation 5 exclusive.

The studio announced the move Wednesday, November 4, in a short statement on X. No specific problem was named — the extra time, the studio said, goes to "putting in the finishing touches and pushing the game to its fullest potential." More updates are promised soon, with no date attached to them.

The timeline

This one moved fast from reveal to release date, which makes the delay sting a little less — and a little more.

• June 2026: Revealed at PlayStation's State of Play, closing the show. Faye — Laufey, Kratos' wife and Atreus' mother — wakes after her own funeral in the Everywhen, an afterlife where gods from different mythologies cross paths. Deborah Ann Woll plays Faye; Jack Quaid voices Phranque, a gelatinous cube traveling with her.

• July 2026: The February 16, 2027 date is confirmed at San Diego Comic-Con.

• September 29, 2026: Pre-orders open, with a Standard Edition and a Digital Deluxe Edition.

• November 4, 2026: The delay to May 20, 2027 — 93 days later.

That is a quick turnaround from announcement to launch by modern standards, and the February date lasted barely four months. If you pre-ordered, the studio's statement says nothing about pre-order terms, bonuses, or editions changing — treat those as unchanged until Sony says otherwise.

Why it matters

Laufey is the first God of War where you do not play Kratos. Faye leads, with a legendary sword, a Serpent's Bow, and combat built around speed and vertical movement — a deliberate break from the weightier Norse games. The reveal trailer ran more than 20 minutes of uncut gameplay, and it introduced two gods standing in Faye's way: Sekhmet and Begtse.

Santa Monica has done this before. God of War Ragnarök slipped from 2021 to November 2022 under similar "polish" framing, and it went on to be one of the best-reviewed games of its year. A 93-day delay this late in the cycle reads like a game that is content-complete and needs optimization and bug fixes — not a rescue job.

The new date also buys breathing room. February 2027 is crowded — Tomb Raider: Legacy of Atlantis lands February 12, Final Fantasy XIV: Evercold on February 23 — and Sony's own Until Dawn 2 arrives January 28. May is, for now, a much quieter month.

NBA · Nov 6, 2026 · 6:08 AM ET

Warriors win sixth straight over Heat

By Matt

Steph Curry shook off a cold first half, passed Oscar Robertson on the all-time scoring list, and dropped 29 as Golden State ran away from Miami 120-100 for its sixth straight win.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Steph Curry scored 29 points as the Golden State Warriors beat the Miami Heat 120-100 on Thursday night at Chase Center, extending their winning streak to six games after an 0-3 start to the season. The Warriors are now 6-3 — and, per Warriors Wire, tied with the San Antonio Spurs for the longest active winning streak in the NBA.

Down early again, then the third quarter happened

The pattern is becoming familiar: the Warriors fell behind early once more. Miami led 29-26 after the first quarter, and the game was tied 53-53 at halftime. Then Golden State blew it open.

The Warriors outscored the Heat 38-23 in the third quarter and won the second half 67-47, stretching the lead to 30 early in the fourth before Curry rested with the game out of hand.

Curry's night: slow start, scorching finish, history made

Curry began 1 for 8 from the field and was 2 of 10 at halftime. Then he caught fire: 22 of his 29 points came in the second half on 7-of-9 shooting, including 5 of 7 from three-point range. He finished 9 of 19 from the floor, 6 of 12 from deep, a perfect 5 of 5 from the line, with four assists, one rebound, and zero turnovers in 28 minutes.

The 29 points left him one short of a fourth straight 30-point game — he sat once the game was decided.

The bigger moment came early. With a three-pointer at the 7:03 mark of the first quarter, Curry passed Oscar Robertson (26,710 career points) to move into 17th place on the NBA's all-time scoring list. He finished the night with 26,739 career points. It's the second time in less than a week Curry has climbed the all-time list, after passing Dominique Wilkins for 18th.

Lendeborg keeps filling the box score

Rookie Yaxel Lendeborg stuffed the stat sheet again: 17 points, eight rebounds, six assists, and three steals — falling just short of a triple-double. Draymond Green drove the defensive effort against Giannis Antetokounmpo and chipped in a game-high 11 assists, a season high.

The bench was a big part of the story, too: Golden State's reserves outscored Miami's 53-27.

The Heat side

Giannis Antetokounmpo finished with 20 points and 14 rebounds for Miami, and Davion Mitchell led the Heat with a team-high 22 points. Bam Adebayo added 18.

Context matters for Miami, though: the Heat were playing the second night of a back-to-back after needing overtime to beat the Los Angeles Clippers 117-116 on Wednesday. They fall to 3-6.

There was a familiar face on the other side, too. Klay Thompson, in his first appearance at Chase Center as a member of the Heat, scored 11 points on 3-of-10 shooting with two three-pointers in 23 minutes.

What's next

Golden State hosts the Orlando Magic on Saturday night at Chase Center. Miami visits the Sacramento Kings on Sunday.

By the numbers

Final score: 120; 100

Halftime: 53; 53

Third quarter: 38; 23

Bench points: 53; 27

Record after game: 6-3; 3-6

Warriors leaders: Stephen Curry 29 pts (9-19 FG, 6-12 3PT), Yaxel Lendeborg 17 pts / 8 reb / 6 ast / 3 stl, Draymond Green 11 ast

Heat leaders: Davion Mitchell 22 pts, Giannis Antetokounmpo 20 pts / 14 reb, Bam Adebayo 18 pts
