NFL · Nov 6, 2026 · 1:07 AM ET

Jaguars rout short-handed Ravens

By Matt

Jacksonville beat Baltimore 33-10 on Thursday night behind Trevor Lawrence's 242 yards and a defense that held the Ravens to 227 total yards — Baltimore's fourth straight loss with undrafted rookie Joe Fagnano making his first career start.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

Jacksonville beat Baltimore 33-10 Thursday night at M&T Bank Stadium, in a game that was decided less by what the Jaguars did than by who the Ravens could not put on the field.

Joe Fagnano's first career start never got going. The undrafted rookie threw for 60 yards with two interceptions, and Baltimore managed just 227 total yards all night. Trevor Lawrence, meanwhile, threw for 242 yards and a touchdown, and the Jaguars improved to 6-2. The Ravens fell to 4-5 — their fourth straight loss, and below .500 for the first time this season after starting 4-1.

The mismatch at quarterback

This was the game Baltimore had been dreading all week. Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley are both on injured reserve — Jackson with a right thumb injury, Huntley with a right toe injury — leaving the Ravens down to their third quarterback on a short week in prime time.

Fagnano finished 10 of 18 for 60 yards and two interceptions. Baltimore took the ball out of his hands as much as possible, leaning on Derrick Henry and the run game, but the Ravens never found a passing attack. The plan to load up on Henry faded fast: he carried 17 times for 57 yards, and Baltimore's only touchdown came on a 65-yard Justice Hill run in the fourth quarter, long after the game was decided.

It was not just a one-night problem. The Ravens have now been outscored 84-10 in the second half during this four-game slide. Baltimore is finalizing a trade for veteran Jacoby Brissett, but he cannot arrive soon enough — and Thursday night showed exactly why.

How it unfolded

Jacksonville took the lead for good on the opening drive, when Bhayshul Tuten powered in from the 1-yard line. The Jaguars' lead never dipped below double digits after halftime.

The decisive blow came just before the break. With under a minute left in the first half and Jacksonville up 10-3, Lawrence hit Parker Washington on a 50-yard catch-and-run touchdown — Washington juggled the ball, corralled it, and finished the play to make it 17-3. Baltimore never got closer.

From there it was Cam Little's night as much as anyone's. The Jaguars kicker hit four field goals — 36, 45, 47, and 33 yards — and Chris Rodriguez Jr. added a 2-yard touchdown run in the fourth quarter to stretch the lead to 33-3. Hill's 65-yard score, the second-longest run of his career, was Baltimore's only answer.

Jacksonville outgained Baltimore 319-227. The Jaguars' defense picked Fagnano off twice and recovered a fumble.

Final score: Jaguars 33, Ravens 10

Trevor Lawrence: 16-of-26, 242 yards, 1 TD

Joe Fagnano: 10-of-18, 60 yards, 2 INT

Jaguars record: 6-2

Ravens record: 4-5 (four straight losses)

Henry's milestone in a lost night

The one piece of history on Baltimore's side belonged to Derrick Henry. His 57 rushing yards moved him into seventh place on the NFL's all-time rushing list with 13,707 yards, passing Hall of Famers Jerome Bettis and LaDainian Tomlinson.

It is a milestone worth pausing on: Henry passed two of the most decorated backs in league history on a night when his team could barely move the ball. He did it in his 11th season, on a team that started 4-1 and has now lost four straight — a reminder that individual greatness and team collapse can share the same box score.

What the win means

Jacksonville has now won five of its past six games and sits at 6-2, firmly in the AFC mix. The Jaguars have outscored their last two opponents 75-17. This was not a statement win in the usual sense — beating a third-string rookie at quarterback is the result the standings said to expect — but the way Jacksonville handled it, cleanly and without drama, is what good teams do with games like this.

Baltimore, meanwhile, is in real trouble. Four straight losses, a quarterback room held together by a rookie and a trade that has not closed yet, and a second-half scoring drought (10 points across four games) that no defense can keep covering for. The Ravens host the Chargers next, and the season's direction depends on how fast Brissett — or Jackson's thumb — can get them a functioning passing game again.

NHL · Nov 5, 2026 · 11:07 PM ET

Mikheyev scores twice as Lightning beat Blackhawks 4-1 to stay perfect at home

By Matt

Ilya Mikheyev scored twice against his former club and Andrei Vasilevskiy made 25 saves in his 600th start as Tampa Bay extended its franchise-record home winning streak to 8-0-0.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The Tampa Bay Lightning beat the Chicago Blackhawks 4-1 on Thursday night at Benchmark International Arena, staying perfect at home to start the season and extending a franchise-record home winning streak to 8-0-0.

Ilya Mikheyev scored twice against his former club, and Andrei Vasilevskiy made 25 saves in the 600th start of his career.

How the game played out

Victor Hedman opened the scoring on the power play at 4:12 of the first period, working a give-and-go with Anthony Cirelli and driving the slot to bury his fourth goal of the season. Mikheyev doubled the lead with 1:38 left in the period, finishing a long shot from above the left circle for his third goal of the season.

The second period was scoreless — and physical. Chicago's Dillon Boucher and Tampa Bay's Jeff Viel dropped the gloves in a heavyweight bout early in the frame, and the Blackhawks kept out-hitting the Lightning, finishing the night with a 31-13 edge in hits.

Tampa Bay put it away early in the third. Ryan McDonagh came off the bench on a line change and belted a 93 mph slap shot at 2:38 for a 3-0 lead, and Mikheyev tipped in his second of the night at 5:08 to make it 4-0. Roman Kantserov spoiled Vasilevskiy's shutout bid with 49 seconds left, extending his point streak to seven games with his rookie-leading eighth goal of the season.

Scoring summary

1st: 4:12; Victor Hedman scores on the power play (Anthony Cirelli, Brandon Hagel) — 1-0

1st: 18:22; Ilya Mikheyev scores (Erik Cernak, Pontus Holmberg) — 2-0

3rd: 2:38; Ryan McDonagh scores (Jake Guentzel, Brayden Point) — 3-0

3rd: 5:08; Ilya Mikheyev scores his second (Erik Cernak, Zemgus Girgensons) — 4-0

3rd: 19:11; Roman Kantserov scores (Sam Rinzel, Ethan Del Mastro) — 4-1

Vasilevskiy's 600th start

The milestone was the quiet headline of the night. Vasilevskiy's 600th career start puts him alongside Sergei Bobrovsky (802) and Connor Hellebuyck (618) as the only active goaltenders to reach the mark. He improved to 15-1-0 in his career against Chicago, and he was 49 seconds from a shutout before Kantserov's late tip-in.

Why it matters

Tampa Bay is now 10-3-2 overall and 8-0-0 at home — a franchise-record home winning streak to open a season. The Lightning controlled the game where it counted: 35 shots to Chicago's 25, two goals from Mikheyev against the team that let him go, and a milestone night from their franchise goalie.

Chicago, meanwhile, keeps sinking on the road. The Blackhawks fell to 0-7-1 away from home and 4-12-1 overall, swept in back-to-back games in Florida after Wednesday's 5-3 loss to the Panthers. Their four-game stretch features matchups against six of the past seven Stanley Cup winners — and it continues Saturday at Carolina, while the Lightning open a four-game trip at Minnesota the same night.

Sports · Nov 5, 2026 · 4:42 PM ET

LIV Golf is shutting down after five years and $5 billion

By Matt

LIV Golf told a bankruptcy court Thursday it is winding down operations and liquidating its assets — no LIV 2.0, no 2027 season — after its $300 million rescue fell apart.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

LIV Golf is dead

LIV Golf is shutting down. The league told a U.S. bankruptcy court in Trenton, New Jersey, on Thursday, November 5, that it is abandoning its plans for a restructured LIV 2.0 and will instead wind down operations and liquidate its assets. The wind-down begins Monday, November 9.

"The debtors are not currently proceeding with the LIV 2.0 plan," attorney Matthew Williams said in court. "Instead, the debtors are toggling to a value-maximizing wind-down in their business."

The hearing lasted 13 minutes. Five years after it launched to upend professional golf, LIV is done.

How it fell apart

The collapse came fast once the money went. Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund, which bankrolled LIV from its June 2022 launch and poured more than $5 billion into the venture, announced in April 2026 that it would end funding after the 2026 season. LIV filed for Chapter 11 bankruptcy protection in New Jersey on September 8, insisting the move was meant to clear the way for a player-owned LIV 2.0 for 2027.

For a moment it looked like a rescue was coming. On October 5, LIV announced a deal with BC Partners Credit worth up to $300 million, including a $4 million initial investment. But the deal depended on enough players committing to the new version — and the commitments never came. On Wednesday, BC Partners pulled out, and by Thursday morning the league's lawyers were in court confirming the wind-down.

"We believe the wind down path is the best path," Williams told the court. In a press release, LIV's board committee said "shifting toward an orderly wind-down is the most responsible path forward for the League at this time."

LIV's attorneys intend to file a plan for winding down operations by November 9. The creditors committee meets November 20, with another bankruptcy hearing set for November 24. The league says it will remain open to third-party offers that could extract more value from its assets during the wind-down — but barring a last-minute investor, LIV will not exist beyond this year.

What it was

LIV launched in June 2022 with one pitch: Saudi money, nine-figure signing bonuses, and a 54-hole, team-based format that broke nearly every convention of professional golf. It lured some of the biggest names in the sport away from the PGA Tour — Dustin Johnson, Jon Rahm, Sergio Garcia, Brooks Koepka, Bryson DeChambeau — and fractured the sport for four years. The PGA Tour and LIV spent years in courtrooms and boardrooms, at one point announcing a framework agreement that never became a deal.

The money was staggering and the burn rate was worse. More than $5 billion from the PIF, and by April the fund decided the circuit no longer fit its investment strategy. CEO Scott O'Neil said as recently as August 5 that LIV had secured funding to keep the league alive. Two months later it was in bankruptcy court.

The human cost is already visible. Jon Rahm, the league's biggest signing, agreed to forfeit all claims against LIV — said to be worth some $100 million — to get out of his contract. Rahm, Garcia, Ian Poulter, and Tyrrell Hatton are among the notable players who have left LIV this year. Around 30 golfers had committed to the planned LIV 2.0, with another 10 undecided — not enough to save it. Players who left for LIV face a one-year suspension on the PGA Tour from the date of their last LIV tournament, making Rahm and others eligible beginning August 24, 2027.

Even in collapse, the numbers tell the story of how the money moved: LIV paid $15.6 million to retain seven of its executives in the months between the PIF pulling funding and the bankruptcy filing, including $6.375 million to CEO Scott O'Neil.

What happens now

• Monday, November 9: the wind-down and liquidation process officially begins.

• November 20: LIV's creditors committee meets.

• November 24: another hearing in the bankruptcy case.

• The players: stars who left are already scattering — Rahm and others are eligible to return to the PGA Tour in August 2027 after serving their one-year suspensions.

• The assets: LIV's remaining assets will be sold off to maximize value for creditors.

Five years, $5 billion, and a sport split in two. LIV Golf is shutting down.

Godzilla is coming to Apex Legends: Storm Point takeover starts November 10

By Matt

Godzilla stomps into Apex Legends on November 10 — a Storm Point Wildcard takeover, a heat-ray Devotion, ten kaiju Legend skins, and a mythic Dorsal Dagger, running through December 1.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Godzilla is coming

Godzilla is stomping into Apex Legends. Respawn announced the Apex Legends | Godzilla Event today, November 5, and it runs November 10 through December 1 — three weeks of the King of the Monsters tearing up Storm Point. The tagline says it all: survive the monster, become a monster.

This is not a skins-only crossover. Godzilla is on the map, and you cannot kill it. You can only survive it.

Storm Point becomes Godzilla's stomping ground

The centerpiece is a Wildcard takeover of Storm Point. The map is mid-attack: damaged buildings, burnt trees, fallen signs, and destruction scattered across the island, with Godzilla looming above it all.

When Godzilla marks an area for destruction, a countdown starts before its Heat Ray strikes. Players brave enough to run into the blast zone before the beam hits can grab a Care Package holding a custom Devotion — a heat-ray weapon that tracks enemies on hit. It is the one piece of Godzilla's power you can actually take for yourself.

There is also a new Kaiju Zone on Storm Point to explore for Kaiju Energy, Kaiju Crates, and kaiju-themed finishers.

The loot: ten kaiju skins and a mythic melee

Ten Legends are getting Godzilla-themed skins drawn from across the franchise, including some deep cuts: Octane, Fuse, Ash, Horizon, Wattson, Ballistic, Valkyrie, Wraith, Loba, and Sparrow. The monsters represented include Godzilla, Mechagodzilla, Mothra, and Destoroyah.

The headline prize is the Dorsal Dagger, a new Universal Mythic Melee inspired by one of Godzilla's dorsal fins, with a matching Deathbox. It is Wraith's first mythic melee and the game's second universal mythic melee. Nine Legendary weapon skins are in the mix too, for the Devotion, Hemlok, Rampage, CAR, Havoc, Longbow, Wingman, Prowler, and Sentinel.

The Godzilla Event Pass runs on challenges: the free track earns rewards like the Jet Jaguar Octane skin and Godzilla Event Packs, while the Premium Track (950 Apex Coins) unlocks the Iconic Ghidorah 1991 Catalyst skin, the Legendary Gravity Beam Prowler skin, Epic Apex Packs, and more. Twitch Drops run November 10–17 with select creators.

Why now

The event is tied to Godzilla's return to theaters — Godzilla Minus Zero hits theaters this week, with a 3D re-release in IMAX and large format on November 6. The timing is no accident: the movie brings the monster back to the big screen, and Apex brings it to the Outlands the same week.

Respawn teased this one first. On November 2, the official Apex account posted a 17-second clip captioned "Something big is coming…" — a glowing Apex logo splitting into a row of shapes that turned out to be Godzilla's dorsal plates, with a roar at the end. Three days later, the full reveal landed.

The details

• Event: Apex Legends | Godzilla Event

• Dates: November 10 – December 1, 2026

• Mode: Wildcard takeover on Storm Point

• Headline weapon: custom Heat Ray Devotion from blast-zone Care Packages

• Skins: 10 kaiju Legend skins, 9 Legendary weapon skins

• Mythic: Dorsal Dagger universal melee + matching Deathbox

• Platforms: PlayStation 4, PlayStation 5, Xbox One, Xbox Series X|S, Nintendo Switch 2, PC (Steam, EA App, Epic Games Store)

NFL · Nov 5, 2026 · 2:07 PM ET

Ravens down to third quarterback for Thursday night

By Matt

Undrafted rookie Joe Fagnano makes his first career start tonight against Jacksonville after injuries to Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley left Baltimore down to its third quarterback.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

Joe Fagnano makes his first career start tonight against the Jacksonville Jaguars at 8:15 p.m. ET on Prime Video.

Baltimore is down to its third quarterback after losing Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley to injury against Buffalo last week.

How the Ravens got here

Fagnano was Baltimore's emergency third quarterback. He was forced into action in the second quarter of Sunday's 36-10 loss to the Bills after Jackson and Huntley both went down, and now the job is his for Thursday night.

His NFL debut came in rough circumstances: 10-of-21 passing for 91 yards and an interception, with no first-team reps during the week. He also took a hit to his ankle in Buffalo, but he practiced fully in Baltimore's final two practices and carries no injury designation.

Who is Joe Fagnano?

Fagnano's path to a Thursday night start is about as unlikely as it gets. He began his college career as a walk-on at Maine, transferred to UConn after four years, and put together a career year as a seventh-year senior in 2025: 3,448 yards, 28 touchdowns, and just one interception.

He went undrafted in the 2026 NFL draft, then led all quarterbacks in passing yards in the preseason on his way to a spot on the 53-man roster. He becomes only the second undrafted rookie quarterback in Ravens franchise history to start a game, joining Anthony Brown, who started against Cincinnati in 2022.

What to watch tonight

The matchup is unforgiving. Jacksonville comes in at 5-2 and just put a 42-7 beating on the Colts, while Baltimore sits at 4-4 after three straight losses. The Jaguars bring the NFL's top-ranked rushing defense and second-ranked scoring defense into M&T Bank Stadium.

Baltimore does get one piece of help: rookie right guard Vega Ioane cleared the final injury report and is expected back, which matters with a rookie quarterback who needs protection.

Fagnano's turn under center is likely a one-week job. The Ravens are finalizing a trade with the Arizona Cardinals for veteran Jacoby Brissett, who has made 73 starts over 11 seasons, but Brissett won't officially join the roster until after tonight's game. Thursday night belongs to Fagnano.

The details

Starter: Joe Fagnano (first career start)

Opponent: Jacksonville Jaguars (5-2)

Kickoff: Thursday, November 5, 8:15 p.m. ET

Watch: Prime Video

Out: Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley (injured vs. Buffalo)

Ravens record: 4-4

Fagnano's debut: Nov. 1 at Buffalo: 10-of-21, 91 yards, 1 INT

Sources: USA Today, USA Today — Who is Joe Fagnano?, Ravens Wire, and Sports Illustrated.

Collectibles · Nov 5, 2026 · 11:40 AM ET

GameStop's $10,000 Pokémon pack gives you a 44.8% chance of pulling a card worth $6,000–$8,000

By Matt

GameStop's new Riptonium Power Pack costs $10,000 for one PSA-graded Pokémon card — and GameStop's own odds say nearly 75% of pulls come back worth less than the pack. The top chase is a $156,000 Lugia.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

GameStop has gone all in on the digital pack. The retailer announced Riptonium on October 10 — its biggest Power Pack tier yet — and the price is exactly what it sounds like: $10,000 for a single digital pack that reveals one PSA-graded Pokémon card.

The catch is right there on GameStop's own listing. According to the odds GameStop publishes for the pack, 44.8% of pulls land in the $6,000–$8,000 estimated value band. That is at least $2,000 less than the pack costs, before resale fees or the gap between an estimate and what a buyer can actually get for the card.

The odds, straight from GameStop

Here is the full value chart for Riptonium, as reported from GameStop's listing:

$6,000–$8,000: 44.8%

$8,000–$10,000: 29.9%

$10,000–$20,000: 22.9%

$20,000–$40,000: 1.5%

$40,000–$80,000: 0.5%

Above $80,000: 0.4%

Add it up and 74.7% of pulls come back worth less than the $10,000 pack price. Only 2.4% of pulls land above $20,000. The top pull currently listed is valued at $156,000 — a PSA 10 2005 #105 Lugia ex-Holo, per Resell Calendar's report on the pool.

GameStop warns on the listing that values can fluctuate and that the probabilities update every five minutes. The estimates are calculated from market data and feed GameStop's instant buyback offers, which pay 90% of the estimated value. So even a "break-even" pull at $10,000 is worth $9,000 the moment you take the buyback.

How Power Packs work

Riptonium is the top rung of a ladder GameStop has been building all year. Power Packs are digital mystery packs made in partnership with PSA: you buy a pack online, it opens digitally to reveal a real, physical, PSA-graded card, and that card goes into your PSA Vault — a secure, insured storage facility in Delaware — until you decide to ship it, sell it, or keep it stored. Every card was already graded and slabbed before you spent a dollar.

The platform launched to the public on April 15, 2026 with four categories — Pokémon, football, basketball, and baseball — and nine price tiers: Starter ($25), Silver ($50), Gold ($100), Sapphire ($250), Platinum ($500), Diamond ($1,000), Lunar ($2,500), Neutronium ($5,000), and Riptonium ($10,000). Higher tiers buy a deeper card pool and a higher ceiling. The category list has since grown to include One Piece and themed Pokémon packs like Rewind and On Fire.

GameStop added the $5,000 Neutronium tier after announcing its bid for eBay, and Riptonium now pushes the ceiling to $10,000. It is, by a wide margin, the most expensive single digital pack in the hobby.

Why people are buying it anyway

The math says most buyers lose money. The hobby says that was never the point.

Power Packs sell the rip — the moment of the reveal, live on a creator's stream, with a real slab on the line. GameStop publishes live pull data, so collectors can watch in real time what other people are hitting. And for the 0.4%, there is a $156,000 Lugia sitting at the top of the pool.

That is the same logic that has powered the hobby's biggest nights all year, from Logan Paul's $1.2 million vintage rip to the record $16.49 million Pikachu Illustrator sale. The difference is that GameStop has industrialized it: nine tiers, published odds, a vault, and a buyback button. The casino is open, the odds are printed on the door, and the line is still forming.

Collectibles · Nov 5, 2026 · 11:39 AM ET

VeeFriends goes luxury: the Treasure Chest packs gemstone pins and graded coins into 5,555 boxes for Black Friday

By Matt

Gary Vaynerchuk's VeeFriends is going physical in the biggest way yet — a premium Treasure Chest with sapphire gemstone pins, NGC graded coins, and Super Stickers, limited to 5,555 boxes and launching Black Friday.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

VeeFriends is ushering in a new era of premium collecting. The Gary Vaynerchuk-founded brand has unveiled the all-new physical Treasure Chest, a luxury collectible box that launches on Black Friday — and with only 5,555 chests in existence, it is the most exclusive VeeFriends Super Stickers and pin release yet.

Each Treasure Chest holds a randomized assortment of four premium collectibles, built around 21 VeeFriends characters. This is not another sticker drop. This is VeeFriends dressing up for the high-end shelf.

What's inside

Every chest contains four treasures, each featuring one of 21 possible VeeFriends characters:

• Sapphire gemstone pins framed in 18K gold plating

• NGC graded coins crafted in various metals, including pure silver and gold

• Debut Super Stickers in new sapphire colorways

• Classic pins

The scarcity is the point. Every collectible inside the chest — except the coins — is limited to 99 or fewer. The 5,555 chests break down into 555 sealed cases of 10 boxes each, and the sealed-case sale is the only chance to buy a case as a case.

How the sale works

The release rolls out in five waves, and the order matters — holders get first crack, the public gets last:

• Wave 1 — Sealed case sale: Monday, November 24 at 12 p.m. ET through Tuesday, November 25. Exclusive to select Series 1 holders, based on an account snapshot taken Monday, November 24 at 9 a.m. ET. One case per account. Price: $1,999.99 (43% off). Cases ship starting Friday, November 28.

• Wave 2 — Series 1 holder sale: Tuesday, November 25 at 12 p.m. ET through Wednesday, November 26, if inventory remains.

• Wave 3 — Series 2 and Iconics holder sale: Wednesday, November 26 through Friday, November 28 at 12 p.m. ET, if inventory remains. Price: $249.99 (29% off). Boxes ship the week of December 3.

• Wave 4 — Black Friday weekend live breaks: Friday, November 28 through Monday, December 1. VeeFriends goes live to break 55 reserved cases on Whatnot.

• Wave 5 — Public sale via EQL: Week of December 3, if inventory remains.

Series 1 Spectacular holders of the 21 featured characters also get a two-week window to claim one Treasure Chest. Hobby retailers have the box listed for preorder at $399.95, with a 10-box case at $3,989.95.

Why this one matters

VeeFriends started as an NFT project, but its physical collecting arm has been building for years. The Super Stickers line debuted at The National Sports Collectors Convention in July 2023, and the brand has been steadily turning its characters into real, hold-in-your-hand collectibles ever since — including a Spectacular Series boxed sticker set whose public pre-order draw ran November 4–5 at $99 a box, and a Monster Squad collaboration with Crocs for Halloween.

The Treasure Chest is the loudest statement of that direction yet: gemstones, gold plating, graded coins, and a print run smaller than most card-shop allocations. It lands as VeeFriends approaches its five-year anniversary in 2026, and as the brand takes a bigger live role in the hobby — VeeFriends joins Goldin, TikTok, and AFA at the Coins, Cards & CHAOS live shopping event at Valley Forge Casino Resort in King of Prussia, Pennsylvania, on November 14–15.

For collectors, the math is simple. There are 5,555 boxes, a holder-first sale ladder, and a public window that only opens if anything survives. If the Treasure Chest sells the way VeeFriends' last few drops have, the week of December 3 may not come at all.

NBA · Nov 5, 2026 · 10:09 AM ET

Wembanyama's 52 points, 24 rebounds lift Spurs past Mavericks

By Matt

Victor Wembanyama dropped a career-high 52 points with 24 rebounds and 8 blocks, sinking the deciding free throw with 24.2 seconds left as the Spurs edged the Mavericks 112-111 for their sixth straight win.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

The San Antonio Spurs edged the Dallas Mavericks 112-111 on November 4, and the entire night belonged to Victor Wembanyama.

Wembanyama finished with 52 points, 24 rebounds, and 8 blocks — a line that reads like a misprint and still somehow undersells how much of the game ran through him.

The deciding moment

With 24.2 seconds left and the game hanging in the balance, Wembanyama stepped to the line and sank the deciding free throw to put San Antonio back on top. One point was all the margin the Spurs needed.

A historic half

Wembanyama did not wait until the fourth quarter to make history. He became the first player in 30 seasons to record at least 25 points, 10 rebounds, and five blocks in a single half.

That is the kind of first-half line that usually takes a player a full game to reach. Wembanyama did it in one half, against a division rival, in a game his team went on to win by a single point.

Why this one is historic

The full line is where it gets absurd. Wembanyama is the first player in 52 years to put up at least 50 points, 20 rebounds, and 5 blocks in a game. The last man to do it was Bob McAdoo in 1974 — 52 points, 22 rebounds, 7 blocks.

Since the NBA started officially tracking blocks in the 1973-74 season, exactly two players have ever posted a 50-20-5 game: McAdoo and Wembanyama. Wilt Chamberlain did the 50-20 part at least five times, but all of those came before blocks were an official stat, so his lines can't be measured the same way. Wembanyama's 8 blocks are also the most by any player in a 50-point game since tracking began, and his 24 rebounds are the most in a 50-point game since Chris Webber went for 51 and 26 in 2001. He is the first Spur in franchise history with a 50-20 game.

And he did it while sick. Wembanyama said after the game that he had been battling a virus — "I haven't been feeling great lately. I've been sick for a while. And today, I felt like I got my legs back, so it should be more like this more often." He had scored just 12 in the previous game against Utah, then rolled his right ankle with 9:11 left in the fourth against Dallas, briefly left the game, and still came back to close it out. His own read on the night: he doesn't think he's playing his best yet.

The box score

Points: 52

Rebounds: 24

Blocks: 8

Final score: Spurs 112, Mavericks 111

Spurs win streak: 6 straight

What it means

The win is San Antonio's sixth straight, and it came the hard way — a one-point margin decided at the free-throw line in the final half-minute. Nights like this one are why the Spurs' streak feels real rather than lucky: when the game tightens, they have a player who can take over both ends of the floor and close it out.

TSL Bets · Nov 5, 2026 · 7:54 AM ET

2-Leg Parlay (about +261): Derrick Henry Under 70.5 + Jaguars -10

By Matt

Two plays, one story: the Ravens' offense is broken at quarterback, and Jacksonville is the wrong team to be broken against. Henry under 70.5 rushing yards plus the Jaguars laying 10 pays about +261.

Game slip

Jacksonville Jaguars (5-2) at Baltimore Ravens (4-4)

Thursday, November 5, 2026 — 8:15 PM ET — M&T Bank Stadium, Baltimore, MD — Prime Video

Spread: Jaguars -10 (-110)

Total: 39.5

Moneyline: Jaguars -535 / Ravens +415

Odds source: DraftKings, via Fantasy Alarm, updated Nov. 5, 5:40 AM ET; consensus via The Sport Stack, Nov. 5, 6:40 AM ET

### Injury report

Jacksonville: RB Bhayshul Tuten (ankle/foot) — questionable, game-time decision. DE Travon Walker (rib) — questionable. S Eric Murray and DT DaVon Hamilton — season-ending IR. S Antonio Johnson — cleared, available.

Baltimore: QB Lamar Jackson (thumb) — OUT, surgery, injured reserve, possibly season-ending. QB Tyler Huntley (toe) — OUT for the season, turf toe surgery. S Jaylinn Hawkins (knee) — OUT. CB Nate Wiggins (stinger/illness) — questionable. QB Joe Fagnano (ankle) — full participant, no designation, starting his first NFL game.

### Storylines

• Baltimore is on its third quarterback: undrafted rookie Joe Fagnano, who went 10 of 21 for 91 yards with two turnovers in relief against Buffalo.

• Jacksonville just won 42-7 at Indianapolis and sits second in the NFL in points allowed per game.

• The Ravens have not topped 13 points in three straight games. The line has moved 13 points from its open, Baltimore -3 to Jacksonville -10.

The parlay

Leg 1: Derrick Henry Under 70.5 rushing yards (-109, DraftKings)

Leg 2: Jacksonville Jaguars -10 (-110, DraftKings)

Combined: about +261

Why these two legs belong together

This is not two random bets stapled together. It is one thesis, expressed twice: the Ravens' offense cannot function tonight, and Jacksonville is built to punish exactly that.

Henry has topped 70 rushing yards just four times all season and has gone 53, 45, and 58 in his last three games. He now faces the NFL's No. 1 run defense by EPA — fewest yards per carry (3.4) and fewest total rushing yards (542) allowed. And if the Jaguars jump ahead, as a 10-point favorite should, Baltimore has to throw with a rookie quarterback, which takes carries away from Henry in the second half.

The spread leg is the same story from the other side. A defense that just held Indianapolis to seven points, against a quarterback making his first NFL start on a short week, with an offense that has not scored more than 13 points in three games. Jacksonville only needs to be competent to cover ten.

Each leg clears on its own merits. Together they pay about +261 — a fair price for two plays that point the same direction without depending on each other to happen.

The honest caveat

Both legs live and die with the same game, so the correlation cuts both ways. If Baltimore somehow keeps this close — Fagnano plays clean, Henry grinds out first downs, the Jaguars play conservative with a lead — both legs can lose together. And Henry owns this matchup historically, with 84 or more rushing yards in seven of his last eight against Jacksonville. This is a two-leg parlay, not a lottery ticket — but it is still one game, and one game can always surprise you.

The pick

2-leg parlay, about +261: Henry Under 70.5 + Jaguars -10. One broken offense, two ways to play it.

TSL Bets · Nov 5, 2026 · 7:53 AM ET

Jaguars -10 (-110): A 10-point favorite against a third-string rookie quarterback

By Matt

Baltimore is starting an undrafted rookie at quarterback, has lost three straight, and hasn't topped 13 points in three games. Jacksonville just won 42-7. Laying 10 on the road is a lot — but this Ravens team is that broken right now.

Game slip

Jacksonville Jaguars (5-2) at Baltimore Ravens (4-4)

Thursday, November 5, 2026 — 8:15 PM ET — M&T Bank Stadium, Baltimore, MD — Prime Video

Spread: Jaguars -10 (-110)

Total: 39.5

Moneyline: Jaguars -535 / Ravens +415

Odds source: DraftKings, via Fantasy Alarm, updated Nov. 5, 5:40 AM ET; consensus via The Sport Stack, Nov. 5, 6:40 AM ET

### Injury report

Jacksonville: RB Bhayshul Tuten (ankle/foot) — questionable, game-time decision. DE Travon Walker (rib) — questionable. S Eric Murray and DT DaVon Hamilton — season-ending IR. S Antonio Johnson — cleared, available.

Baltimore: QB Lamar Jackson (thumb) — OUT, surgery, injured reserve, possibly season-ending. QB Tyler Huntley (toe) — OUT for the season, turf toe surgery. S Jaylinn Hawkins (knee) — OUT. CB Nate Wiggins (stinger/illness) — questionable. QB Joe Fagnano (ankle) — full participant, no designation, starting his first NFL game.

### Storylines

• This line opened at Baltimore -3. It is now Jacksonville -10. A 13-point move tells you everything about what the market thinks of a Ravens team on its third quarterback.

• Jacksonville just beat Indianapolis 42-7, with Trevor Lawrence going 10 of 14 for 210 yards and 3 touchdowns. The Jaguars have won four of their last five.

• Baltimore has lost three straight and has not topped 13 points in any of them. The Ravens sit 30th in yards per play over that span.

• Jacksonville's defense is second in the NFL in points allowed per game. It just held the Colts to seven points.

• Fagnano gets a full week of practice reps this time — he had none before the Buffalo game — but a full week does not turn an undrafted rookie into a starting NFL quarterback on a short week against this defense.

The bet

Jacksonville Jaguars -10 (-110, DraftKings)

Why

Sometimes the obvious play is obvious for a reason. The Ravens are starting a quarterback who completed 10 of 21 passes for 91 yards against one of the worst defenses in the league last week, and now he faces one of the best. Baltimore's offense has scored 13 points or fewer in three straight games with better quarterbacks than the one playing tonight.

Jacksonville does not need to be spectacular here. It needs to be competent. The Jaguars' defense should keep Baltimore in the teens at most, and an offense that just scored 42 points — with a quarterback completing 65.7 percent of his passes for 17 touchdowns against 4 interceptions this season — only needs two or three clean drives to cover this number.

The honest caveat

Ten points on the road on a Thursday night is a big number, and there are real ways this goes wrong. Fagnano is better prepared this week than he was against Buffalo. Bhayshul Tuten is a game-time decision, but if he plays, Jacksonville's run game keeps the clock moving and shortens the game. And a big Jacksonville lead means a conservative second half — the Jaguars led 42-7 last week with Lawrence throwing only 14 times, which is exactly the kind of game state that lets a backdoor cover sneak in. If you want a safer way to play this game, the Henry under is the cleaner bet. This one is the conviction play.

The pick

Jaguars -10 (-110, DraftKings). The Ravens are broken at quarterback, and the Jaguars are the wrong team to be broken against.

Derrick Henry Under 70.5 Rushing Yards (-109): The league's best run defense meets a back on a cold streak

By Matt

Derrick Henry has topped 70 rushing yards just four times all season and has gone 53, 45, 58 in his last three games. Tonight he faces the NFL's No. 1 run defense by EPA — with a rookie third-string quarterback under center.

Game slip

Jacksonville Jaguars (5-2) at Baltimore Ravens (4-4)

Thursday, November 5, 2026 — 8:15 PM ET — M&T Bank Stadium, Baltimore, MD — Prime Video

Spread: Jaguars -10 (-110)

Total: 39.5

Moneyline: Jaguars -535 / Ravens +415

Odds source: DraftKings, via Fantasy Alarm, updated Nov. 5, 5:40 AM ET; consensus via The Sport Stack, Nov. 5, 6:40 AM ET

### Injury report

Jacksonville: RB Bhayshul Tuten (ankle/foot) — questionable, game-time decision. DE Travon Walker (rib) — questionable. S Eric Murray and DT DaVon Hamilton — season-ending IR. S Antonio Johnson — cleared, available.

Baltimore: QB Lamar Jackson (thumb) — OUT, surgery, injured reserve, possibly season-ending. QB Tyler Huntley (toe) — OUT for the season, turf toe surgery. S Jaylinn Hawkins (knee) — OUT. CB Nate Wiggins (stinger/illness) — questionable. QB Joe Fagnano (ankle) — full participant, no designation, starting his first NFL game.

### Storylines

• Baltimore is down to its third quarterback. Fagnano, an undrafted rookie, completed 10 of 21 passes for 91 yards with two turnovers in relief against Buffalo on Sunday.

• Jacksonville just hung 42-7 on Indianapolis. Trevor Lawrence went 10 of 14 for 210 yards and 3 touchdowns — and only had to throw 14 times.

• The Ravens have not topped 13 points in three straight games. The Jaguars sit second in the NFL in points allowed per game.

• The line has moved a staggering 13 points from its open — Baltimore -3 to Jacksonville -10 — and the total has crashed from 48.5 to 39.5.

The bet

Derrick Henry Under 70.5 rushing yards (-109, DraftKings)

Why

Henry is the only proven weapon left in this Ravens offense, and that is exactly the problem. Jacksonville knows it too.

The numbers are stark. Henry has topped 70 rushing yards just four times all season. His last three games: 53, 45, and 58 yards, at 3.5 yards per carry. And now he runs into the worst possible matchup: Jacksonville ranks No. 1 in the NFL in EPA per rush, allowing the fewest yards per carry (3.4) and the fewest total rushing yards (542) in the league this season.

The game script works against him as well. Baltimore is a 10-point underdog starting a rookie quarterback making his first start. If the Jaguars jump ahead — and everything about this matchup says they will — the Ravens have to throw to catch up, and Henry's carries dry up in the second half. Even in a close game, Baltimore's path to yards is through a stacked box, because no defense is respecting Fagnano's arm on a short week.

The honest caveat

History is the one thing arguing against this bet. Henry has recorded 84 or more rushing yards in seven of his last eight appearances against the Jaguars, and he owns this matchup in a way few backs own any opponent. If Baltimore leans on him early and the game stays close, he can clear this number on volume alone. But the 2026 version of Henry — 3.5 yards a carry over his last three, against the league's best run defense, with a third-string quarterback — is not the back who built that history.

The pick

Derrick Henry Under 70.5 rushing yards (-109, DraftKings). A cold back, the league's best run defense, and a game script that points away from the run. That is the bet.

Air Jordan 41 'Space Jam' drops today as the 30th anniversary collection tips off

By Elijah

The Air Jordan 41 'Space Jam' leads the first wave of Jordan Brand's 30th anniversary Space Jam collection today — $205, grey mesh with a light blue lattice and a glow-in-the-dark outsole.

What happened

The Air Jordan 41 "Space Jam" drops today, November 5, leading the first wave of Jordan Brand's 30th anniversary Space Jam collection. It retails for $205 (style code HV6474-003) through Jordan Brand and select retailers.

The shoe is grey mesh with a light blue lattice, a glow-in-the-dark blue outsole, and a 30th anniversary insole. It is the flagship of a campaign Jordan Brand is calling "Greatness Is Never Out of Reach," fronted by Azzi Fudd, Aaron Judge, Rema, and Teyana Taylor — plus a six-part documentary, Space Jam: Beyond the Jam, on Jordan Brand's YouTube channel.

The rest of the first wave

The Jordan 41 is not dropping alone. The first wave also includes:

• Air Jordan 1 Mid SE "Space Jam" — $140

• Air Jordan 1 Mid "Space Jam" — $165

• Air Jordan 1 Low "Lola Bunny" — $130

Call of Duty: Warzone ends on PS4 and Xbox One on November 6

By Matt

When Modern Warfare 4 Season 1 starts on Friday, Warzone stops working on PlayStation 4 and Xbox One — ending the battle royale's run on the consoles it launched on.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

Call of Duty: Warzone is ending support on PlayStation 4 and Xbox One on Friday, November 6, when Modern Warfare 4 Season 1 begins. From that point on, the battle royale will no longer be playable on either last-gen console.

It is the end of an era in the most literal sense. Warzone launched on PS4 and Xbox One in March 2020, and for more than six years those consoles have been its home base. Season 1 moves the game exclusively to current-generation hardware — PlayStation 5, Xbox Series X|S, and PC — and November 6 also marks the first time Warzone is available on a Nintendo console.

What Season 1 brings

The new season is not just a platform cull. It adds 33 new weapons from Modern Warfare 4 and introduces a new battle royale map, Hajin, as the game enters its next chapter.

For players still on last-gen hardware, though, the headline is simple: one more day. Warzone remains playable on PS4 and Xbox One through Thursday, November 5. After the Season 1 update lands, that is it.

The last-gen farewell tour

Sony gave the PlayStation 4 one last nod this week, releasing system firmware 14.02 on November 4 — a security-only patch, with no new features. It is the kind of quiet maintenance update a console gets when the industry has already moved on.

The timing is hard to ignore. GTA VI arrives on PS5 on November 19, and PlayStation has already pushed the PS5 firmware update required to play it. The last generation is not being switched off overnight, but the biggest games are done building for it.

Warzone's departure is the clearest marker yet. When the most-played free-to-play shooter on the planet stops supporting your console, the generation is over in every way that matters.

What to do if you still play on PS4 or Xbox One

If Warzone is your game and you are still on last-gen, Friday is the deadline to make a plan — upgrade to a current-gen console or PC, or find your next game. Your account, progress, and unlocks carry over to current-gen platforms, so nothing you have earned is lost. Only the old hardware is being left behind.

NFL · Nov 5, 2026 · 7:53 AM ET

Ravens finalizing trade for Jacoby Brissett after losing Jackson and Huntley

By Matt

With Lamar Jackson headed to injured reserve and Tyler Huntley out for the season, Baltimore is finalizing a trade for Cardinals veteran Jacoby Brissett — though an undrafted rookie still starts Thursday night.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

The Baltimore Ravens are finalizing a trade for Arizona Cardinals quarterback Jacoby Brissett, according to NFL Network's Ian Rapoport and ESPN's Adam Schefter, who both reported the move on Wednesday, November 4.

The deal is not done yet. Compensation for Arizona has not been announced, and the trade is expected to be completed this weekend — which means Brissett will not be available for Thursday night's game against Jacksonville. His earliest possible debut would be Monday, November 16, when Baltimore hosts the Los Angeles Chargers.

How Baltimore got here

This is a quarterback room that has been wiped out in the space of a week.

Lamar Jackson returned to the lineup last Sunday against Buffalo after missing three games with an ankle injury, then hurt the thumb on his throwing hand on the first play from scrimmage. He needs surgery, is headed to injured reserve, and may not play again this season.

His backup, Tyler Huntley, came in and went down with a turf toe injury in the second quarter. Tests revealed the injury is season-ending, and Huntley needs surgery too.

That left undrafted rookie Joe Fagnano, who completed 10 of 21 passes for 91 yards with two turnovers in his relief debut as Baltimore lost 36-10 to the Bills. Fagnano will make his first NFL start on Thursday night against the AFC South-leading Jaguars. He has been dealing with an ankle injury himself, but he was a full participant in practice on Tuesday and Wednesday and carries no injury designation.

Who Brissett is

Brissett, 33, is the definition of a veteran insurance policy. He has started for four different franchises across his career, and he opened this season as Arizona's starter before losing the job to Gardner Minshew on Sunday — benched during the Cardinals' 33-30 come-from-behind win over Dallas, then demoted to emergency third quarterback this week.

He has thrown for 1,719 yards with 10 touchdowns and 7 interceptions in 2026. He is on a one-year, $15.5 million deal he negotiated to remain Arizona's starter this season.

Baltimore had also been linked to Chicago backup Tyson Bagent, but the Ravens moved for Brissett instead. He is not a savior — he is a steady hand, and right now that is exactly what this team needs. A 4-4 club that has lost three straight and sits last in the AFC North cannot keep sending out a quarterback room held together by a rookie and a practice squad.

What it means

The trade tells you everything about how the Ravens view the rest of their season. They are not waving a white flag on a year that started with real expectations. They are buying a bridge.

Fagnano gets Thursday night, on a short week, against one of the league's best defenses. Brissett gets the weekend to learn the offense, and then the job is presumably his until Jackson can return — if Jackson can return.

For a team that has already lost its two best quarterbacks to season-altering injuries, "steady" is the whole plan now.

Celtics survive a 22-point Bucks comeback

By Matt

Boston beat Milwaukee 109-102 at TD Garden on November 4, holding on after the Bucks erased a 22-point deficit — and staying perfect at home at 5-0.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

Boston beat Milwaukee 109-102 on November 4 at TD Garden.

It was not the comfortable night it looked like becoming. The Celtics built a 22-point lead, watched the Bucks erase every bit of it, and then steadied themselves late to close out the win.

The comeback

A 22-point deficit is the kind of hole that usually ends a game early. Milwaukee refused to let it. The Bucks clawed all the way back to level terms, turning a blowout-in-progress into a genuine contest at TD Garden.

That is what makes the final score deceptive. A seven-point margin reads like a solid home win. The reality was a game Boston had to win twice — once to build the lead, and again after giving it all back.

Steadying late

The difference, in the end, was composure. After Milwaukee erased the 22-point deficit, Boston steadied itself late and found a way to finish. The Celtics did not let the comeback become the story of the night. The win did.

What the win means

Record: 6-2

Home: 5-0

Boston improves to 6-2 on the season, and the Garden remains a fortress: 5-0 at home. That perfect home record is the early-season statement. Teams can come to TD Garden and make it interesting — Milwaukee just proved that — but so far, nobody has left with a win.

Amadio scores late winner as Senators edge Sabres

By Matt

Michael Amadio buried a Tim Stutzle feed with 3:09 left to complete Ottawa's third-period comeback in a 2-1 win at Buffalo.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The Ottawa Senators stole one in Buffalo on Wednesday night, scoring twice in the third period to turn a 1-0 deficit into a 2-1 comeback win over the Sabres.

The tying goal

With the Senators trailing 1-0, Shane Pinto tied it 1-1 at 11:33 of the third period. Colten Ellis stopped Philip Tomasino's shot, but the rebound came free in front — and Pinto swept it home to pull Ottawa level.

The winner

With 3:09 left in regulation, Tim Stutzle sent a cross-ice feed to Michael Amadio, who buried it for the go-ahead goal and the 2-1 win.

Scoring summary

3rd: 11:33; Shane Pinto ties it 1-1 (rebound off Philip Tomasino's shot, stopped by Colten Ellis)

3rd: 3:09 remaining; Michael Amadio scores the winner off Tim Stutzle's cross-ice feed

Why it matters

Two third-period goals, one comeback, one road win — the kind of late swing that flips a game in under nine minutes of hockey. Pinto's rebound finish got Ottawa back in it, and Amadio's finish off Stutzle's feed closed it out before Buffalo could force overtime.

Gaming · Nov 4, 2026 · 1:11 PM ET

Dead by Daylight Removes TheBurntPeanut's Free Content in a Second, Longer Statement

By Matt

Behaviour Interactive has removed the Dead by Daylight content gifted to streamer TheBurntPeanut, saying it moved too fast and broke trust — and its second statement, posted November 3, lays out exactly what it says went wrong and what changes now.

Dead by Daylight's Halloween-weekend controversy has a second chapter. Behaviour Interactive has now removed the in-game content it gifted to Twitch streamer TheBurntPeanut, and its longer follow-up statement — posted November 3 on the main Dead by Daylight account — admits the studio moved too fast, skipped its own checks, and broke trust with its community.

This is a follow-up to our November 2 post on the first apology. That first statement, posted November 1 on the smaller DbD Pulse account, said the gift was made without appropriate approvals. The new statement, a four-page post, is Behaviour's attempt to answer the questions players were actually asking.

What happened

On October 30, during a Halloween stream, TheBurntPeanut played Dead by Daylight for the first time. Members of Behaviour's team noticed a very large audience in the game's Twitch directory watching a streamer who had not played it before, and saw a chance to welcome new players.

A staff member then gifted TheBurntPeanut access to the game's DLC characters, perks, and in-game currency during the livestream. When TheBurntPeanut offered to give the content back, concerned about the backlash to come, the official account replied in stream chat: "WE CAN TAKE THE HEAT! THEY ARE YOURS TO KEEP!"

The backlash that followed had two layers. Critics raised concerns about Behaviour partnering with TheBurntPeanut, pointing to clips from his past streams, and many players objected to the dismissive tone of the official account's reply. Some players said they uninstalled the game as the dispute escalated.

What Behaviour says it got wrong

The November 3 statement breaks the failure into three parts, in the studio's own framing:

Due diligence. The decision to give the creator in-game content and join his chat was made before taking the necessary time to look at whether it was a good idea. It happened quickly and outside the team's usual discussions. Behaviour says it normally looks thoroughly at a new creator's past content to judge whether they represent the values of inclusion its community expects. In this case, it says, that was not taken into proper consideration or discussed as a group.

Internal process. The studio says it did not have stringent enough processes and guardrails to prevent in-game items from being granted directly to players without larger consensus and approval. A team member acted without a supporting system behind them to guide them to another outcome.

Communication standards. The "WE CAN TAKE THE HEAT!" reply, along with some other messages in that session, showed a disregard for the context the team was suddenly in and crossed the line of appropriate responses from the brand, Behaviour says. That reply escalated the situation for the studio and for the community, especially for content creators on the frontlines of those conversations, and that was unacceptable.

Behaviour also states its values in the post: respect, transparency, diversity, and creating a space where people from different backgrounds and experiences feel welcome. Diversity is one of Dead by Daylight's core principles, it says, and when it chooses to work with a creator, it is putting its name next to theirs.

What is changing now

• The content is gone. Behaviour says it has already removed the content that was granted to the creator.

• Creator guidelines are being rewritten. That includes how the team interacts with creators playing Dead by Daylight, who on the team can make those calls, and how to identify creators whose past conduct is in direct opposition to the inclusive community Behaviour says it is trying to build.

• Approval methods are being updated. Request and approval methods for granting content and currency to players are being reworked, with checks and balances at the right time, and the team will be trained on the new processes.

• Creator Program rules are being enforced directly. Creators found to have violated Creator Program policy will result in removal. Any hold or removal will be communicated directly to the creator involved, with no additional communications on social channels or in program Discords. All removals are at the team's discretion.

• Internal action is confidential. Behaviour says it takes the situation very seriously and is taking appropriate action internally, but as a confidential matter it will not share further details.

The statement closes with a direct admission: "This incident has broken trust with our community who hold expectations and regards for the types of people we partner with, but more importantly expectations of the conduct and actions taken by our team in our daily work within this community. We understand it will be a slow road to regain that trust but we are committed to doing the work necessary to get there."

The response, by the numbers

The November 3 statement post itself drew a large reaction on X, with 11K comments, 4.4K reposts, and 28K likes at the time of the screenshots shared for this story — a measure of how widely this one is still being watched, not a verdict in itself.

TheBurntPeanut has also responded on stream, saying he saw the reaction online and noting a refund request that cited the collaboration and the communication team's response. He said he did not think he had said anything about transgender people in the last two to two-and-a-half years of streaming, and asked whether he ever had. That response is his, not Behaviour's, and the underlying allegations from critics remain allegations about past clips, not findings in Behaviour's statement — which focuses on its own process, not on adjudicating the streamer's history.

Why this one matters professionally

Strip away the noise and this is a process story, which is exactly why it is worth covering straight. Giving a creator a sneak peek at content is not unusual in itself — Behaviour's own first statement notes that. What made this one different, by Behaviour's own account, was speed without approval, a check that never happened, and a public reply that poured fuel on it instead of slowing it down.

The test now is not the statement. It is the next time a big streamer drops into the Fog: who approves the gift, what check happens first, and whether the public response matches the values the studio just restated.

By Matt

Rockstar has moved GTA VI's digital pre-load up to Tuesday, November 10 — two days earlier than planned — and the download runs up to 212 GB depending on your device.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Rockstar has pulled GTA VI's digital pre-load forward. Instead of waiting until November 12, players can start downloading the game on Tuesday, November 10 — two extra days to get one of the biggest downloads in gaming history onto your console before launch.

What changed

When pre-orders opened, Rockstar gave November 12 as the pre-load date. That date has now moved up to Tuesday, November 10 — two days earlier than originally planned. The launch date itself is unchanged: GTA VI still arrives November 19, 2026.

Senators at Sabres Over 6.5 (-118): Two Leaky Nets, Two Hot Attacks, One Obvious Total

By Matt

Ottawa's No. 1 goalie is on injured reserve, Buffalo's goalie sits at .874, both penalty kills rank in the bottom ten, and the total is 6.5. The over at -118 is the cleanest total on tonight's NHL board.

The game slip

Matchup: Ottawa Senators (9-6-1) at Buffalo Sabres (7-4-3)

Puck drop: Wednesday, November 4, 7:30 PM ET — ESPN+, KeyBank Center, Buffalo

Line: Sabres -125 (LetsBetMD) | Senators +110 (DraftKings/Fanatics) | Total 6.5, Over -118 (via FanDuel, per rg.org's morning prices)

Senators injury report: OUT — Linus Ullmark (lower body, injured reserve, targeting around November 7), Andre Burakovsky (lower body, injured reserve), Warren Foegele (knee, injured reserve), William Eklund (ankle, injured reserve). Ullmark is the one that changes this game: he has a .945 save percentage in eight starts, and he is not playing.

Sabres injury report: OUT — Matt Grzelcyk (lower body), Conor Timmins (lower body), Justin Danforth (upper body), Jack Rathbone (undisclosed), Konsta Helenius (upper body), Alex Lyon (upper body, days away from a return). DAY-TO-DAY — Jiri Kulich (concussion, expected back November 4). Zach Benson missed a practice last week on a maintenance day but returned to the ice Monday and is available for Wednesday's game. Buffalo's absences are mostly on defense, which is not what you want to hear when the total is 6.5.

Storylines driving the bet:

1. The goaltending gap is the whole bet. With Ullmark out, Ottawa turns to Samuel Ersson (.866) and Jonas Johansson (.848) — a long way from .945. Buffalo's Ukko-Pekka Luukkonen sits at .874 in nine starts. Neither net is stealing a low-scoring game tonight.

2. Neither penalty kill helps keep this under. Buffalo's kill ranks 28th at 68.6 percent; Ottawa's ranks 23rd at 72.9 percent. Power plays should produce, and both teams have the finishers to convert them — Tim Stutzle leads Ottawa with 21 points, and Tage Thompson leads Buffalo with 10 goals.

3. Buffalo has won three straight and is playing with confidence at home, while Ottawa is 9-6-1 and scores enough to keep up in a track meet. This is not a bet on either team winning — it is a bet that seven goals is a low bar for two offenses facing backup-level goaltending and bottom-ten penalty kills.

The bet

Over 6.5 goals (-118 at FanDuel). Seven goals is the ask, and the case is structural, not vibes: a .945 goalie is out, his replacements are at .866 and .848, the other goalie is at .874, and both penalty kills are in the bottom ten. The over is -118 at FanDuel, -120 at Fanatics and BetRivers, and -122 at DraftKings — shop it, but the number is the number.

The honest caveat: totals are the cruelest bets in hockey. One goalie gets hot for a night, one period goes scoreless on a handful of posts, and a 6-0 game can still stay under if the third period goes quiet. Seven goals is not a guarantee just because the goalies look shaky on paper — it is a probability play, and the probability is on the over's side tonight.

2-Leg Parlay (about +292): 76ers Moneyline + Senators-Sabres Over 6.5

By Matt

Two plays, no filler: the 7-1 76ers at plus money in Detroit, and seven goals in a game where both nets are leaky. Together at +112 and -118, they pay about +292.

The legs

Leg 1 — Philadelphia 76ers moneyline (+112 at FanDuel), at Detroit, 7:00 PM ET on ESPN. Philadelphia is 7-1, unbeaten on the road, and getting plus money against a 6-1 Detroit team missing starting center Jalen Duren (hamstring) and Isaiah Joe (hip). Joel Embiid is off the injury report after managing plantar fasciitis, and the 76ers' only loss this season came against Detroit in a game Embiid rested. This time he plays, Detroit's frontcourt has to handle him without Duren, and the better team is the underdog. Full game slip, injury reports for both teams, and the honest caveats are in the standalone 76ers post.

Leg 2 — Ottawa Senators at Buffalo Sabres, Over 6.5 goals (-118 at FanDuel), 7:30 PM ET on ESPN+. Ottawa's No. 1 goalie, Linus Ullmark (.945 in eight starts), is on injured reserve; his replacements sit at .866 and .848. Buffalo's Ukko-Pekka Luukkonen is at .874. Both penalty kills rank in the bottom ten (Buffalo 28th at 68.6 percent, Ottawa 23rd at 72.9 percent), and both teams have the scorers to punish that — Tim Stutzle leads Ottawa with 21 points, Tage Thompson leads Buffalo with 10 goals. Seven goals is a low bar for this matchup. Full game slip and injury reports are in the standalone Senators-Sabres post.

Why these two, together

A parlay only works if each leg clears on its own, and both of these do. The 76ers leg is a value moneyline on the better team at plus money. The total leg is a structural play on goaltending and penalty kills, not a guess about which team wins. Neither leg depends on the other, neither is a long shot dressed up as a lock, and there is no third leg added just to juice the payout.

At the morning prices (+112 and -118), the two legs combine to about +292. Books vary, so check your book's parlay price before placing it — the legs are the bet, not the exact combined number.

The honest caveat, stated plainly: parlays need everything to go right. Embiid's minutes could stay restricted and turn the Detroit game into a coin flip, and a hockey total can die on a single hot period from either goalie. If either leg makes you uncomfortable on its own, play it straight instead of parlaying it. That is always the right call.

76ers Moneyline (+112): The East's Best Team Is Somehow the Underdog in Detroit

By Matt

Philadelphia is 7-1, unbeaten on the road, and getting plus money against a Detroit team missing its starting center. With Embiid off the injury report, the 76ers at +112 is the value side of the night's marquee game.

The game slip

Matchup: Philadelphia 76ers (7-1) at Detroit Pistons (6-1)

Tip-off: Wednesday, November 4, 7:00 PM ET — ESPN, Little Caesars Arena, Detroit

Line: Pistons -2.5 | Total 218.5 (via Odyn's morning board); 76ers moneyline +112 (via FanDuel, per rg.org's morning prices)

He has been managing plantar fasciitis in his right foot and played under a minutes restriction against Chicago on Monday (15 points, 8 rebounds in about 25 minutes), but the clean report is a real development — and the restriction could come off entirely if the game is tight.

Pistons injury report: OUT — Jalen Duren (right hamstring strain, targeting a return around November 9), Isaiah Joe (hip bruise, was targeting this game but ruled out). Detroit's starting center is out, and its bench shooting takes a hit with Joe sidelined.

Storylines driving the bet:

1. These are the East's two best records, and Philadelphia's only loss came against this opponent — in the first meeting on October 25 in Philadelphia, a game Embiid rested. This time Embiid is available, and Detroit's frontcourt of Paul Reed and John Collins has to deal with him without Duren.

2. Philadelphia is 7-1 after a 126-107 win over Chicago on Monday, when Tyrese Maxey had 26 points and 11 assists, Jaylen Brown had 25 and 11, and VJ Edgecombe added 21. Detroit is 6-1 after a 124-91 win in Charlotte on Monday, when Cade Cunningham scored 33 and Duncan Robinson added 24. Both teams are hot; only one is getting plus money.

Philadelphia 76ers moneyline (+112 at FanDuel). A 7-1 team that has not lost on the road, getting plus money against a team missing its starting center, with its own superstar back off the injury report — that is the kind of price that does not need a complicated case. The spread is only 2.5, so the moneyline is barely more expensive in probability terms than laying the points, and it pays plus money instead.

The honest caveats: Embiid's foot is still a management situation, not a solved problem — if the restriction stays tight, Philadelphia's paint edge shrinks. Detroit is 6-1 at home for a reason, Cunningham is playing at an All-NBA level, and the Pistons already beat this team once. If Embiid is limited to 25 minutes again, this becomes a coin flip, and +112 on a coin flip is still fine — it just isn't the steal it looks like if he plays 35.

Sports · Nov 4, 2026 · 10:37 AM ET

Jets Pull Off the Latest Four-Goal Comeback in NHL History, Stunning the Canadiens 5-4 in OT

By Matt

Down 4-0 with 2:39 left in Montreal, the Winnipeg Jets scored four goals in 2:06 and won 5-4 in overtime — the latest four-goal comeback in NHL history, snapping a nine-game winless skid.

Down 4-0 with 2:39 left on the clock, at the Bell Centre, against a Montreal team riding a five-game winning streak, the Winnipeg Jets did the impossible. Four goals in 2:06 of regulation, then Dylan Samberg's overtime winner, and a 5-4 victory that will live in the record book — and in Canadiens fans' nightmares — for a very long time.

The 2:06 that changed everything

The comeback began at 17:21 of the third period, with 2:39 remaining. Tyrel Bauer scored his first NHL goal, on a Dylan DeMelo dump-in that took a funny bounce off the left boards, to make it 4-1 and end Jacob Fowler's shutout bid. Fowler had stopped the first 37 shots he faced and was 2:39 from his second shutout in three games.

It only accelerated from there. Morgan Barron made it 4-2 at 18:03, shooting on a 2-on-1 and beating Fowler glove side off the crossbar. Kyle Connor then scored twice on one-timers from the right circle with Stuart Skinner pulled for the extra attacker — cutting it to 4-3 with one minute left, then tying it 4-4 with 33 seconds left on a shot that deflected in off Canadiens defenseman Noah Dobson.

In overtime, Gabriel Vilardi took the puck away from Lane Hutson along the boards, Adam Lowry fed a cutting Samberg, and Samberg scored blocker side from the right circle at 3:30 to complete it.

Bauer's first NHL goal, 17:21 of the 3rd: 4-1 Canadiens

Barron, 18:03 of the 3rd: 4-2 Canadiens

Connor, 1:00 remaining: 4-3 Canadiens

Connor again, 33 seconds remaining: 4-4

Samberg, 3:30 of overtime: 5-4 Jets

Why this one goes in the record book

Winnipeg began its comeback 57 minutes and 21 seconds into the game, making it the latest four-goal comeback in NHL history, per NHL Stats cited by ESPN. The previous mark belonged to the Los Angeles Kings, who began their rally against Dallas at 52:46 on November 10, 2007 — with 7:14 remaining. The Jets beat that record by nearly five minutes.

The four goals in 2:06 were also the fastest four goals in Jets franchise history. The previous franchise record was four goals in 2:16, set on November 9, 1984.

And it ended a miserable stretch. Winnipeg entered the game on a nine-game winless skid (0-7-2) and had just been beaten 7-0 at Carolina on Sunday. This was the Jets' first win since October 13. "We needed something to happen, some kind of crazy thing to happen, and four goals in 2:06, that doesn't happen very often, if ever," Winnipeg coach Scott Arniel said. "It was amazing. That felt like a playoff win in there."

The other side of it

Montreal had done everything right for 57 minutes. Owen Beck opened the scoring at 3:05 of the first, Josh Anderson made it 2-0 at 18:18, and Cole Caufield scored twice on the power play — at 4:00 of the second and 46 seconds into the third — to build the 4-0 lead. Caufield has now scored against every NHL team.

Then it all came apart, and Caufield did not hide from it. "You look at that game, we were winning but we weren't beating them," Montreal coach Martin St. Louis said. Caufield was blunter: "The guys thought the game was over, I think. Listen, a four-goal lead, there's no excuse for this. Yeah, it was terrible."

The loss ended Montreal's five-game winning streak, though the Canadiens (9-3-3) extended their point streak to eight games (6-0-2). Fowler finished with 42 saves. Skinner made 18 for Winnipeg (5-8-3), and Bauer and Barron each finished with a goal and an assist.

What it means going forward

One win does not fix a 5-8-3 start, and the Jets know it. But a team that had lost nine straight, that had been shut out 7-0 two days earlier, that trailed 4-0 with under three minutes left — that team now has a night nobody in that room will ever forget. "That's a long, hard stretch, but this group believed in each other," Bauer said. "We're proud of that. We had each other's backs in that room. We're going to run with that and we're not looking back."

New Era's Brewers World Series Collection Gives a First Title Three Distinct Looks

By Matt

New Era's Brewers World Series collection covers Milwaukee's first championship three ways: a black Locker Room set for the celebration, team-colored side-patch caps in six silhouettes, and a white Parade cap — already sold out in one style.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Milwaukee waited a long time for a first World Series title, and New Era is marking it the way only New Era can: with a lot of caps. The Brewers World Series collection, now listed at New Era's official store, gives the same milestone three distinct treatments — one for the celebration itself, one for everyday team colors, and one for the parade.

The three approaches

The Locker Room Collection is built around the championship's first moments. Its black 9FORTY A-Frame and 9TWENTY adjustable caps put "World Series Champions" across the front, with the Brewers logo centered in the composition — the look of the clubhouse celebration, made wearable.

The Side Patch designs take the opposite route: team-colored caps carrying commemorative side patches. Six silhouettes carry the treatment — the 59FIFTY, Low Profile 59FIFTY, 9FIFTY, 9SEVENTY Stretch Snap, 9FORTY M-Crown, and 9TWENTY — so the choice is about fit and format (fitted, snapback, or adjustable) without changing the central design idea.

Then there is the Parade cap, and it breaks away from both. The Parade 9FIFTY A-Frame uses a white crown and gray visor as a lighter setting for the Commissioner's Trophy, positioned behind an embroidered waving flag reading "World Champions '26." It is the most distinct piece in the collection — and, per the current listing, the one already marked sold out.

Why the hat story is the story here

Championship merch usually collapses into one design stamped on everything. What makes this collection worth noting is that it doesn't. The Locker Room set is for the night it happened. The side-patch set is for wearing a Brewers cap that happens to carry a World Series patch, long after the celebration. The Parade cap is for the day the city actually celebrates together. Three different jobs, three different caps, one title.

That matters more than usual for Milwaukee because this is a first. The Brewers beat the Tampa Bay Rays 4-1 in Game 5 to win the franchise's first World Series, and "2026" on a side patch is not a generic anniversary number — it is the year it finally happened. Availability varies by style, and the Parade cap's sold-out listing is a snapshot of retail status at a particular moment, not a claim about permanent rarity or future value. Check New Era's official store for current stock, pricing, and sizing before assuming any style is still there.

The bottom line for collectors

If you want the celebration look, the Locker Room set is the obvious pick. If you want a cap you will actually wear every week, the side-patch range — especially the 59FIFTY fitted — is the one built for that. And if you wanted the Parade cap, you may already be late: that white-and-gray A-Frame was the first to go. For a first championship, that is probably the most Milwaukee outcome of all — the parade cap selling out before the parade feeling has even faded.

Gaming · Nov 4, 2026 · 10:37 AM ET

The Elder Scrolls VI Has a Name at Last: Sentinel

By Matt

Eight years after The Elder Scrolls VI was first announced, Bethesda finally gave it a full title — The Elder Scrolls VI: Sentinel — in a video celebrating the studio's 40th anniversary. No release date, no gameplay, just a name — and a desert.

After more than eight years of silence, Bethesda finally said the word. The Elder Scrolls VI is called The Elder Scrolls VI: Sentinel, studio chief Todd Howard revealed on November 3 at the end of a video celebrating Bethesda Game Studios' 40th anniversary. No release date. No platforms. No gameplay. Just a title card, a few seconds of desert, and the loudest single word in gaming in years.

How the reveal actually happened

The announcement came in a video looking back at the studio's history, with Howard turning at the end toward Bethesda's future. The teaser that followed is brief: a camera looking down on the crest of a dune, the Bethesda Game Studios logo flashing as it zooms in, and the wind unearthing an Elder Scroll — complete with an inlaid blue gem and surrounding golden runes. The background cuts to black, the runes hang in the dark, and then the wordmark appears: The Elder Scrolls VI: Sentinel.

Xbox Wire posted the anniversary video and the name reveal on the morning of November 3. Bethesda's own follow-up was deliberately short: development continues, and it will be "some time" before there is more to share.

What the name tells us — and what it doesn't

Sentinel is the capital city of Hammerfell, the desert province on Tamriel's western coast, home of the Redguards. To Hammerfell's northeast is Skyrim; to its southeast is Cyrodiil, where Oblivion takes place. The dune in the teaser fits a province predominantly known for its deserts, and reports ahead of the reveal had already pointed to Hammerfell as the setting.

But be precise about what Bethesda actually confirmed: the title. The studio has not officially announced the game's setting, its story, or its platforms, and the teaser contains no combat, exploration, or gameplay footage of any kind. The Hammerfell reading is the overwhelming consensus of every outlet covering the reveal — and it is a very good reading — but it is still a reading, not a Bethesda announcement.

The teaser does drop one more lore breadcrumb. It closes with a quote attributed to Yazar, a Moth Priest in service to High Queen Iradne of Hammerfell, dated 4E 206: "The scroll offered only a shimmer, a veil thinly lifted to my eyes. A future lost. A victory undone. Sand of time scattered by fate." Skyrim begins in 4E 201, so a quote dated five years later strongly implies Sentinel takes place after Skyrim — not a prequel, and not set between earlier games. Whether the game itself is set in 4E 206 or later is still unclear, and Yazar and Iradne are best read as the first two names attached to the game, not confirmed characters with confirmed roles.

The wait is not over

Here is the cold water, and it matters. Bloomberg's Jason Schreier said on the day of the reveal that the game is still more than two years away. If that reporting holds, Sentinel does not launch before 2028 — a full decade after the game was first announced in June 2018. Bethesda has confirmed no window of its own, so treat 2028 as the earliest date consistent with Schreier's reporting, not as an official date.

That is the strange shape of this reveal: eight years to get a subtitle, and at least two more years to get the game. Fans spent the day celebrating a word, because after eight years, a word is progress. The 40th-anniversary video was a celebration of a studio's past — and, for the first time in years, a real signal about its future. It just isn't a release date, and nobody should pretend it is.

Why this one matters anyway

The Elder Scrolls is one of the few franchises where a title reveal alone moves the whole industry's calendar. Skyrim, released November 11, 2011, is approaching its 15th anniversary, and it has been re-released on essentially everything with a screen since. A new mainline Elder Scrolls game is not just another release — it is the kind of game other publishers schedule around. Sentinel's reveal, even as just a name, finally gives that future a shape: a desert province, a Redguard capital, a prophecy dated five years after Skyrim, and a wait that is measured in years, not months.

Hughes Scores Twice, Hellebuyck Wins Home Debut as Devils Top Oilers

By Matt

Jack Hughes scored twice in 45 seconds of the second period, and Connor Hellebuyck stopped Kasperi Kapanen in the shootout to seal a 4-3 Devils win over Edmonton — Hellebuyck's first home win as a Devil.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Jack Hughes needed just 45 seconds to flip a game. The New Jersey Devils star scored twice in the second period, turning a 1-1 tie against the Edmonton Oilers into a 3-1 Devils lead — and New Jersey held on from there, closing out a 4-3 shootout win.

The 45-second burst

With the game knotted 1-1 in the second period, Hughes struck twice in rapid succession. In less than a minute of game time, the Devils went from level to up two goals, and the momentum of the night swung firmly to New Jersey's side.

Game tied in the second period: 1-1

Hughes scores twice in 45 seconds: 3-1 Devils

Shootout: Devils win 4-3

It was the kind of burst that defines a game: two goals before the opposition can even regroup, and a two-goal cushion for the Devils to protect the rest of the way.

Hellebuyck seals it in the shootout

The game still needed a shootout to decide it, and that's where Connor Hellebuyck made his mark. The Devils goaltender stopped Kasperi Kapanen in the shootout to seal the 4-3 victory — his first win at home as a Devil.

For Hellebuyck, it was a milestone night in a new sweater: a shootout stop on the final shooter to close out a win over one of the league's heavyweights.

Why it matters

Beating Edmonton is never routine, and doing it with a 45-second scoring explosion from your star plus a shootout-clinching stop from your new goaltender is a statement. Hughes provided the offense in a flash; Hellebuyck provided the finish.
