Jason Robertson's Natural Hat Trick in 5:43 Sparks Stars Past Capitals 6-5

By Matt

Jason Robertson scored three goals in the opening 5:43 of the second period — his seventh career hat trick — as Dallas built a 4-0 lead and held on to beat Washington 6-5.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Jason Robertson needed less than six minutes to turn a close game into a rout — and then the Dallas Stars needed the rest of the night to survive what Washington threw back at them. Robertson's natural hat trick in the opening 5:43 of the second period powered Dallas to a 6-5 win over the Capitals on Tuesday night at Capital One Arena.

The blitz

Dallas carried a 1-0 lead into the second period, thanks to Joel Kiviranta's first-period goal — the only tally of a low-shooting opening frame. Then Robertson took over, scoring three consecutive goals against Capitals goaltender Logan Thompson and chasing him from the net.

Robertson (power play): 0:23; 2-0 Dallas; Wyatt Johnston, Roope Hintz

Robertson: 3:18; 3-0 Dallas; Arttu Hyry, Tyler Myers

Robertson: 5:43; 4-0 Dallas; Thomas Harley, Roope Hintz

Three goals in 5:43, on just four shots. It was the seventh hat trick of Robertson's career — and because no Capital scored in between, it was a natural hat trick.

The comeback

A 4-0 lead was not enough to put Washington away. The Capitals clawed back to 5-3 by the second intermission, then kept pushing in the third. Roope Hintz briefly restored breathing room, scoring 1:23 into the third — assisted by Esa Lindell and Robertson, giving Robertson his fourth point of the night — to make it 6-3.

Washington answered again. Justin Sourdif scored at 12:55 of the third to cut it to 6-4, and Ryan Leonard made it a one-goal game with 1:02 left. But Dallas held on through the final minute to escape with the 6-5 win.

By the numbers

1st: 1; 0

2nd: 4; 3

3rd: 1; 2

Final: 6; 5

• Jason Robertson: 3 goals, 1 assist, 4 points, 5 shots on goal in 17:05 of ice time — the game's top scorer.

• Roope Hintz: 1 goal, 2 assists, including assists on two of Robertson's three goals.

• Dallas scorers: Joel Kiviranta, Jason Robertson (3), Mikko Rantanen, and Roope Hintz.

• Washington's late push: goals from Justin Sourdif and Ryan Leonard in the final eight minutes of the third period made it 6-5.

Why it matters

Robertson has made a habit of these bursts — this was his seventh career hat trick — but the speed of this one stands out: three goals before the second period was six minutes old, against a goaltender in Logan Thompson who had no answer for any of them. Dallas built a four-goal cushion off one player's stick, then showed just enough resolve to protect it when Washington refused to go quietly.

Razer Unveils $350 Raiju V3 Pro Signature Edition — Only 1,337 Units

By Matt

Razer's new Raiju V3 Pro Signature Edition wraps a tournament-grade PS5 controller in a magnesium alloy exoskeleton — and only 1,337 serialized units will ever be made in this initial run.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Razer has unveiled the Raiju V3 Pro Signature Edition, a limited-edition PlayStation 5 controller that turns its competitive flagship into a collector's piece. The price is $349.99, and the run is capped at 1,337 individually serialized units worldwide.

A magnesium exoskeleton

The headline is the shell. The Signature Edition swaps the standard body for a magnesium alloy exoskeleton with an open polygonal design across the top and back plates — you can see the internals through it. Chamfered edges and a nano coating keep debris out while leaving that open-air look intact.

The rest of the materials match the premium billing: a laser-etched Razer logo on a semi-transparent, tinted glass touchpad, matte black micro-textured TPU grips built to resist oils and abrasion, and double-shot PBT buttons so the legends never fade. The whole controller weighs just 260 grams.

Razer calls it its most ambitious PS5 controller to date, and the design celebrates a decade of Raiju development — the line debuted in 2016.

Same tournament-grade core

Underneath the new shell, it's the same competitive feature set as the standard Raiju V3 Pro:

• Contactless TMR thumbsticks in a symmetrical layout — anti-drift sensors for precise aim

• Six remappable multi-function buttons: two claw-grip bumpers and four mouse-click back buttons

• Up to 2,000Hz HyperPolling Wireless on PC for sharper input reporting

• Officially licensed for PS5, optimized for PC

The honest read, per the coverage: the $349.99 premium over the standard model (which launched at $220) buys materials and exclusivity, not new sensors or extra buttons. This is a statement piece that happens to be tournament-grade, not a performance upgrade over the base V3 Pro.

What's in the box

Every unit ships with a collector-grade kit: a leatherette carrying case, alternate thumbstick caps, four interchangeable back button covers, an anodized aluminum back button tool, and a HyperSpeed Wireless dongle.

When and where to get one

The Raiju V3 Pro Signature Edition goes on sale November 10, exclusively at Razer and select RazerStores, in regionalized drops. Units are first-come, first-served, and Razer will share drop timing and access details on its social channels on November 6.

Two things worth knowing before you set an alarm: the 1,337 figure is described as an initial supply, so a future run isn't fully ruled out — and Razer hasn't said how units split by region, so buyers outside the US shouldn't assume local stock until allocation is confirmed. The timing is no accident either: the launch lands just before GTA 6, and Razer is clearly positioning this as the upgrade to have in hand for it.

• Razer's $350 PS5 controller ships November 10, limited to 1,337 units

Sports · Nov 3, 2026 · 7:41 PM ET

Rosas Jr. Taps Covington in 43 Seconds in Rome Shocker

By Matt

Raul Rosas Jr. needed just 43 seconds to submit Colby Covington with a rear-naked choke in the Hype Italy main event in Rome.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Raul Rosas Jr. just pulled off one of the wildest results of the year. The UFC bantamweight submitted former interim welterweight champion Colby Covington with a rear-naked choke just 43 seconds into their submission-only grappling main event at Hype Italy in Rome on Tuesday night.

The finish

It was over almost before it started. Rosas pressured Covington immediately, secured an early takedown, and quickly worked his way onto the bigger man's back. Covington couldn't clear the choking arm before Rosas locked up the rear-naked choke and forced the tap 43 seconds into a match scheduled for 10 minutes.

The size gap made it even more stunning. Rosas competes at 135 pounds in the UFC; Covington used to fight at 170. None of it mattered once the action started.

One timing note: Hype's own announcement and the ESPN graphic going around tonight round it to 40 seconds. The 43-second figure comes from MMA Fighting's report of the finish, and it's the number most outlets have settled on.

Three days' notice

Rosas wasn't even supposed to be in this match. Covington was booked to face Dillon Danis in the main event, but Danis withdrew — MMA Mania reports it was an injury — and at Sunday's press conference, the promoter asked if anyone in attendance would step in. Rosas, sitting in the audience, raised his hand.

"I'll do it, f—k it," he said. "Chiwiwis!"

That gave the 22-year-old roughly three days to prepare for a much larger opponent with elite NCAA Division I wrestling credentials. He needed less than a minute to finish the job.

The reaction

The broadcast cut to Conor McGregor at ringside laughing as the tap came, and both men referenced him afterward. McGregor, a teammate of Danis, was a special guest at the show.

Covington owned it. He said he started slowly and had been focused mainly on his wrestling rather than jiu-jitsu — he hadn't trained Brazilian jiu-jitsu in years — but offered no excuses for the loss.

Rosas thanked Covington for accepting the last-minute matchup and reiterated that his primary goal remains becoming the youngest champion in UFC history. He rides a six-fight MMA winning streak into whatever comes next, fresh off his first UFC main-event win over Raoni Barcelos in September.

What's next

Covington isn't slowing down. He wrestles Khamzat Chimaev for the RAF Cruiserweight Crossover Championship in Abu Dhabi on November 14, and his next Hype match is already set against UFC lightweight Ignacio Bahamondes on December 20.

TSL Bets · Nov 3, 2026 · 7:52 AM ET

2-Leg Parlay (~+111): Hurricanes ML + Lightning ML — The Two Best Home/Road Favorites on the Board

By Matt

Two favorites that should win outright: Carolina, the NHL's best team at 12-2-2 on a 10-game win streak, and Tampa Bay at home against a back-to-back Detroit team missing its captain. Together at -192 and -260, they pay about +111.

The legs

Leg 1 — Carolina Hurricanes moneyline (-192, FanDuel). The Hurricanes are 12-2-2, the defending champions, winners of 10 straight, with a goaltender in Pyotr Kochetkov who is 10-0-0 to open the season. Nashville is 5-4-5 and starting Juuse Saros at 3-3-4. Carolina doesn't need to cover anything here — just win, which is what this team does.

Leg 2 — Tampa Bay Lightning moneyline (-260, FanDuel). Tampa is 8-3-2 at home against a Detroit team on the second night of a back-to-back, coming off a physical win at Florida on Monday, and missing captain Dylan Larkin (injured reserve/suspension). Tampa's scorers — Kucherov, Hagel, Guentzel, Point — are all available, and numberFire gives the Lightning a 69.1 percent win probability.

The reasoning

Each leg clears on its own: Carolina is the best team in hockey against a .500 team, and Tampa is a -260 home favorite against a tired, short-handed visitor. That's the parlay discipline — no leg that needs a miracle, no leg that only works if the other one hits. At -192 and -260, the two together pay about +111 — a little better than even money for two favorites that should both win.

The honest caveat: parlays are still parlays. One bad night — Saros standing on his head, or Vasilevskiy's shaky .877 save percentage turning into another soft night — and the whole ticket is gone even if the other leg cashes easily. That's why this stays a two-legger: two favorites, no lottery tickets.

Lightning vs. Red Wings Over 6.5 (+110): Two Offenses, a Back-to-Back, and a Shaky Vezina Winner

By Matt

Tampa Bay averages 3.69 goals a game, Detroit 3.4 — and the Red Wings are on the second night of a back-to-back after beating Florida on Monday. The total is 6.5, and the over pays +110. Take the goals.

The game slip

Matchup: Detroit Red Wings (7-5-3) at Tampa Bay Lightning (8-3-2)

Puck drop: Tuesday, November 3, 7:00 PM ET — ESPN+, Benchmark International Arena, Tampa

Line: Lightning -260 | Red Wings +210 | Total 6.5, Over +110 / Under -134 (via FanDuel)

Red Wings injury report: OUT — Dylan Larkin (captain, injured reserve/suspension, expected back November 14), Marco Kasper (upper body), Mason Appleton (undisclosed), Albert Johansson (undisclosed, expected back November 5). Larkin being out is the biggest name on either injury list — but Detroit just beat Florida 5-3 on Monday without him, and this bet doesn't need Detroit to win. It needs goals, from either side.

Lightning injury report: OUT — Yanni Gourde (hip, injured reserve to December 2). Day-to-day — Erik Cernak (undisclosed), Zemgus Girgensons (lower body). Tampa's core scorers — Kucherov, Hagel, Guentzel, Point — are all available.

Storylines driving the bet:

1. The offenses are both live: Tampa Bay averages 3.69 goals per game, Detroit 3.4, and both clubs went 6-2-2 over their last 10. Tampa's last 10 averaged four goals for and 3.3 against; Detroit's averaged 3.4 for and 3.0 against. Add those up and 6.5 looks short.

2. Detroit is on the second night of a back-to-back after a physical 5-3 win at Florida on Monday night, with Daniil Tarasov (who beat his former team Monday) likely going again or John Gibson stepping in. Back-to-back legs mean looser defense late — exactly when totals get decided.

3. Andrei Vasilevskiy, Tampa's starter, is not having a Vezina season: 6-3-1 with a 3.00 goals-against average and a .877 save percentage, 48th in the league, and he gave up four goals on 33 shots (.879) in his last outing before a shootout loss. A shaky Vasilevskiy is the over's best friend.

The bet

Lightning vs. Red Wings Over 6.5 (+110). This is the game-total leg of tonight's rotation — no side, no star, just goals. Two teams combining for roughly seven goals a game, a back-to-back visitor, and a home goalie saving under 88 percent of shots. At plus money, the over is the value.

The honest caveat: Tampa has lost three of its last four, and a tight, low-event game is always possible when a favorite is slumping — and Detroit without Larkin can go quiet for stretches. But the total is 6.5, not 7.5, and both teams' recent form says seven goals is the baseline, not the ceiling.

If gambling stops being fun, call 1-800-GAMBLER.

By Matt

The game slip

Matchup: Edmonton Oilers (11-2-2) at New Jersey Devils (9-5-0)

Puck drop: Tuesday, November 3, 7:30 PM ET — ESPN+/Hulu/Disney+, Prudential Center, Newark

Line: Devils -154 | Oilers +128 | Total 6.5 (via FanDuel/Bleacher Nation); McDavid anytime goal +140

Devils injury report: Anthony Mantha suffered an undisclosed injury October 30 against Vegas, left the game early, and had no update from head coach Sheldon Keefe beyond "we just don't know at this point." Connor Hellebuyck — the Devils' blockbuster acquisition from Winnipeg — is 2-0-0 in his first two Devils starts (1.50 GAA, .923 save percentage) but was scratched entirely one night after his debut with no official explanation, then listed as the expected starter for tonight. That uncertainty is real, and it's priced into this bet's caveat below.

Storylines driving the bet:

1. McDavid has a point in all 15 games this season — 39 points on 9 goals and a league-leading 30 assists, with 11 multi-point games. He has exceeded his points prop in 11 of 15 games with a set line.

2. He has scored in seven of 15 games, including multiple goals twice, and per PickDawgz he has scored at least one goal in five of Edmonton's last six games as a road underdog. Edmonton is a road underdog again tonight (+128).

3. Edmonton is 7-0-0 on the road this season. The Oilers' offense travels: McDavid and Draisaitl (28 points) are the two most dangerous players on the ice in this game, and New Jersey's goaltending situation — brilliant new starter, unexplained scratch, backup Jake Allen at 5-3-0 with a 3.11 GAA — is unsettled enough to attack.

The bet

Connor McDavid anytime goal (+140). This is a player-prop lead for a reason: you're not betting on Edmonton to win, or on a goalie you can't predict — you're betting on McDavid, who has scored in nearly half his games this season and has a point in every single one, to find the net once against a team whose crease is a question mark tonight.

The honest caveat: Hellebuyck, if he starts and plays like he did in his first two Devils games (one goal allowed to Dallas, two to Vegas), is exactly the kind of goalie who ruins a +140 anytime-goal ticket. And McDavid has not faced New Jersey yet this season. But +140 on the best player in the sport, in a game where his team is an underdog and he has scored in five of his last six as one, is a price worth taking.

Hurricanes -1.5 (+124): A 10-Game Win Streak Meets a .500 Predators Team

By Matt

Carolina is 12-2-2, riding a 10-game win streak behind a goaltender who has won all 10 of his starts. Nashville is 5-4-5 and starting a goalie at 3-3-4. Laying 1.5 at plus money is the value side of the NHL's best team.

The game slip

Matchup: Carolina Hurricanes (12-2-2) at Nashville Predators (5-4-5)

Puck drop: Tuesday, November 3, 8:00 PM ET — ESPN+, Bridgestone Arena, Nashville

Line: Hurricanes -192 | Predators +158 | Total 6.5 (via FanDuel); Hurricanes -1.5 at +124 (via ESPN lines)

Hurricanes injury report: OUT — Jordan Staal (calf), Taylor Hall (upper body). Alexander Nikishin (not injury related) is working back as an extra. Staal's absence is real — he's the captain — but it's a longer-term absence, not a fresh flag, and Carolina has won 10 straight with this group.

Predators injury report: OUT — William Trudeau (upper body, since early October). Nashville is otherwise healthy, which makes the gap in the standings harder to explain away — this isn't an injury story, it's a talent and form story.

Storylines driving the bet:

1. Carolina is the NHL's best team by record (12-2-2), the defending champion, and the No. 1 team in ESPN's power rankings every week since the opener. The Hurricanes just shut out Winnipeg 7-0 on Sunday.

2. Pyotr Kochetkov has won 10 straight starts to open the season (10-0-0, 1.50 GAA, .929 save percentage, two shutouts). Nashville counters with Juuse Saros at 3-3-4 with a 3.00 GAA and .902 save percentage. That goaltending gap is the bet.

3. The last time these teams met twice in 11 days last December, Carolina swept both games by three goals. Nashville's top four scorers — Forsberg, Stamkos, O'Reilly, Josi — are all 32 or older and carrying the offense; Forsberg is hot (four goals, nine points in his last four), but one hot line doesn't beat this Carolina team.

The bet

Carolina Hurricanes -1.5 (+124). The moneyline at -192 is the safe play and a bad price for this column. The puck line pays plus money because Carolina has to win by two — and this Carolina team wins by two a lot: a 7-0 shutout Sunday, a sweep by three goals in the last head-to-head set, and a goalie who hasn't lost yet. Nashville is 5-4-5 for a reason.

The honest caveat: Forsberg is genuinely on fire, Saros can steal a game, and a one-goal Carolina win — the most common way good teams win on the road — burns this ticket while cashing the moneyline. If you can't stomach that, take the -192 and move on. But at +124, the puck line is where the value lives tonight.

If gambling stops being fun, call 1-800-GAMBLER.

Collectibles · Nov 3, 2026 · 7:51 AM ET

FBI Warns Pokemon Card Shops About a New No-Payment Scam Hitting Stores

By Matt

The FBI's Newark office warned November 2 that scammers are targeting local card shops and Pokemon vendors with a scheme that lets them walk out with high-value cards without paying — contactless payments that never clear, and a phone app loaded with stolen credit cards.

The FBI is now, officially, a player in the Pokemon card hobby — and not in a fun way. The bureau's Newark, New Jersey, office warned on November 2 that a new scam is targeting local card shops and Pokemon TCG vendors, one designed to let bad actors buy high-value cards without spending any money at all.

How the scam works

According to the FBI notice, reported November 2, seven shops have been targeted so far. The scheme has two faces. First, thieves try to pay for cards via a contactless mobile payment that never actually clears — the transaction looks plausible at the counter and falls apart after the cards are already gone. Second, and more brazen, a cell phone app "operated by a criminal network that stores multiple stolen credit cards" is used to present payment that isn't theirs.

In one example cited in the warning, a man attempted to sell a box of trading cards to a store while an accomplice reached over the counter and stole additional cards — the old distraction play, dressed up for a hobby where a single card can be worth thousands.

Why now, and why Pokemon

The warning lands weeks after the Department of Justice announced an investigation into the biggest Pokemon card heist ever recorded, and it fits a pattern the hobby has felt all year: as card values have climbed, robberies and fraud targeting shops have climbed with them. A shop's singles wall is, functionally, a jewelry case that the public is invited to browse.

For collectors, the practical takeaway is boring and useful: buy from shops you know, be suspicious of deals that route around normal payment, and if you're selling, don't let a "payment" walk out the door with the card before it clears. For shops, the FBI's message is the same one the hobby keeps learning the expensive way — the cards are worth real money now, and the people targeting them know it.

This is also the week the hobby's calendar turns: Pokemon's November slate — the Delta Reign expansion and the 30th Celebration Day & Night Ultra-Premium Collections — lands November 6, which means foot traffic and high-value inventory in shops are both about to spike. That's exactly the moment a warning like this matters most.

Sources: FBI Issues Surprise Official 'Pokemon' Trading Card Warning, Every Pokemon TCG Product Releasing in November 2026

PS Plus November Is Live: Dead Island 2, the Tomb Raider Darkness Trilogy, and Monster Train 2

By Matt

November's PlayStation Plus Monthly Games are claimable today through November 30: Dead Island 2, Tomb Raider IV-VI Remastered, and Monster Train 2. Because the Tomb Raider collection holds three games, the month technically delivers five games for the subscription you're already paying for.

November's PlayStation Plus Monthly Games are live today, and this is one of the stronger months of the year. Sony announced the lineup on October 28 on the PlayStation Blog, and as of today, November 3, all three titles are claimable through Monday, November 30, for members at every tier — Essential, Extra and Premium alike. Claim them in that window and they stay playable for as long as your subscription stays active.

The lineup

Dead Island 2 (PS5, PS4) is the headline. Dambuster Studios' first-person zombie action RPG drops you in HELL-A — a quarantined, blood-soaked Los Angeles — for melee combat built around the FLESH system, which renders zombie damage in spectacularly gross detail, solo or in three-player online co-op.

Tomb Raider IV-VI Remastered (PS5, PS4) is the three-for-one. Aspyr's collection bundles the whole Darkness Trilogy: The Last Revelation (Lara in Egypt, racing her mentor Von Croy to an ancient tomb), Chronicles (a globe-trotting set of flashback adventures told after Lara is presumed dead), and The Angel of Darkness (the darker, divisive Paris-and-Prague chapter) — with a toggle between original and remastered visuals.

Monster Train 2 (PS5 only) is the one gap for PS4-only subscribers: there is no PS4 version in this offer. The deckbuilding roguelite sequel has you leading clans of former angels and demons aboard trains through Hell, Heaven and the Abyss, with five new clans in the mix.

The fine print, and the regional wrinkle

October's games — F1 25, Hunt: Showdown 1896 and Earth Defense Force: World Brothers 2 — left the claim list after Monday, November 2, so if you missed that deadline, they're gone. November's window runs the other way: claim by November 30, play whenever.

One regional note: subscribers in Japan get Secret Neighbor in place of Dead Island 2, a Japan-only swap. Everyone else gets the lineup as listed. Sony has not yet detailed November's Extra and Premium Game Catalog additions — those usually land mid-month — but the Monthly Games trio alone makes this a five-game month in practice, and Dead Island 2 is the kind of big-budget open-world game that usually anchors a month on its own.

The game

Release stage: Game Preview

Platforms: Xbox Series X/S, PC, and cloud

Game Preview is the key detail here: this is not a finished, final release dropping onto the service. It is arriving in preview form, which means Game Pass subscribers get in on day one while the game is still taking shape.

One Piece Sets Sail in Fortnite: Treasure Hunters Is Live

By Matt

The Straw Hats are in Fortnite. Treasure Hunters launched November 1 with a new 80-player island, Devil Fruit Mythics, and the One Piece crew rolling out in waves through mid-November.

The wait is over, and the Going Merry has replaced the Battle Bus. Fortnite's pirate mini-season, Treasure Hunters, launched Sunday, November 1 with update v43.00, and its centerpiece is the One Piece crossover fans have wanted for years — the first time the anime has gotten a dedicated Fortnite season.

This is not a skins-only collab. One Piece is built into how the season plays.

A new island built for a treasure hunt

Chapter 7 Season 4, Override, is out. In its place is Crossbones Isle, a skull-shaped island that holds 80 players instead of the usual 100, and the whole season is structured around the chase: track targets, crack chests, beat rival crews, and claim treasure before anyone else does. Sprites are staying too, with new variants and Mastery Mondays.

Two of the island's locations come straight out of the anime: Arlong Park and Windmill Village. And the season's opening shot says everything about the tone — the Going Merry sailing through fog under a full moon, carrying players into the drop.

The powers are the point

The gameplay trailer, which dropped October 30, shows the One Piece abilities working as Mythic items rather than cosmetic nods. Luffy's Gum-Gum Fruit gives you the stretchy rubber attacks, including the Gum-Gum Pistol. Ace's Flame-Flame Fruit brings the fire. Zoro's Three Sword Style and Usopp's slingshot are in as Mythics too, alongside a tangerine healing item for the Nami fans.

That is what separates this from most crossovers. You are not just dressed as the Straw Hats. You are fighting like them.

Who you can play, and when

Here is the part that tripped a lot of people up: only two One Piece characters are in the Battle Pass. Portgas D. Ace and Nico Robin headline the pass (reported at 800 V-Bucks), alongside a new original character called Captain X — the voice you hear at the One Piece chest on the island — and anime-style remixes of Jonesy, Cuddle Team Leader, and Charlotte.

The rest of the crew comes through the Item Shop in waves:

• November 1 (launch day): Monkey D. Luffy, Nami, and Usopp

• November 7: Roronoa Zoro, Sanji, and the Chopper Sidekick

• November 13: Buggy

So if you want the full crew, pace your V-Bucks. Luffy is not in the pass, and Zoro and Sanji are a week out.

The season keeps rolling

Treasure Hunters is a mini-season — about five weeks, closing with a Chapter 7 live event on December 5 — and it does not stand still. Each week brings a new captain with their own crew and point of interest, on what Epic calls Captain's Days: November 1, 7, 12, 14, and 21, each expanding the loot pool with new weapons and Sprites. The Straw Hats and A$AP Rocky — who has his own battleship on the map and a signature Shock Rocket — are first. Lil Uzi Vert follows, and Cap'n Hatsune Miku and Kasane Teto close out the lineup.

One Piece is not stopping at Fortnite, either. Rocket League's limited-time event runs November 1 through November 23, with challenges unlocking a Luffy Straw Hat Topper, a Straw Hat Crew Boost, and a Going Merry Sea Trail.

Watch the trailer

The official gameplay trailer is the best way to see the season's loop in action — the treasure hunt, the crews, and the Devil Fruit powers all in one place:

The season is live right now, and Luffy, Nami, and Usopp are already in the shop. The only question is whether you are dropping Arlong Park first — or going straight for the treasure.

2-Leg Parlay (~+141): Cavaliers ML + Panthers ML

By Matt

Two home favorites in the best spots on Monday's board — Cleveland against a six-man injury list, and Florida against a leaky Detroit penalty kill. Together they pay about +141.

The legs

Leg 1 — Cleveland Cavaliers moneyline (-180, BetMGM). Oklahoma City is down six players, including Chet Holmgren and Jalen Williams, and has lost its last two games by an average of 22.5 points. Cleveland's core — Harden, Mitchell, Mobley, Allen — is intact at home. The Cavs do not need to cover 4.5 here; they just need to win, and everything about the injury report says they will.

Leg 2 — Florida Panthers moneyline (-182, FanDuel). Detroit is missing its captain (Dylan Larkin) and a chunk of its lineup, its penalty kill has allowed 14 goals in 45 chances, and its goaltending has been shaky. Florida has won three of four at home despite its own injury list. The Panthers are the better, healthier team in this building.

Combined price: about +141. Two favorites, no lottery tickets, no ten-leg dreams. Each leg has to clear on its own — and each one does, on the injury report and the form, before you ever combine them.

The honest caveat

Parlays are still parlays: one bad night from either favorite sinks the whole ticket, and Shai Gilgeous-Alexander can ruin the Cleveland leg by himself if he goes for 40. That is why the legs are moneylines, not spreads — you are not asking either team to win by a margin, just to win. If either number moves against you before tipoff, or if a late injury report changes the picture, pass and take the single bets instead.

2. This should be a competitive game, which means Seattle keeps throwing late. A blowout either way is the enemy of a receiving prop; a close game is its friend.

3. Seattle just beat Kansas City 20-3 in Week 7 behind its defense, and Chicago arrives with extra rest after beating New England on Thursday night in Week 7. Both teams have something to prove in primetime, and primetime games lean on stars. JSN is Seattle's star tonight.

The bet

Jaxon Smith-Njigba over 99.5 receiving yards (-114). The math is simple: a receiver averaging 123.9 yards a game, in a game projected to be close, at a line almost 25 yards below his average. Even a below-average JSN night clears this if Seattle throws 35-plus times, which it will if Chicago's offense keeps it close — and Williams' recent form says it will.

The honest caveat: Cross being out is real. Darnold has already thrown eight interceptions and faced the highest pressure rate per dropback of any quarterback this season, and without his left tackle that pressure gets worse. If Darnold is running for his life, JSN's targets get shorter and scarcer. But JSN's game is built on quick separation and yards after the catch — the exact profile that survives a shaky pocket. Take the over, and expect him to clear it in the fourth quarter, not the first.

Gaming · Nov 2, 2026 · 7:50 AM ET

Nagoshi Studio Is Officially Dead — and So Is Gang of Dragon

By Matt

Toshihiro Nagoshi's NetEase-backed studio was dissolved by shareholder vote on October 31, ending Gang of Dragon, the Yakuza-style action game announced at The Game Awards last December.

It is official now, in the least ceremonious way possible: Nagoshi Studio is gone, and the game it was built to make is gone with it. A notice in Kanpo, the Japanese government gazette, confirms the studio was dissolved by a shareholders' resolution on October 31, 2026 — ending development of Gang of Dragon, the melee-based action-adventure set in Shinjuku, Tokyo, that was announced at The Game Awards in December 2025.

The news had been expected for months, but the quiet of it is striking. There was no farewell statement, no social post, no final trailer. The studio simply faded: its YouTube channel and official website disappeared over the summer, co-head Daisuke Sato changed his bio to "Ex-Nagoshi Studios," and its Google Business listing was marked permanently closed.

How it fell apart

Nagoshi Studio was founded by Toshihiro Nagoshi, the creator behind Yakuza and Like a Dragon, under NetEase's umbrella — an attempt to build a new contender and evolution for that series. Gang of Dragon starred Ma Dong-seok (Don Lee) and was pitched as a hard-hitting, street-level action game in the tradition Nagoshi built his name on.

The money ran out first. Reports dating back to March 2026 said NetEase pulled funding after it emerged that another ¥7 billion — roughly $44 million — would be needed to finish the game and bring it to market. For all of NetEase's size, that was apparently the line.

Why it stings

This is not just another cancelled game. Nagoshi is one of the most important creators in Japanese games, and Gang of Dragon was supposed to be his second act — a new studio, a new publisher backer, a new world, with one of Korea's biggest action stars attached. Instead, it becomes a cautionary tale about how quickly even a legendary name can lose its runway when the funding math stops working.

The game was only revealed a few months before the money stopped. Fans never got a release date, never got a playable demo, never really got more than the promise. Now they will not get the game at all.

Tom Brady's Card Vault Opens Its 20th Store — First in Pennsylvania, in Pittsburgh's Strip District

By Matt

Card Vault by Tom Brady opened its first Pennsylvania location Sunday at The Terminal in Pittsburgh's Strip District — its 20th store nationwide, with fans lining up before the doors opened.

The hobby's retail boom has a new address. Card Vault by Tom Brady opened its first Pennsylvania store on Sunday at The Terminal in Pittsburgh's Strip District, its 20th location nationwide, with fans lining up before the opening to get a first look.

The timing was deliberate: the grand opening was scheduled to coincide with a major Pittsburgh sports weekend, and the Strip District — already one of the city's busiest foot-traffic corridors — gave the store a ready-made crowd.

What the store offers

The Pittsburgh location carries singles, sealed boxes, packs and collectibles from Topps, Panini, Upper Deck, Pokémon and Fanatics Authentic, according to the store announcement. Customers can also submit cards for professional grading through PSA, Beckett and SGC — the three names that matter most to anyone trying to turn a pull into a graded asset.

The location also features Card Vault's signature vault: a rotating selection of autographed and authenticated memorabilia, including helmets, bats, balls and other pieces. That vault concept is the brand's calling card — part retail floor, part museum case — and it is a big reason the chain has scaled to 20 stores.

Why this opening matters

Brady did not attend the grand opening, though he is expected to visit the Pittsburgh location in the near future. His name on the door is the point, though: Card Vault is the most celebrity-backed retail bet in the hobby, and its expansion into Pennsylvania — a state with deep sports-card roots and a Pittsburgh collector scene that has been waiting for a flagship — is a signal of how mainstream the modern card shop has become.

Twenty stores is not a pop-up experiment anymore. It is a chain, and Pittsburgh is the latest proof that the card boom did not end when the pandemic-era frenzy cooled. It just got a storefront.

Gaming · Nov 2, 2026 · 7:50 AM ET

Dead by Daylight Apologizes After TheBurntPeanut Stream Sparks a Weekend Backlash

By Matt

Behaviour Interactive apologized November 1 after a staff member gifted streamer TheBurntPeanut free access to Dead by Daylight's full roster during a Halloween stream — a decision the studio says was made without proper approval.

Halloween weekend brought Dead by Daylight an unwelcome kind of attention. Behaviour Interactive apologized on November 1 after a staff member gifted popular Twitch streamer TheBurntPeanut free access to the game's entire roster of Survivors and Killers during a Halloween stream — setting off a community backlash that ended with the studio admitting it handled the whole thing badly.

The sequence, as reported by Dexerto and Games.gg, went like this. TheBurntPeanut, who built his audience largely on extraction shooters like Arc Raiders, played Dead by Daylight for the first time on stream and discovered he had been given instant access to dozens of characters that regular players unlock over time with in-game currency. "Guys! I just got all the guys!" he said on stream, joking about "streamer privileges."

Then the game's official Twitch account weighed in with a blunt message: "WE CAN TAKE THE HEAT! THEY ARE YOURS TO KEEP!"

The apology — and why it did not land cleanly

The backlash had two layers: objections to Behaviour supporting TheBurntPeanut, including criticism of his past on-stream language, and anger at the dismissive response from the official account. Some players posted that they had uninstalled the game as the dispute escalated.

Behaviour's November 1 statement, posted on DbD Pulse, its community account, identified two failures: the decision to award the content was made without the appropriate approvals, and when concerns were raised during the stream, the team should have investigated them instead of dismissing them. The studio also condemned inflammatory language directed at members of the community and said threats against employees were unacceptable, adding that it has begun reviewing its internal processes to prevent a repeat.

Even the apology drew criticism — for appearing on the smaller DbD Pulse account rather than the main Dead by Daylight account, which has 1.3 million followers. For a community that has stuck with the game through ten years of service, the placement read as part of the problem: the loud response went on the big stage, the apology on the small one.

What to watch

Giving creators a sneak peek at content is not unusual in itself — Behaviour's own statement notes that — and this story is really about process: who approved it, how concerns were handled in the moment, and where the accountability shows up afterward. The studio says its review is already underway. The community, judging by the weekend, will be watching whether anything actually changes the next time a big streamer drops into the Fog.

By Matt

Two passing-game props from the Vikings-Lions game, both off the same October 31 snapshot: Goff's yardage line and Hockenson's receiving line. Props only — no moneyline legs.

The game slip

Matchup: Minnesota Vikings (6-0) at Detroit Lions (4-2), Ford Field, Sunday at 1:00 PM ET on FOX.

Line: Detroit -3 | Total 46.5 | Moneyline: Lions -170, Vikings +142 (via ESPN scoreboard, DraftKings).

Minnesota injury report: Running back Aaron Jones is listed questionable with a knee issue, a status that could change the Vikings' rushing plan if he is inactive.

Detroit injury report: The Lions' secondary is banged up — Joseph is out and McCreary's status was in doubt heading into the weekend. Amon-Ra St. Brown, who tweaked an ankle after catching nine passes for 153 yards against Green Bay in Week 7, is not on the Lions' latest injury designations and is set to play.

Storylines driving the bet:

1. The NFL's last unbeaten team visits a home favorite, and a 46.5 total says the books expect points — points come from passing yards.

2. Jared Goff has hit the passing-yards over in 13 of his last 16 games, and he is at home, where Detroit's offense is at its best.

3. Minnesota's passing game has been grinding — Jefferson had four catches for 44 yards last week, and Murray attempted just 24 passes. If the Vikings are going to keep up at Ford Field, the ball has to go somewhere, and the tight end is the safety valve when the outside is covered.

The bet

Leg 1: Jared Goff Over 260.5 passing yards (-113, DraftKings, via Football Nation's October 31 snapshot). Goff at home, in a dome, against a Minnesota team that has to score to keep up. Thirteen overs in his last sixteen is not a fluke — it is a quarterback in a system built for exactly this. The line is 260.5, and Goff's recent form says that number is a touch low for a Ford Field game with a 46.5 total.

Leg 2: T.J. Hockenson Over 39.5 receiving yards (-114, FanDuel, via Football Nation's October 31 snapshot). Hockenson is Minnesota's release valve. When Jefferson draws the coverage and the pass rush gets home — Detroit has 21 sacks, tied for the league lead — the ball goes to the tight end underneath. Forty yards is four catches. If Minnesota is playing from behind in the second half, which the spread says is likely, Hockenson's volume only grows.

Both legs are props. No moneyline, no spread, no "just pick the winner" — this ticket lives or dies on two passing-game numbers, which is the whole point of going props-led.

The honest caveat, and it is a real one: these two legs are tied to the same game script. If this turns into a slow divisional grind — and divisional games do that, even at Ford Field — both legs can miss together. A low-possession game damages passing overs and shrinks the number of chances for receiving markets. And both lines come from an October 31 snapshot, not a live board — confirm the exact lines and prices in your book before playing, because a move to 270.5 on Goff or 44.5 on Hockenson changes the math. I am not stating a combined parlay price here because books price two-leg prop combos differently; check what your book is dealing and only play it if both legs are still at or near the numbers above.

Ravens-Bills Over 54.5: Lamar Is Back, and Both Offenses Can Score

Storylines driving the bet:

1. Jackson returns with a 119.6 passer rating across his four 2026 appearances before the injury — and Baltimore's offense scored just 23 combined points in its last two games without him. The upgrade at quarterback is enormous.

2. Buffalo owns the league's top scoring offense and comes off its bye, with Josh Allen posting a 102.1 rating plus eight rushing touchdowns already this season. James Cook adds 627 rushing yards and six scores.

3. The defenses have holes: Buffalo is giving up 28.5 points a game, and Baltimore's secondary is down two contributors. This total didn't get to 54.5 by accident.

The bet

Over 54.5 (-108). This is a game-prop play, not a side — and that's deliberate, because picking a winner here means guessing how mobile Lamar Jackson looks in his first game back. The over doesn't care. It just needs both offenses to do what they're built to do: Allen and Cook against a thin Baltimore secondary, Jackson and Derrick Henry against a Buffalo defense that gives up points. Buffalo's last two wins came by exactly three points, which is why I'm not touching the spread — but 55 combined points between these two attacks, in a dome-free November afternoon in Orchard Park, is a fair ask.

The honest caveat: if Jackson's ankle limits him to a pocket passer — he ran for just 71 yards across four games following his hamstring return last season — Baltimore's ceiling drops, and a grind-it-out Bills win could land this under. And Buffalo's questionable list is long enough that a late inactive or two could thin the offense. Still, with two top-ten attacks and two defenses that bleed points, the over is the cleanest way to play this game.

If gambling stops being fun, call 1-800-GAMBLER.

2-Leg Parlay (~+114): Rams ML + Bills ML

By Matt

Two favorites in the best spots on the Sunday slate — the Rams at home against the winless Chargers, and the Bills at home with Lamar Jackson returning on a bum ankle. Together they pay about +114.

The legs

Leg 1 — Rams moneyline (-278) vs. Chargers. The Chargers are 0-6, last in the league in scoring at 15.0 points per game, and down Derwin James. The Rams are at home at SoFi with Matthew Stafford throwing to Puka Nacua. The moneyline is steep, but it's the safe half of this ticket for a reason — see the full Rams -6.5 writeup for the case.

Leg 2 — Bills moneyline (-175) vs. Ravens. Buffalo is 5-1, off a bye, with the league's top scoring offense, at home in Orchard Park. Lamar Jackson is back for Baltimore, which makes the Ravens dangerous — but he's returning from a high-ankle sprain after three missed games, and Buffalo's offense doesn't need help to score. The Bills at -175 are the second-favorite leg that still pays.

The math

Rams -278 and Bills -175 combine to roughly +114 — bet $100 to win about $114. Two favorites, no lottery tickets, no ten-leg dreams. Both teams are at home, both are the better team on paper, and both are playing opponents with real, documented problems: a winless team missing its defensive star, and a team starting a quarterback in his first game back from injury.

The honest caveat: parlays multiply risk, not just reward. If either favorite stubs its toe — a post-bye Chargers surprise, or a vintage Lamar performance in his return — the whole ticket loses, even if the other leg cruises. That's the deal with every parlay, and it's why this one stays at two legs.

Pokémon Returns to Hoenn on November 6 With Mega Evolution: Delta Reign

By Matt

Pokémon TCG: Mega Evolution — Delta Reign launches November 6 with 135+ cards, headlined by Mega Rayquaza ex's first appearance since 2015, plus a new Legendary Stadium mechanic and retailer-exclusive Hoenn promos.

Pokémon is going back to Hoenn. On Friday, November 6, the Pokémon TCG launches Mega Evolution — Delta Reign, the set that brings Generation 3's most iconic Legendaries back to the card table as part of the franchise's 30th anniversary celebrations — and it is shaping up to be the biggest TCG launch of the month.

The set

Delta Reign packs over 135 cards, led by Mega Rayquaza ex — appearing in the TCG for the first time since 2015's XY—Ancient Origins expansion. Rayquaza is joined by Mega Golurk ex, Mega Malamar ex and Mega Golisopod ex, plus 14 illustration rares and six special illustration rare Pokémon and Supporter cards.

The set's headline mechanic is Legendary Stadium: three Legendary Stadiums that can be combined into a single connected illustration, adding a new strategic wrinkle for players and a display-piece chase for collectors. Booster packs, Elite Trainer Boxes and other collections arrive through participating retailers, and the set hits Pokémon TCG Live a day early, on November 5.

The Hoenn promos

The Hoenn homecoming extends beyond the packs. Starting November 6, fans who spend $15 or more on Pokémon TCG products at participating retailers receive a promotional card, and the Legendary you get depends on where you shop:

• Kyogre — Hot Topic, Barnes & Noble, Five Below and Macy's

• Groudon — GameStop

• Giratina — Best Buy

All three promos carry the exclusive Pokémon 30 treatment, tying them to the anniversary year. For completionists, that means at least three stops — and for everyone else, a good excuse to pick a favorite Legendary and plan accordingly.

The same-day competition

Delta Reign doesn't arrive alone. November 6 also brings the Pokémon 30th Celebration Ultra-Premium Collections — one built around Espeon ex, one around Umbreon ex — each packing 29 30th Celebration packs, a Classic Collection pack, a Pikachu ex promo, the Espeon or Umbreon ex promo, a playmat and a metal coin. Same day, same anniversary, same crowded shelf: it is the biggest single Friday the hobby has seen in a while, and it lands right as the McDonald's Pokémon Happy Meal promotion (November 10) is about to send the whole franchise into overdrive.

If you're planning to hunt Delta Reign on release day, get there early. The Hoenn nostalgia is real, the Rayquaza chase is real, and November's card calendar only gets busier from here.

Nintendo Declares November 1 Pikmin Day in a Surprise Direct

By Matt

Nintendo's surprise nine-minute Pikmin Day Direct on October 31 officially designated November 1 as Pikmin Day, with a free Pikmin 4 Switch 2 demo landing November 2 ahead of the full game's November 12 launch.

Nintendo loves a surprise, and this one arrived with almost no warning. On October 31, a roughly nine-minute Pikmin Day Direct quietly appeared on Nintendo's YouTube channels — not streamed live, announced only shortly beforehand — and it came with a piece of official franchise history: November 1 is now Pikmin Day.

Pikmin creator Shigeru Miyamoto opened the presentation with the reasoning, and it is delightfully on-brand: the original Red, Blue and Yellow Pikmin standing side by side resemble the three 1s in "11/1." Starting this year, November 1 belongs to the little guys.

Everything the Direct announced

No new Pikmin game was announced — Nintendo was clear about that, and the Direct's real job was setting the table for the Switch 2 edition of Pikmin 4. Here's the full rundown:

• Free Pikmin 4 Switch 2 demo — launching November 2, covering the opening Sunspeckled Terrace area and two Dandori Academy stages. Save data carries over to the full game.

• Pikmin 4: Nintendo Switch 2 Edition + Dandori Academy — launches November 12 for $69.99, with a $9.99 Upgrade Pack for owners of the Switch 1 version. The edition adds improved visuals and voice commands for Oatchi.

• Hello! Pikmin short film series — a new animated series from Nintendo Pictures, with the first short debuting in the Direct, a second arriving November 1, and new shorts monthly from December through the Nintendo Today! app.

• Pikmin Bloom Secret Caves — coming winter 2026, letting players obtain cave maps and send Pikmin underground in search of treasure. Finishing Dandori Academy exams in the Switch 2 edition unlocks special cave maps.

• Nintendo Treehouse Plays: Pikmin 4 — streaming November 1 at 9 a.m. PT.

Why a Pikmin Day matters

Nintendo has devoted serious airtime to Pikmin this year — the Switch 2 edition was announced in the September Direct — and a dedicated day is the company planting a flag for one of its most beloved, least blockbuster franchises. Pikmin has never been a Mario-sized seller, but it has one of the most devoted fanbases in gaming, and a yearly date on the calendar is how cult favorites become traditions.

The demo-first strategy is smart, too. A free taste on November 2, ten days before launch, with progress carrying over, is the lowest-friction way to turn curious Switch 2 owners into day-one buyers. If you have ever wondered what the Pikmin fuss is about, Sunday's Treehouse stream and Monday's demo are the on-ramp.

Happy Pikmin Day. The Red, Blue and Yellow ones are waiting.

The $50 Million Mickey Mantle Sale That Wasn't — Goldin Calls the Claim 'False News'

By Matt

A podcast claim that a PSA 10 1952 Topps Mickey Mantle sold privately for $50 million sent the hobby into a frenzy — until Goldin founder Ken Goldin shot it down 'with 100% fact' and Darren Rovell reported the deal was contemplated but never consummated.

The hobby got its biggest headline of the year this week — and then the headline got debunked within hours.

Entrepreneur and collector Patrick Bet-David said on his podcast that one of the three PSA 10 examples of the iconic 1952 Topps Mickey Mantle #311 had traded privately for $50 million. Had it been real, it would have been the most expensive trading card ever sold by an enormous margin. The claim spread fast. Then the pushback arrived even faster.

"That is false news"

Ken Goldin, founder of Goldin auctions, responded directly on X: "That is false news. An aggressive rumor that is not true; I can state that with 100% fact. A 1952 mantle has NOT sold for $50 million."

Sports business reporter Darren Rovell added the nuance that explains how the rumor likely started: "While this transaction has been more than contemplated, I have been told that it has not been consummated." In other words, a deal involving one of the three PSA 10 Mantles may have been discussed or negotiated — but no money changed hands, no ownership transferred, no buyer or seller has been publicly identified, and no auction house has confirmed any sale.

Why $50 million never passed the smell test

The card's rarity is real: PSA's population report lists only three copies of the 1952 Topps Mantle graded Gem Mint 10, with none graded higher. But the math never worked. The highest recorded public sale for a 1952 Mantle is the CGC-graded 9.5 version that sold for $12.6 million through Heritage Auctions on August 28, 2022. A jump from $12.6 million to $50 million in four years — with no public confirmation from any party to the deal — was always going to need more than a podcast mention.

The episode is a useful reminder for a hobby running hot: the difference between a contemplated deal and a completed one is everything, and the biggest number in the room is not always a sale. The record for a Mantle remains $12.6 million. The $50 million card, for now, exists only as a rumor.

Sources: Men's Journal, Collectibles News

Soulframe Opens to Everyone on PC November 6 — No Invite Needed, and Wolf Mounts Are Coming

By Matt

Digital Extremes announced during Soulframe Devstream 18 that Open Preludes drops the invitation requirement on Friday, November 6 — anyone on PC can play the fantasy RPG's alpha, with rideable wolf mounts arriving in Preludes 17 later in November.

The invite wall is coming down.

Digital Extremes announced October 30, during Soulframe Devstream 18, that Soulframe's Open Preludes will open to all Windows PC players on Friday, November 6 — no invitation, no key hunt, no Founders purchase required. Sign-ups are open now at soulframe.com, and players will access the game through Digital Extremes' own PC launcher.

It is the single biggest inflection point in the game's development since its first pre-alpha builds began in 2023. Until now, access has been invite-only — keys distributed in waves by email and via Twitch drops during devstreams — with a paid Founders program (launched November 25, 2025) as the only guaranteed way in. That program stays active for supporters, but buying in is no longer necessary to play.

What is Soulframe?

Soulframe is Digital Extremes' free-to-play fantasy action RPG — the Warframe studio's open-world cooperative follow-up, set on the island of Midrath, where players take on the role of an Envoy working to heal a world corrupted by the invading Ode. It is still a pre-alpha, not a finished release: there is no Steam launch and no console version as part of this opening, though the game can be wishlisted on Steam for its eventual full release.

Preludes 17: Verse of the Veilands

Alongside the open-access date, the studio detailed Preludes 17: Verse of the Veilands, the first major content update after the gates open, arriving later in November. The headliner is the community's most-requested feature since Soulframe was first shown publicly: rideable Omen Wolf mounts.

Players who have been raising wolf pups will finally be able to mature them into mounts — completing the pup's training, purifying two Veilands regions, then crafting a Bairnday Biscuit and feeding it to the pup so it grows into a fully rideable Omen Wolf. The update also introduces the Veilands themselves, a new environmental progression system built around reclaiming seven regions corrupted by the Ode, plus a new Ode Shrike Pact focused on traps and deception, new weapons and armor, enemy variants, fish and cave generation updates.

After years of watching from behind an invite wall, anyone with a PC can finally walk Midrath for themselves on November 6. The wolves, mercifully, are coming with them.

By Matt

Season 1 lands at midnight ET on November 6 with Backstab, Canyon, and a snowy Shoot House, the X01 black site in DMZ, two new Warzone maps, and a Battle Pass headlined by the Cronen Osprey and CSG-35.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Two weeks after launch, Modern Warfare 4 gets its first real season. Season 1 goes live at midnight ET on Friday, November 6 — 9 p.m. PT on Thursday, November 5 — across PlayStation 5, Xbox Series X|S, Nintendo Switch 2, and PC. And this is not a light content drop with a Battle Pass stapled to it. It is new maps, new modes, a new DMZ location, and the day Warzone fully becomes a Modern Warfare 4 game.

The maps

Three 6v6 maps arrive at launch. Backstab drops players into a war-torn zone of abandoned military equipment. Canyon is the mountainous one, with elevation changes built around an ambush on a river convoy. And Shoot House — the fan favorite — returns reimagined in snowy North Korean terrain.

That is just the launch wave. More than six maps are planned across the season, with Ministry and Overflow plus additional maps still classified for the mid-season update. Kill Block gets its own twist too: Kill Block: Crash, where the map stays static and Crash is always front and center.

The modes

Season 1 brings back Grind, adds Drop Zone, and runs Gauntlet 1v1 as part of the Wager Match event. Mid-season adds Counter Attack, a bomb mode built around detonating the enemy's Comm Station while defending your own. And the two Halloween party modes that dropped this week — Possession and Infected — are part of the same build-up, already live before the season even starts.

DMZ gets X01

The extraction crowd gets the biggest single addition: X01, a secret weapons facility captured by enemy forces on the outskirts of the Hajin location. Deploying to X01 means hunting the Commander-in-Chief in Threat Briefing contracts, searching for unique equipment blueprints, and running Resupply Runs, alongside new boss threats, new orders, DMZ events, and FOB enhancements.

The arsenal and the Battle Pass

Two free Battle Pass weapons headline the grind: the Cronen Osprey assault rifle at Tier 16 and the CSG-35 SMG at Tier 36. Later in the season, the JAK 12 Shotgun and the ZLR Talonhawk melee weapon join the arsenal. The Season 1 Battle Pass runs 1,100 COD Points with over 100 reward tiers and a new catch-up system, and the C.O.D.E. Valor Pack arrives in Season 1 along with the "Locked Down" weapon camo.

Warzone's new era

Season 1 is also the moment Warzone integrates Modern Warfare 4 content and seasonal progression. Two new Warzone maps arrive — Hajin, the Battle Royale version of the DMZ map, and Zodiac, a Resurgence map built around a train running through it — alongside the return of Verdansk and Rebirth Island. Ricochet Anti-Cheat extends to MW4 Warzone in Season 1. The full Warzone briefing lands in a separate post on Monday, November 2.

One hard cutoff to know: Warzone will no longer be playable on PlayStation 4 and Xbox One starting with Season 1. Progression and purchases carry to a current-gen console or PC on the same Activision account.

Modern Warfare 4's Halloween Week: Shipment Returns, Possession Possesses, and Infected Is Back Today

By Matt

The pre-season drops keep coming: Shipment returned October 27, the brand-new Possession mode landed October 29, and the classic Infected mode arrives today, October 30 — all warming up Season 1 on November 6.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Before Season 1 even starts, Modern Warfare 4 is already in its Halloween era. Three pre-season drops have rolled out in four days, and they are a perfect little sampler of what this game is: one beloved classic, one brand-new idea, and one party mode that never really left.

Shipment is back (October 27)

First up, the map everybody knows by heart. Shipment returned on October 27 as a remastered pre-season map, set in a Russian harbor — an abandoned container yard hemmed in between the water and brutalist concrete panel buildings. It supports 6v6 multiplayer, and it is exactly what it has always been: tiny, chaotic, and the fastest way to grind weapon challenges. If you have ever played Call of Duty, you already know every corner of this map. That is the point.

Possession is the new idea (October 29)

Then came the genuinely new thing. Possession, which landed October 29, is a party mode with a twist nobody asked for and everybody will argue about: when you eliminate an enemy, you possess them. You teleport to their spot and take their loadout. Activision describes it running on Hardpoint and Search and Destroy rules, which means every kill is not just a point — it is a relocation, a new gun, and a completely different plan. Get it right and you are suddenly holding the objective from the other team's side of the map. Get it wrong and you just handed them your position.

Infected returns today (October 30)

And today, the classic. Infected is back for Halloween week: one player starts infected, and every survivor they eliminate joins the infected side until the last holdouts are cornered. The loadouts tell you everything about how this version plays. The infected get the FSS Carbon Edge Combat Knife, a Throwing Knife, and the Ninja perk — fast, quiet, and close. Survivors start with the Rezi 12 Shotgun firing explosive slugs, plus a Trip Wire and a Concussion Grenade — loud, messy, and built for holding a corner until the clock runs out.

Why this week matters

None of these three drops is Season 1. That is the point. Shipment, Possession, and Infected are the warm-up act, and they are doing exactly what a warm-up should do: get the weapon grind moving, get the party modes populated, and get everybody talking before November 6, when the season, the Battle Pass, and Warzone's new era all land at once. One note: Activision has not published exact start times for Possession or Infected, so if a mode is not in your playlist tab yet, check back on the day — the dates are confirmed, the hours are not.

Modern Warfare 4 Will Remove Real Itaewon Disaster Footage From Its Opening on November 12

By Matt

Activision apologized on Friday and confirmed the real 2022 Itaewon crowd-crush footage in Modern Warfare 4's opening cinematic will be removed in the November 12 update, after bereaved families demanded its removal.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

One week after launch, Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 4 is facing its first real controversy — and Activision has now said it will fix it. The company apologized on Friday, October 30, and confirmed that real news footage of the 2022 Itaewon crowd crush, used in the game's opening cinematic, will be removed in an update on November 12.

What happened

Modern Warfare 4 launched worldwide on October 23. Its story is built on a fictional premise: a North Korean invasion of South Korea. To set that scene, the opening cinematic used real news coverage of the Itaewon disaster — the crowd crush in Seoul's Itaewon district on October 29, 2022, when more than 100,000 people packed narrow streets for Halloween and nearly 160 people died.

That choice did not stay quiet for long. The 10.29 Itaewon Disaster Bereaved Families Association and civic groups issued a statement calling the use of real disaster footage for entertainment inappropriate and demanding the scene be removed. Reporting on their statement described footage of rescue attempts, people carried out on stretchers, and body bags on the street being repurposed for the game, including a clip of CPR being performed on a collapsed person reported to run about three seconds.

Activision's response

Activision had previously explained that it used real news footage to establish the game's fictional narrative, and said it was "not intended to reference or trivialize the Itaewon disaster or any other real-world tragedy in any way, shape or form."

On Friday, the company went further: a public apology to the bereaved families and victims, plus a date. The footage will be completely removed in the game update releasing November 12. Until that patch lands, the opening still plays as shipped.

One point of friction remains. The families association said Friday that it had still not received a direct apology from the company, even as Activision apologized publicly. That gap — a public statement versus a direct one — is worth watching as November 12 approaches.

Why this one matters

Games borrow from real history all the time, but there is a line between evoking a real place and reusing footage of real people who died there, and this week showed exactly where players and families draw it. Credit where it is due: Activision moved fast once the families spoke, with a dated fix rather than a vague promise. The test now is simple — ship the November 12 patch, and make the apology direct.

Sources: TheGamer, Pixel Herald, Digital Today

Pokémon's Dual Fusion Is Live — and the Espeon and Umbreon Decks Land Tomorrow

By Matt

Pokémon TCG Pocket's Dual Fusion expansion is out today with the game's first dual-type cards — Mega Mewtwo X ex and Mega Greninja ex lead the set — and tomorrow the physical game gets its 30th Celebration battle decks, Espeon ex and Umbreon ex, ready to play out of the box for $39.99.

Pokémon's 30th anniversary year keeps stacking releases, and this week is one of the biggest yet — on both sides of the game. The app gets its first dual-type cards today. The physical game gets two ready-to-play decks tomorrow. Here's the full picture.

Dual Fusion is live today

Pokémon TCG Pocket's Dual Fusion expansion launched today, and its headline mechanic is something the app has never had before: dual-type cards. One card, two types at once. The two stars are Mega Mewtwo X ex (Psychic and Fighting) and Mega Greninja ex (Water and Darkness), split across two booster packs — one for Mega Mewtwo X, one for Pikachu — with new versions of Pikachu ex, Mew ex, Venusaur, Charizard, Blastoise, and Mega Dragonite ex in the mix.

For players, dual-type cards change deckbuilding in a real way. A Psychic/Fighting attacker doesn't just hit harder — it opens lines your deck couldn't run before, because a single card can answer matchups that used to need two different attackers. That's the kind of mechanic that reshuffles a meta overnight, and it's why this set is getting more attention than a normal Pocket drop.

The launch comes with a lot around it. Six Dual Fusion rental decks are free to play through December 15, so anyone can try the new cards without building from scratch. A Special Pack called First Partners runs October 31 to December 26, featuring the first partner Pokémon from all nine regions. And second anniversary events run from today into mid-December, with an anniversary ticket exchangeable for a booster pack with a guaranteed high-rarity card.

The bigger structural news is the schedule change that arrives with the new C Series: sets move from roughly a four-week cycle to roughly six weeks. The developers said it plainly — players told them big sets were making collecting hard. Collectors asked for breathing room, and they got it. That's a rare thing in a live game: a publisher slowing down because the players asked.

The Espeon and Umbreon decks land tomorrow

If physical cards are more your thing, tomorrow (October 30) brings the Pokémon TCG: 30th Celebration Battle Decks — Espeon ex and Umbreon ex. Each is a ready-to-play, all-foil 60-card deck for $39.99, with a playmat, coin, and deck box in the box. The Espeon ex deck runs with Victini; the Umbreon ex deck runs with Zeraora. No chasing packs to play — open it and you're in a game.

That's the part worth noticing. In a year full of chase products and 30 different Pikachu variants to collect, a pair of decks built for actually playing — every card foil, everything you need in the box — is a different kind of release. It's aimed at the person who wants to sit down and play tonight, not the person hunting a pull. For a 30th anniversary celebration, that's a good look: the game remembering it's a game.

Sources: Shane the Gamer, Gaming Route, GameTrader.SG, CBR, CBR, Pokémon.com

Pokémon Is Back at McDonald's — and the Promo Accidentally Spoiled Next Year's Cards Too

By Matt

McDonald's confirmed its Pokémon Happy Meal returns in the US on November 10 with 15 toys and 15 trading cards — and, in announcing it, reportedly showed off next year's M27 set a full year early.

The Pokémon Happy Meal is back — and this time the announcement itself became a collectible story.

McDonald's has confirmed the return of its Pokémon Happy Meal, launching in the US on November 10 with a 15-piece toy set and a 15-card trading card collection, plus a digital game built into the packaging. The international rollout follows through February 2027, starting with Canada, Australia and several other markets in November.

What's in the box

The toys are miniature Happy Meal boxes, each carrying the face of a different Pokémon — and they do more than sit on a shelf. McDonald's executive Guillaume Huin showed that each box-shaped toy converts into a bag charm you can clip onto keys, bags, or just about anything.

Confirmed headliners so far: Pikachu, Eevee, Charmander, Squirtle and Umbreon, with promotional artwork also showing Piplup. The card side includes holofoil cards, with Jirachi, Pikachu and Absol among the previews — though McDonald's has not detailed the complete 15-card checklist yet. Fifteen cards to collect, fifteen toys to chase: the classic Happy Meal math, with a charm twist collectors didn't see coming.

The accidental spoiler

Here's the part collectors are actually talking about: when Pokémon officially confirmed this year's cards, the post reportedly showed an entirely different set — stamped M27, not M26 — before it was quickly taken down.

That M27 set, per reporting on the since-removed post, includes Raichu and Rowlet among its 15 cards, with a new Squirtle card as one of the notable fresh designs. If those really are next year's Happy Meal cards, they were revealed roughly a year early — and they carry an interesting implication: with no sign of new Pokémon in the set, the next Pokémon games (Winds & Waves) may not arrive until later in 2027, since McDonald's sets historically include the newest starters.

Treat the M27 details as reported, not officially confirmed — the post came down fast, and McDonald's hasn't confirmed next year's set. But the M26 set itself is locked: November 10 in the US, 15 toys, 15 cards, and a charm for your bag.

Why collectors care

McDonald's Pokémon promos are one of the hobby's great equalizers — cheap, everywhere, and genuinely fun to chase. A 15-and-15 set with a usable charm gimmick is already a strong one. An accidental year-early leak of the next set on top of it? That's the kind of story the hobby talks about for months.

Sources: Hypebeast, TheGamer

The Air Jordan 3 "Fireside" releases Friday, October 30, 2026 for $205, a women's-exclusive general release through Nike SNKRS (10:00 a.m. ET) and select retailers. No raffle gauntlet, no verified sellout story — just a Friday-morning drop, with individual stores running their own systems.

Collectibles · Oct 29, 2026 · 9:38 PM ET

Ohtani's $3.24M Logoman Leads a Monster Week for Sports Cards

By Matt

Shohei Ohtani's 1/1 Logoman patch auto sold for $3.24 million — the most expensive Dodgers card ever — headlining a week that also saw a $2.16M Cooper Flagg, a record Messi Topps NOW, and a $1.47M vintage football set.

It's been a monster week for sports cards, the kind where the top of the market moves in a bunch of directions at once: modern superstars, a farewell record, and vintage all putting up numbers in the same seven days.

Ohtani's $3.24 million Logoman

The headline is Shohei Ohtani's 2025 Topps MLB MVP The Game Dynasty Black Auto Patch 1/1, graded PSA 9, which sold for $3.24 million through Fanatics Premier on October 23. It's the most expensive Los Angeles Dodgers card ever sold at auction, and the third-biggest Ohtani sale of all time.

Logoman cards are the hobby's ultimate chase for a reason: the patch is cut from a game-worn jersey's actual MLB logo, so there's exactly one of each. A 1/1 Logoman auto of the game's biggest star, in a set built to honor one of the greatest performances in North American sports history, is the kind of card that doesn't come around twice. The $3.24 million price says the market agrees — even in a week where Ohtani's on-field postseason was quiet, the cardboard didn't care.

Flagg and the dual Logoman right behind it

The same Fanatics Premier run put two more seven-figure cards on the board. A 2025 Topps Motif Logoman Auto 1/1 Cooper Flagg (unscratched redemption) sold for $2.16 million — the week's second-biggest sale and a statement about where the hobby thinks Flagg's market is going. And a 2026 Topps Chrome Dual MVP Gold Logoman Auto 1/1 of Aaron Judge and Shohei Ohtani went for $1.23 million. Two of the game's biggest names on one card, one copy in the world, seven figures. That's the modern high-end market in one line.

Messi's farewell sets a different kind of record

The record that isn't an auction at all might be the most telling one. Lionel Messi's Topps NOW "Final Farewell" card is now the best-selling Topps NOW card of all time at 664,325 copies sold, passing Shohei Ohtani's 50/50 card (653,737). No bidding, no 1/1 — just an enormous number of people buying the same farewell card in the same week.

That's the other side of the modern hobby: retirements and historic moments have become instant mass-market events. One buyer also gets a 1-of-1 autographed White OpalFractor version, and the card has Chrome and Opal Chrome parallels including a 1-of-1 SuperFractor — so the chase exists inside the mass release. Fanatics, which now controls the exclusive trading card rights for MLB, NBA, NFL, and FIFA World Cup properties, has turned the farewell card into its own category.

Vintage: the $1.47 million football set

And vintage had its moment too. A nearly complete 1962 Topps football set — 175 of the 176 cards, the top set on PSA's All-Time Finest registry at a 9.337 grade point average — sold for $1,473,638 in Mile High Card Company's Fall Auction, the highest price ever paid for a football card set. Only about 1% of all submissions from the set have ever earned a PSA 9, and 101 of those are in this collection. The auction also moved a $360,913 baseball collection and an $833,138 Babe Ruth card (a 1933 Sport Kings, SGC 9, the auction's highest-selling individual card).

Put it together and the week tells one story: the hobby's top end is not cooling off. Modern 1/1s are doing millions, farewell cards are doing hundreds of thousands of copies, and the best vintage sets are setting records of their own. Different corners, same heat.

Sources: Sports Illustrated, Men's Journal, Yardbarker, Sports Collectors Daily

John Lewis x RIXO: the 44-Piece Party-Season Collection, in Stores Now

By Matt

John Lewis' biggest fashion collaboration to date — 44 vintage-inspired pieces with British label RIXO, built around an 1849 archive floral print — is in stores and online now, from £65 to £525.

John Lewis' biggest fashion collaboration to date is in stores now. The 44-piece John Lewis x RIXO collection, launched October 22, pairs the department store's heritage with the vintage-inspired occasionwear of British label RIXO — and it is timed squarely for party season.

The collection

The range covers dresses, knitwear, blouses, skirts, jackets, scarves, shoes and bags, priced from £65 for accessories to £525 for the most elaborate dresses. The hero piece is The Vesper, a full-length dress in an archive print with cap sleeves and a ruffle neckline.

The story inside the story is the print itself. Designers working in the John Lewis Partnership Archive found a floral motif from an 1849 block-printed textile — the first time an archive print has been used in a John Lewis fashion collaboration — and it now runs across two dresses, a blouse, a skirt and a scarf. Around it: vintage florals in autumn colours, plus velvet, sequins, cashmere and lamé, finished with fur and lace trims, ruffles and metallic embroidery.

The brands

RIXO was founded by friends Henrietta Rix and Orlagh McCloskey and built its following on vintage-inspired dresses and distinctive prints. McCloskey called the collaboration "a true extension of RIXO — taking the heritage of John Lewis, leaning into its incredible archive, and bringing it to life with that RIXO magic." John Lewis fashion director Rachel Morgans called RIXO "an iconic British brand adored by so many, much like John Lewis."

The collection is available in John Lewis stores and online. As a limited edition, the pieces worth having are the ones to grab early — the line's own coverage puts it bluntly: identify your favourites sooner rather than later.

Sources: Retail Gazette, TheIndustry.fashion, Hood Magazine

By Matt

The XT-4K Mid turns Salomon's low-top XT-4K into a mid-cut silhouette, headlining the Kith for Salomon Winter 2026 collection launching globally today.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The Kith for Salomon Winter 2026 collection launches globally today, Monday, October 26, at 11 AM regional time, available via Kith shops, Kith.com, and the Kith App. Headlining the drop is a brand-new silhouette: the XT-4K Mid.

The XT-4K Mid

The XT-4K Mid takes the low-top XT-4K and rebuilds it as a mid-cut silhouette. The upper pairs mesh with synthetic overlays, giving the shoe a technical, trail-ready look with a little more coverage than the original.

Silhouette: XT-4K Mid

Base model: Low-top XT-4K, rebuilt as a mid-cut

Upper: Mesh and synthetic overlays

Colorways: Black, Green Gaze, and Olive Night

The debut colorways keep things grounded: Black, Green Gaze, and Olive Night. It's a restrained, outdoorsy palette that fits the Winter 2026 season — no loud color blocking, just a clean mix that works with the mid-cut shape.

Banana Republic x Zankov: 'Future Heirlooms' Holiday Capsule in Stores Now

By Matt

Banana Republic's 27-piece holiday capsule with New York designer Henry Zankov is out now — brushed-cashmere knits, a burgundy leather bomber and a 'Future Heirlooms' campaign, priced $90 to $600.

Colour is the whole point. Banana Republic's limited-edition holiday capsule with ZANKOV — the New York label founded by Henry Zankov — is available now at select stores and online, after launching October 20, 2026.

The collection

The 27-piece collection spans menswear, womenswear and accessories, built around brushed cashmere, proprietary wool yarns, premium leathers and engineered plaids, with relaxed proportions and universal sizing designed to mix and match. Prices run $90 to $600.

The heroes:

• The Auden sweater — brushed cashmere in vivid stripes and solid colours, inspired by vintage sportswear

• The Sasha cardigan — navy and pistachio stripes in brushed cashmere

• The Frankie polo — a classic long-sleeve polo, subverted with a pop of malachite green on the placket

• The burgundy bomber — leather, with Zankov's signature stripe concealed inside the cuff

The campaign

The launch campaign is titled "Future Heirlooms," shot by photographer Andrew Jacobs and paired with commissioned work by Belgian artist Katrien de Blauwer — a nod to Zankov's idea that these are pieces to live with, love, and eventually pass down.

Why it matters

Zankov won the CFDA American Emerging Designer of the Year award in 2024, and this is his knitwear language — vivid stripes, saturated colour, tactile fabric — at Banana Republic scale and prices. For a holiday capsule, that's the rare collaboration where the designer's actual signatures survive the translation.

Sources: Gap Inc., WebWire
